This slice also fixes a real, narrow defect found in the code it was rewriting: `elevationChartGradient.ts`'s local-gradient chart overlay coerced any run outside the currently-narrowed detail range to a synthetic grey "unknown" colour instead of falling through to plain `currentColor` like its sibling macro-feature overlay already did, which — since both screens have narrowed this range to the selected/active feature ever since the fifth slice — painted a solid grey stroke over every other climb/descent's own macro colour; the replacement `buildFeatureDetailChartRuns` preserves `visualKey: null` for an out-of-range run, and `ElevationChart.tsx`'s overlay now omits `null` runs entirely rather than rendering a coercion. `MicroDetailVisualKey` (`routeFeaturePalette.ts`) is the shared nine-value key space (five climb bands, three descent bands, `neutral`) driving one authoritative `MICRO_DETAIL_COLOURS`/`MICRO_DETAIL_LABELS` pair consumed identically by the map's micro layer, the chart's detail overlay, the new `ClimbGradientBandLegend` (replacing `GradientLegend`), and `GradientSegmentDetailsPanel` — no second copy of thresholds or colours anywhere. An eighth slice completes Future-backlog item 8: the openrouteservice adapter already requested `instructions: true` and `normalizeOpenRouteServiceRoute.ts`'s `buildManoeuvres` already built `PlannedRoute.manoeuvres` from ORS's `segments[].steps[]`, but nothing consumed that data anywhere in Riding, and the `type` field was a bare stringified ORS numeric code, not a canonical value. `Manoeuvre.type` (`src/domain/types.ts`) is now the canonical, provider-independent `ManoeuvreType` union (`start`/`continue`/six directional variants/`u-turn`/`roundabout`/`waypoint`/`finish`/`unknown`); `src/routing/manoeuvreTypes.ts`'s `decodeOrsManoeuvreType` decodes ORS's raw codes into it, mirroring `surfaceCodes.ts`'s own never-throws decode style, and `buildManoeuvres` now also drops a step whose `way_points[0]` is not a genuinely valid in-bounds index (rather than defaulting it to distance 0), caps instruction length, and sorts the result by distance. This surfaced and fixed a real, previously-latent bug in `stitchPlannedRouteLegs.ts`: its leg-boundary manoeuvre dedup only ever matched an exact type-and-instruction equality, so it could never recognise ORS's own real per-leg Arrive/Depart step pair (distinct types, distinct instruction text) — every internal waypoint of a multi-leg Planning route was silently getting both a spurious "arrived" and a spurious "departed" manoeuvre. Since every leg's own trailing step now decodes context-free to canonical `finish` and its own leading step to `start`, the dedup rule was replaced with a narrower, correct one: a leg boundary's `finish`+`start` pair (within the existing seam tolerance) now collapses into one `waypoint` manoeuvre, dropping its instruction (neither leg's own arrive/depart text is correct mid-route); the very first leg's `start` and the very last leg's `finish` are never touched. A route's `manoeuvres.length > 0` is already sufficient, existing proof of trusted (openrouteservice-derived) data — GPX import (`normalizeGpx.ts`) always sets it to `[]` and no other code path writes to it — so no new provenance flag was added anywhere (superseded by the ninth slice below, which adds an explicit `ManoeuvreProvenance` field once GPX import can legitimately carry manoeuvres too). A new pure `src/navigation/nextManoeuvre.ts` selects the next manoeuvre from the existing frozen/reliable `presentationDistanceFromStartMetres` (never the live matched distance), giving the off-route-freeze and stale-restore behaviour for free; a named `MANOEUVRE_REACHED_TOLERANCE_METRES` (15 m, order-of-magnitude comparable to `offRoute.ts`'s own thresholds) must be passed by at least this much, not merely approached within it, before a manoeuvre counts as reliably passed, and advancement is monotonic against a caller-held `previousReachedIndex` so GPS jitter can never regress the shown manoeuvre. `RidingNextManoeuvrePanel` (Riding-only, shown once riding is active, mirroring `RidingClimbSelector`'s own idle-only visibility inverted) shows a small project-owned SVG `ManoeuvreIcon` (no external asset, inline-styled like `GradientColourSwatch`), the provider's own instruction text (or a generic per-type label when absent), and a distance whose rounding tier and font-size/weight band (this slice's own judgement calls, not literal CLAUDE.md figures) become more prominent inside 500 m and again inside 100 m — driven by inline style, not a CSS class, for the same "Vitest never loads index.css" reason `GradientColourSwatch` already documents. Only the instruction text carries `role="status"`, so it announces a genuine manoeuvre or urgency-band change but never the continuously-updating numeric distance beside it. A planner route with no usable manoeuvres shows an explanatory "Turn information is unavailable for this route" message; an ordinary imported GPX shows its own explanatory message and never an inferred turn, since `normalizeGpx.ts` never produces manoeuvres from geometry. No IndexedDB schema/migration was needed: `manoeuvres` already round-trips through `routesRepository.ts`'s raw Dexie `put`/`get` untouched. A ninth slice closes the GPX round-trip gap this left behind: exporting a planned route already wrote a flat, unbound `<acn:manoeuvre>`/`<acn:source>` extension, but nothing on import ever read it back, so a planned-then-exported-then-reimported route silently lost its turns. The exporter's existing namespace (`https://adf-md.github.io/amazing-cycling-navigation/gpx-extensions/v1`, unchanged — never yet consumed by anything, so restructuring its contents cost nothing) now writes a geometry-bound envelope, `<acn:navigation version="1" pointCount="N" geometrySha256="<64-char lower-case hex>">`, nested (as before) inside the track's own shared `<extensions>` alongside the unrelated `<acn:source>` provenance element; each `<acn:manoeuvre trackPointIndex="N" distanceMetres="X" type="...">` inside it carries an `<acn:instruction>` child (previously an attribute). `trackPointIndex` is the nearest of the route's own exported track points to that manoeuvre's distance (`navigation/distance.ts`'s new `nearestPointIndexForDistance`, a binary search — needed because a stitched multi-leg route's manoeuvre distance can legitimately fall between two points, not exactly on one) and is the round trip's primary anchor; `distanceMetres` is the canonical route distance, kept as a secondary validation/forward-compatibility value. `geometrySha256` binds the envelope to the exact exported track: `src/gpx/geometryDigest.ts`'s `canonicalizeTrackGeometry` joins each point's `lon,lat` (via the exact same bare `String(x)` the exporter already writes into `<trkpt>`, so export-time and import-time canonicalisation provably agree; elevation excluded, since anchoring is index/distance-based) with `\n`, and `computeGeometryDigestHex` hashes it with `crypto.subtle.digest("SHA-256", …)` — an integrity/binding check, not a digital signature. This makes `exportRouteToGpx` genuinely `async` for the first time (both UI export call sites and every test call site updated accordingly); if trusted manoeuvres exist but Web Crypto is unavailable, export throws a typed `GpxExportError` rather than silently dropping the turn data, while a route with no trusted manoeuvres is unaffected either way. Trust is now an explicit, provider-independent `ManoeuvreProvenance` field on `PlannedRoute` (`{ kind: "routing-provider"; provider: string }` or `{ kind: "acn-gpx-extension"; version: 1 }`, `src/domain/types.ts`), read by one shared predicate, `src/domain/manoeuvreTrust.ts`'s `hasTrustedManoeuvres` — replacing the old `sourceKind === "gpx-import"` short-circuit `RidingNextManoeuvrePanel` and `RidingScreen` used to gate on. A route saved before this field existed (or an ordinary GPX import that never carried a validated ACN extension) has no `manoeuvreProvenance` at all and falls back to exactly the previous implicit rule (`source.kind === "planner" && manoeuvres.length > 0`), so every pre-existing stored route and every ordinary GPX import behaves identically to before, with no IndexedDB migration (a plain new optional field on the already-blob-stored `PlannedRoute`). `RidingScreen` gates both `selectNextManoeuvre`'s own input (an empty list when untrusted) and the panel's messaging on `hasTrustedManoeuvres`, restoring the invariant "a non-null selection is always trustworthy" even though a `gpx-import`-sourced route can now legitimately carry manoeuvres. Import-side reading (`src/gpx/parseAcnExtension.ts`'s `readAcnNavigationExtension`) is namespace-aware throughout (the `acn:` prefix itself is never trusted, only the namespace URI) and scoped to exactly the track `parseGpx.ts`'s `extractRoutePoints` actually selected (a new `selectedTrackElement` result field) — never a second, non-selected `<trk>`, and never an extension nested inside a per-point `<extensions>`, since only the track's own direct-child `<extensions>` is ever consulted. Validation is strictly all-or-nothing: an unsupported `version`, a `pointCount`/digest mismatch, an out-of-range or non-integer `trackPointIndex`, a non-finite/negative/out-of-order `distanceMetres`, or a manoeuvre count above the defensive `MAX_ACN_MANOEUVRES` (10,000 — a sanity cap, not this file's primary size defence, which remains `validateGpxFile`'s whole-file `MAX_GPX_FILE_SIZE_BYTES`) discards the entire envelope, never a partial manoeuvre list, while the ordinary track/elevation import always still succeeds; a rejected-but-present extension surfaces one non-blocking `GpxImportNotice` ("This GPX contained turn information, but it did not match the route geometry and was ignored."), reusing the same notice mechanism as the existing multiple-track/-route warnings. An unrecognised `type` string coerces to `"unknown"` rather than rejecting the envelope (matching `decodeOrsManoeuvreType`'s existing never-throws policy), and each manoeuvre's `distanceMetres` is sanity-checked against its `trackPointIndex`'s own recomputed distance using a tolerance that adapts to the local point spacing at that index (never a flat constant, which would either be too tight for sparse provider geometry or too loose for dense geometry) — a structural guard layered on top of the digest, not a replacement for it. Re-exporting a re-imported ACN route reproduces the same manoeuvres losslessly, since the reader stores each manoeuvre's canonical, recomputed point-distance rather than the raw attribute text, making the next export's own nearest-point lookup exact. Interoperability is unchanged from what the namespace mechanism always implied: this remains a standards-compatible GPX 1.1 track that any reader can open, ignoring the unknown `acn:` extension; another application may strip unknown extensions entirely when rewriting the file; round-trip turn preservation is guaranteed only for direct export-then-import through this app; and none of this implies Garmin or FIT course-point compatibility, which remains a distinct, unimplemented feature. A tenth slice completes the milestone: an optional, off-by-default **Keep screen awake** control in active Riding, implemented entirely through platform-neutral capability detection (`"wakeLock" in navigator && typeof navigator.wakeLock?.request === "function"`, `src/platform/wakeLock.ts`) behind the same small typed DI boundary already used for geolocation and clocks — no user-agent or browser-specific branching anywhere. An unsupported browser renders no control at all, not a disabled one. `src/ui/riding/useScreenWakeLock.ts` owns the live sentinel using the same generation-token discipline `useRideNavigation`'s geolocation watch already established, extended with an explicit mounted flag and a status state machine (`inactive`/`acquiring`/`active`/`unavailable`) so a stale request, an unmount, or a superseded ride can never leak a sentinel or a state update; the control is mounted only while a ride is active (the same `nav.geolocationStatus !== "idle"` gate `RidingNextManoeuvrePanel` uses), so React's own unmount already provides "release when riding stops/the route changes/the rider leaves the screen." The rider's desired on/off preference (never a global setting) is a new optional `wakeLockDesired` field on `StoredRideState`, restored/persisted alongside the existing camera and elevation-view fields with no schema version bump; an unrelated new ride always starts with the option off. A held lock releases defensively while the document is hidden and reacquires automatically once visible again if still desired; a rejected request or an unexpected release while visible surfaces a concise, non-technical retry state instead of looping. This provides no background location and does not replace the existing suspension/reload recovery — it is a pure display-on convenience layered on top of it. An eleventh slice completes backlog item 13: an off-by-default-until-entered **Climb** elevation view, offered in active Riding only while the reliable presentation distance lies inside an already-recognised climb. One pure derivation, `selectEffectiveElevationView` (`src/navigation/climbElevationView.ts`), decides the displayed view from the rider's standard Full/2/5/10 km preference, the currently active climb, and one new persisted scalar, `dismissedClimbFeatureId` (`StoredRideState`, no schema bump) — auto-opening once per distinct climb, respecting a manual dismissal for the rest of that climb, and auto-reopening on a different climb, with no effect and no second "already shown" flag needed. Climb-relative metrics (`computeClimbProgressMetrics`) reuse the existing shared smoothed profile and already-classified local-gradient segments (no new elevation or gradient calculation), reporting distance completed/remaining, current/summit elevation, elevation remaining (the net smoothed-elevation difference to the climb's own detected finish, not a new cumulative-ascent sum) and current local gradient, with no percentage-complete value anywhere. `ElevationChart` gained an opt-in `areaFill` prop (geometry in `src/ui/shared/climbFillGeometry.ts`) that fills the profile down to the chart's own existing padded lower bound using the same Garmin-inspired climb local-gradient band colours, splitting the ridden portion (dimmed) from the remaining portion at the rider's live progress — Full/2/5/10 km views and Planning's own chart usage are unaffected. A new `RidingClimbProgressPanel` shows these live metrics with its own numbered heading, alongside the unchanged, selection-driven `RouteFeatureDetailsPanel`. A twelfth slice compacts the wake-lock control's presentation without touching `useScreenWakeLock`'s own lifecycle, state machine or generation-token discipline: the control now renders as a single compact row immediately above the route title while a ride is active (the same `isWakeLockSupported()`-and-ride-active gate, unchanged), and the always-visible battery/behaviour explanation is replaced by a tap/keyboard-accessible information popover — the first anchored, non-modal disclosure in this codebase (deliberately not the existing native `<details>` pattern, since that expands in document flow rather than overlaying). Acquisition success and the retry-on-failure state are shown compactly inline rather than as separate paragraphs; an unsupported browser still renders no control at all. A thirteenth slice extends the pre-ride climb selector with a detailed, read-only preview of the selected climb's own elevation profile, reusing the active-Riding current-climb view's rendering path rather than a second implementation: one shared pure function, `buildClimbChartViewModel` (`src/navigation/climbElevationView.ts`), takes an explicit `ClimbChartMode` (`"active-current-climb"`, carrying the rider's own progress marker, or `"pre-ride-selected-climb"`, carrying none) and builds whatever `ElevationChart` needs — points, domain, gradient segments, marker, area-fill — from the same precomputed climb/gradient-segment data the existing overview and active view already use, with no second climb-detection or grade-classification pass. The active mode reproduces today's route-global points/domain unchanged (a pure refactor of `RidingScreen.tsx`'s existing inline construction, proven behaviour-identical by its own unchanged test suite); the pre-ride mode rebases every point and gradient-segment boundary so the climb's own start is distance 0, satisfying "local distance from climb start" as a genuine, unit-tested property of the pure model itself (rendering is pixel-identical either way today, since `ElevationChart` draws no distance tick labels), and supplies no marker. This exposed one real gap in `ElevationChart`'s existing `areaFill`: its filled area previously required a marker to render at all (`buildClimbFillRuns` needed a numeric pixel split point); both now accept `markerX: number | null`, rendering one uniform-opacity fill with no completed/remaining distinction when there's no rider progress to split against — the one deliberate, spec-required behaviour change, with its own existing test rewritten (not just relaxed) to assert the new behaviour. `ElevationChart` also gained an optional `ariaLabel` prop, applied identically to both the figure and the chart's own `role="img"` name, so the new chart's accessible name identifies which climb it shows (e.g. "Elevation profile for Climb 2") without a new dependency. The chart itself slots into the existing shared `RouteFeatureDetailsPanel` via a new optional `detailChart` prop, rendered directly between the heading and the fact list — populated only by Riding's pre-ride call site (gated identically to that panel's own existing `climbNumber`, so heading/chart/facts always describe the same climb in the same render), leaving Planning's own use of the same panel, and Riding's own active-climb view (which continues to show its own chart and `RidingClimbProgressPanel` above this panel, unchanged), untouched. One accepted, documented (not fixed) pre-existing edge case: the new chart's idle-gate matches the panel's own existing gate rather than the top-of-screen chart's separate `matchedDistanceFromStartMetres` gate, so a reload that restores in-progress ride progress while the rider hasn't yet tapped "Resume riding" can show both this pre-ride preview and the active Climb view/progress panel simultaneously — an already-existing quirk of that one specific, rare state, not introduced or worsened by this slice. Manually verified on the user's iPhone across multiple climbs: the detailed chart's colours match the active current-climb view, no GPS marker appears pre-ride, and the preview still renders after a reload. A fourteenth slice reverts a design choice from the sixth slice above, after this same real-device check showed it was surprising rather than helpful: the pre-ride "Recognised climbs" dropdown no longer auto-selects the route's first recognised climb, and instead starts on "All route" until the rider explicitly picks a climb, matching Planning's own screen (which never auto-selects). The mechanism the sixth slice introduced is unchanged — `RidingScreen.tsx`'s `selectedRouteFeatureId` is still "an explicit choice, tagged with the route id it was made for, otherwise falls back"; only the fallback value changes, from `climbs[0]?.id ?? null` to a plain `null`, so a fresh pre-ride session and a route change while already viewing Riding both now start on "All route" rather than the first recognised climb. This has no effect on active-ride behaviour: `activeFeature`/`activeClimb` (GPS-progress-driven, not explicit-selection-driven) and the Climb elevation view's own separate auto-open/dismiss state machine are entirely untouched, since neither ever read `selectedRouteFeatureId`'s fallback value.

Do not start a later milestone by weakening or bypassing earlier reliability requirements.

## Manual acceptance status (17 August 2026)

This is the authoritative manual-test ledger. It records verification evidence only; the behaviour contracts remain defined by the sections above and are not restated here.

### Manually verified on the deployed iPhone PWA

- **Routes:** long route names wrap and remain usable; GPX export works; inline route deletion and its confirmation work. Route renaming now happens inside the existing route card, preserves the card and route summary while editing, and persists the changed name correctly.
- **Planning:** waypoint placement/editing, waypoint identification and map controls work; both Road bike (`cycling-road`) and General cycling (`cycling-regular`) profiles work and produce viable, meaningfully different routes; route warnings work; saving and GPX export work.
- **Pre-ride:** a newly opened route is framed correctly; recognised-climb selection works; the selected climb's detailed elevation profile works; Start riding and Resume riding have suitable prominence. The restored **Resume riding** state now reliably shows the route overview instead of occasionally remaining at MapLibre's whole-world default while restored follow mode awaits a fresh GPS fix.
- **Active Riding (road-bicycle field test):** a real bicycle ride confirmed live position and route progress updating plausibly; the mounted map stayed readable outdoors; Follow framed the rider appropriately; direction-up and North-up behaved sensibly while moving; a manual map gesture paused Follow and pressing Follow restored it; trusted manoeuvres appeared sufficiently early for road-cycling speeds; manoeuvre distance counted down plausibly and advanced correctly; Full, 2 km, 5 km and 10 km elevation views updated during movement; wake lock behaved correctly; and suspension or locking the phone restored a stale fix and then replaced it with a fresh fix (confirmed 17 August 2026).
- **Current-climb interaction and live climb progress:** automatic entry into Climb view, manual exit, and manually returning to Climb view work; a real bicycle climb test additionally confirmed distance completed and remaining, current/summit elevation and elevation remaining updating plausibly, local gradient responding without unreasonable jumping, the chart marker and completed/remaining fill moving through the climb, leaving or completing the climb restoring the normal selected elevation view, and manually leaving Climb view suppressing it for that climb without suppressing a later climb (item 13, confirmed 17 August 2026).
- **Diagnostics and Settings:** layout, wrapping, disclosures and touch targets are satisfactory.
- **PWA recovery:** ordinary reload and suspension recovery work.
- **Wake lock:** previously verified separately on the user's iPhone and working as intended.
- **Waypoint-row marker colour parity:** the list badges' start/ordinary/finish/loop role colours and shapes match the map's own waypoint markers as intended.
- **Ride screen scroll reset and library scroll restoration:** opening a route from Routes and saving a newly planned route from Planning both work as intended.
- **Navigation chrome and waypoint rows:** the sticky navigation safe-area background and its lower buffer look correct; `Delete` stays on the same waypoint row at double-digit waypoint counts; the centred fixed-width ordinal badge slot and aligned labels look correct; waypoint labels stay single-line through waypoint 106, including endpoint and closed-loop start/finish badge roles; the three-digit layout remains acceptably readable (item 34, checked at v0.3.25 / commit `7fae104`).
- **Planning camera framing for restored/seeded waypoints:** the restored/seeded-waypoint Planning view no longer opens at MapLibre's whole-world default zoom and is now framed appropriately (item 35, checked at v0.3.26 / commit `b8cec2c`, confirmed 13 August 2026).
- **Route Library compact grouping:** the pinned/unpinned route list reads correctly with the `Pinned`/`Other routes` headings removed, and pin/search/sort/rename/export/delete behaviour is unaffected (item 33, confirmed 17 August 2026).
- **Compact per-draft routing preferences:** the compact routing summary and its `Change` disclosure work as intended; changing profile or the ferries setting for a draft triggers automatic recalculation and Save stays correctly disabled while the result is stale or recalculating (item 36, confirmed 17 August 2026).
- **Clear draft and draft terminology:** the `Clear draft` action and the "draft" terminology throughout Planning read correctly (item 37, confirmed 17 August 2026).
- **Reverse route inside Planning:** reversing a draft's waypoint order works for both a hand-planned draft and an edit-copy draft; the default reversed name reads `Planned route (reversed)` and a genuinely blank route name stays blank (item 38, confirmed 17 August 2026).
- **Low-zoom surface-warning legibility:** warning line widths are legible at both overview and close zoom on a route carrying warnings (item 39, confirmed 17 August 2026); the warning-over-climb overlap case was not separately checked and is not currently required.
- **Riding information and action proximity:** the climb-selector/route-profile proximity and the top-of-screen End-ride confirmation flow read correctly; closed-loop completion and `Finish ride` are also now confirmed, following a fresh field test of the build carrying item 46's fix (item 40, confirmed 17 August 2026).
- **Ride launcher:** the empty/resumable/missing-route launcher states and their End-ride paths work correctly; the Finish-ride finalisation path is also now confirmed, following a fresh field test of the build carrying item 46's fix (item 41, confirmed 17 August 2026).
- **Closed-loop route completion:** a post-fix real bicycle test of the deployed build confirmed that starting at the shared start/finish did not offer `Finish ride`; completion became armed only after departure and credible interior progress; returning to the shared endpoint retained progress near the route total instead of snapping back to zero; continuing a few metres past the endpoint still allowed completion within the existing proximity allowance; `Route complete` appeared only after the required consecutive eligible fixes; `Finish ride` cleared the unfinished session and returned to the empty Ride launcher; the saved route remained available; and suspension, reload and `Resume riding` during the loop did not break the distinction between starting and finishing (item 46, confirmed 17 August 2026).
- **Free roam (partial — walking test only):** a real-device walk of approximately 10 minutes at ordinary walking speed (not a bicycle ride) confirmed live position following movement, the camera centre following the updated location, a manual map interaction pausing Follow with a Follow press resuming it, North-up working, leaving or suspending the PWA stopping active tracking appropriately, `Resume free roam` restoring a useful camera state and fresh GPS tracking, correct wake-lock behaviour including release and reacquisition around visibility changes, no visible track/speed-history/location-history recording, `End ride` clearing the session and returning to the empty launcher, and no obvious heat or battery-drain problem during this short walk (item 42, confirmed 17 August 2026). Direction-following map rotation remains unverified: the implementation deliberately trusts GPS course only at or above 2.5 m/s (approximately 9 km/h), and this walk never reached that threshold, so the map retaining its initial orientation while continuing to follow position is expected rather than a defect. A dedicated bicycle test above the threshold, including a meaningful turn and stopping afterwards, and longer representative battery/thermal acceptance both remain outstanding; do not describe free roam as fully bicycle-verified until they have been done.

### Preliminarily checked, bicycle field test still pending

- **Free roam bicycle-specific behaviour:** direction-following map rotation (GPS-course-based bearing, trusted only at or above the ~2.5 m/s / 9 km/h threshold), stationary-bearing stability while riding, and broader camera behaviour under road-bike speeds and vibration have not been exercised — only the approximately 10 minute walking test recorded above has been done, and it never reached the direction-following threshold. Longer representative battery/thermal acceptance is also outstanding.

Do not treat the item above as fully verified until it has been tested on a moving road bicycle.

## Future backlog

The following items are approved directions or confirmed bugs for future work. They are recorded here for continuity across sessions and must not be implemented until a future slice explicitly scopes them in.

### Navigation and library interface

6. **Header hierarchy — done**
   - The persistent visible "Amazing Cycling Navigation" heading was removed from the app shell; the product name remains, unchanged, in the document title, PWA manifest and Home Screen identity. Every screen now renders exactly one primary `<h1>` (`.screen-title`): `Routes`, `Ride` when no route is selected, the route's own name once one is, `Plan a route`, `Diagnostics`, or `Settings` — with Settings' nested "OpenRouteService" subsection bumped from `h3` to `h2` to keep a valid descending hierarchy beneath its new `h1`.
   - The previous five unclassed `<nav>` buttons in `App.tsx` became `MainNavigation` (`src/ui/shared/MainNavigation.tsx`, with `src/ui/shared/NavIcon.tsx` supplying five small project-owned inline SVG glyphs, mirroring `ManoeuvreIcon.tsx`'s existing conventions exactly — `aria-hidden`, `fill="currentColor"`, inline `style` only, never a CSS class). It renders a compact, non-sticky, equal-width row of five icon-and-label destinations (`Routes`/`Ride`/`Plan`/`Diagnostics`/`Settings`, unchanged labels and order); the active destination is never colour alone — `aria-current="page"` is the single source of truth, paired with a soft accent surface plus an inset ring (`.main-nav-button[aria-current="page"]`), mirroring the existing `.cycling-profile-button.is-selected` convention. `Screen` (the app's own navigation-state union) now lives in `MainNavigation.tsx` and is exported from there rather than declared locally in `App.tsx`.
   - Riding's own previously-untested Ride-with-no-selected-route state is now a proper empty screen (`Ride` heading, a short explanation, and a primary "Choose a route" action back to Routes) rather than a bare paragraph.

7. **Inline route-deletion confirmation — done**
   - Pressing a route's Delete button now opens a non-modal, per-row confirmation (`role="alertdialog"`, no `aria-modal`) rendered inside that route's own `<li>`, directly beneath its Rename/Export/Delete row, replacing the previous screen-detached `ConfirmDialog`. `RouteLibrary` keeps single-source-of-truth ownership of `pendingDeleteId`/`isDeleting`/`deleteError` and the actual `deleteRoute` call; `RouteListItem` only ever renders its own row's confirmation, so at most one is open at a time and opening a second row's replaces the first.
   - Opening the confirmation moves focus to Cancel; Cancel and Escape-within-the-confirmation both close it without touching storage and return focus to that row's own Delete button — both actions are disabled while a deletion is in flight so Cancel can never appear to undo a delete that storage is still carrying out. Clicking Rename while a delete confirmation is open on the same row cancels the pending delete first, rather than leaving it silently orphaned.
   - On success the reactive `useLiveQuery`-backed list removes the row automatically; a small pure helper (`routeDeleteFocus.ts`) then moves focus to the next, else previous, else (empty library) the Routes heading — with no global selectors or route-name matching, since route names aren't guaranteed unique. A failed deletion keeps the route, shows an inline error, and leaves the confirmation open for cancel or retry; the destructive button reads "Deleting…" while in flight, to prevent double submission.
   - `ConfirmDialog.tsx` itself is unchanged and still backs Settings' "Delete OpenRouteService key" confirmation — only `RouteLibrary`'s own usage of it was removed.
   - Renaming a route (the "Rename" button) is likewise an in-card edit state: `RouteListItem` renders exactly one `<li className="route-card stack">` per route across its normal, renaming and delete-pending states — entering rename mode no longer swaps in a separate, unstyled `<li>` above the list. Distance/ascent stay visible while renaming; Export/Delete (and any open delete confirmation) are fully unrendered, not merely disabled, while renaming; the input autofocuses with its existing name selected; Save and Cancel both return focus to the row's own Rename button, and Escape performs the same discard-and-restore as Cancel, mirroring the delete confirmation's own Cancel/Escape-returns-focus precedent above.

The ninth slice extends trusted manoeuvres to a validated ACN GPX re-import too, via the explicit `ManoeuvreProvenance` field and `hasTrustedManoeuvres` predicate described there. ### Separate feasibility project

12. **Offline map storage**
    - Do not implement until the active tile provider explicitly permits deliberate offline prefetching.
    - Investigate route-corridor/selected-area storage, style/sprite/glyph dependencies, iOS eviction, size estimates and available-storage checks.
    - Preferred eventual architecture: global tile cache keyed by URL, per-route references, deduplication across routes, deletion only when no route references a tile.
    - Offer bounded detail presets and estimate storage before download.
    - Never bulk-prefetch from an OSMF community tile endpoint that prohibits offline download.

### Riding elevation enhancement

13. **Current-climb elevation view (Riding) — done**
    - Milestone 4's eleventh slice completes this item exactly as scoped: Climb view is offered only while the rider is inside an already-recognised climb, auto-opens once per distinct climb, respects a manual dismissal for the remainder of that climb, and reopens automatically on a different climb — see Milestone 4's own eleventh-slice paragraph above for full detail (state machine, metrics, chart fill).
    - One accepted deviation from the original brief's own open question: the heading's "announce meaningful transitions" requirement is implemented via `aria-live="polite"` rather than `role="status"`, since an explicit `role` would have overridden the heading's implicit accessible role.
    - Manual real-device phone acceptance: automatic entry into Climb view, manual exit, manually returning to it, and — following a real bicycle climb test — the live distance/elevation progress while actually cycling through a climb are all now manually verified on the user's iPhone (see "Manual acceptance status" above).
    - Milestone 4's thirteenth slice extends this item: the pre-ride climb selector now also shows a detailed, read-only preview of the selected climb's whole elevation profile (same colours, fill and ground-line as the active view above, no rider marker), reusing one shared pure view-model rather than a second chart implementation — see Milestone 4's own thirteenth-slice paragraph above for full detail. Manually verified on the user's iPhone across multiple climbs (colours, no GPS marker, survives a reload). A fourteenth slice changed the dropdown's own default to "All route" (see the fourteenth-slice paragraph above) after this same real-device check.

### Planning routing profiles

14. **Selectable Planning cycling profiles — done**
    - Milestone 3's fifth slice completes this item: an explicit Road bike (`cycling-road`, default)/General cycling (`cycling-regular`) profile selector, profile-aware leg caching (already supported by the existing cache-key design), draft and saved-route persistence, a stale-result indicator that gates Save/Export until a matching recalculation lands, and GPX round-tripping of the recorded profile via the existing `<acn:source>` extension. See Milestone 3's own fifth-slice paragraph above for full detail.
    - Total cycleway-distance or cycleway-percentage reporting is intentionally not planned. The user does not want this additional statistic, and a provider's cycleway classification is not equivalent to paved surface quality or road-bike suitability; the existing surface analysis remains the relevant measure for this app. Do not add this reporting unless the user explicitly changes the scope.

### Settings defaults

15. **Avoid ferries by default — done**
    - Implemented in the second Settings-visual-migration slice — see this file's own Interface and accessibility section (the `src/index.css`/Settings paragraph) for full detail: a locally persisted `Route planning` setting, backed by a new singleton `planningPreferences` table (schema v3), resolving to `avoidFerriesByDefault: true` whenever no row exists.
    - A genuinely fresh Planning draft (no draft row, or a draft row with zero waypoints — the pre-existing draft-lifecycle boundary) copies this setting into the draft's own `avoidFerries` field exactly once; a restored draft always keeps its own already-stored value, and changing the Settings default afterwards never rewrites, recalculates, or otherwise touches an already-open draft.
    - Planning's previous directly editable "Avoid ferries" checkbox is removed; Planning now shows the current draft's own stored ferry policy as concise read-only text ("Ferries: avoided/allowed for this plan") next to a "Change default in Settings" action, reusing the existing `onNavigateToSettings` path.

### Planning visual-organisation follow-ups

16. **Desktop two-column Planning layout**
    - The fourth visual-migration slice (Interface and accessibility section) deliberately kept Planning as a single, width-constrained column (`.planning-section`, max-width 720px) rather than a two-column grid, since the brief explicitly permitted either and a single column carries no DOM/tab-order risk.
    - If pursued later, use CSS Grid with explicit `grid-template-areas` placement while keeping JSX/DOM order linear (Waypoints → Route options → Route overview → Save/export) — never reorder the markup itself to achieve a visual pairing, so keyboard and reading order stay sequential regardless of visual column position.

17. **Waypoint-row marker colour parity — done**
    - `src/map/planningLayer.ts`'s new `deriveWaypointRoles(coordinates)` derives each waypoint's ordinary/start/finish/combined-start-finish role purely from position, and is now the single source both `buildWaypointMarkerSpecs` (the map marker) and `WaypointList` (via `PlanningScreen.tsx`'s new `waypointRoles` prop) are built from — never two independently-guessed `index === 0`/`index === last` checks. Unlike the map's own collapsed "1/n" combined marker for a closed loop, the list keeps both the first and final row with their own individual ordinal number, sharing only the visual role — see `deriveWaypointRoles`'s own doc comment. `MapMarkerRole` (`src/map/mapAdapter.ts`) is renamed `WaypointRole`, its single other reference site being `MapMarkerSpec.role` itself.
    - `.waypoint-row-ordinal`'s base rule and three new `--start`/`--finish`/`--start-finish` modifiers (`src/index.css`) mirror `.planning-waypoint-marker`'s own colours/border-widths/shape literally, using the same hardcoded hex values — deliberately not `--colour-accent`/`--colour-border-strong`, since the map marker's own colours never respond to `prefers-color-scheme` while those tokens do; a variable-driven badge would only match the map in light mode. The badge stays smaller than the map marker (24px vs 26px, a decorative list element, not a map marker) and omits the map's extra `--start-finish` padding, since the list badge's label is always a single ordinal number, never the map's combined "1/n".
    - The ordinal badge remains purely decorative (`aria-hidden`) and its row's own accessible name/text ("Start"/"Waypoint N") is unchanged; no routing, selection, persistence or map-marker-lifecycle behaviour changed. Automated tests (unit, component and a real-browser Playwright check comparing computed badge/marker styles at the phone viewport) pass, and the visual result is manually verified on the deployed iPhone PWA (see "Manual acceptance status" above).

### Navigation and library interface

18. **Ride screen opens from the top; library scroll position remembered for the session — done**
    - Opening a route from Routes, or saving a newly planned route from Planning, previously kept whatever document scroll offset Routes (or Planning) happened to be at, so a route opened from far down a long library — or saved after scrolling deep into a long Planning form — could land on Riding already scrolled away from its own heading. A new hook, `useResetScrollForNewRideContent` (`src/ui/shared/`), scrolls the document to `(0, 0)` with `behavior: "auto"` (never animated) the instant `App.tsx`'s `screen` becomes `"riding"` and a token it owns internally has advanced past whatever it last actually applied, bumped only by `handleOpenRoute` and `handleRouteSaved` — App's only two genuine "a new route is now the selected ride" call sites. It never fires on a plain nav-tab click back to an already-open ride: `RidingScreen` always fully unmounts/remounts on every screen switch (no `key` anywhere in `App.tsx`'s conditional screen rendering), so only `App` itself — which never unmounts — can tell "genuinely new" apart from "already open."
    - `RouteLibrary` gained one new optional prop, `restoreScrollYRef` (a plain `RefObject<number | null>`, never a dereferenced value — reading a ref's `.current` during render would both trip this project's `react-hooks/refs` lint rule and not actually work, since a ref mutation alone never triggers the re-render needed to pick up a changed prop). `App.tsx`'s `handleOpenRoute` captures `window.scrollY` into this ref synchronously, as the very first statement of the click handler, strictly before any state setter — the only point guaranteed to run before React unmounts the library's tall content (a browser's own scroll clamping only ever bounds `scrollY` to the new, shorter document's max, never zeroes it, so an explicit reset is required regardless). `RouteLibrary` restores it, via `window.scrollTo`, inside a `useLayoutEffect` gated on real route cards having rendered (never the "Loading routes…" placeholder), applies it at most once per mount, and always nulls the ref out afterwards, so neither a later reactive `useLiveQuery` update from the same mount (rename/delete) nor an unrelated later remount (e.g. Routes → Diagnostics → Routes with no intervening route-open) ever reapplies a stale offset. Both refs live only in `App`'s own in-memory state — nothing is persisted to IndexedDB or any storage, so a full reload always starts Routes at the top, and this has no effect on the restored-ride map camera contract, GPS state, progress restoration, or the Resume-riding route framing.
    - Automated coverage: `App.test.tsx`, `src/ui/library/RouteLibrary.test.tsx`, a new `src/ui/shared/useResetScrollForNewRideContent.test.ts` (needed because `App.test.tsx` cannot mount a real `PlanningScreen` in jsdom — see that file's own "Deliberately excludes 'Plan'" comment — so the Planning-save leg of this contract is proven at the unit level), plus a new `e2e/routeLibraryScroll.spec.ts` and one new assertion added to `e2e/planning.spec.ts`'s existing save-and-reopen test. `App` also gained a new optional `mapFactory` prop, forwarded only to `RidingScreen`, purely so these tests can inject a stub map and avoid mounting a real, unmocked MapView in jsdom — production's `main.tsx` passes no props and is unaffected. Manually verified on the deployed iPhone PWA (see "Manual acceptance status" above).

19. **Route library search and sort — done**
    - `RouteLibrary` gained a session-only name search (a labelled `<input type="search">`, "Search routes") and a persistent sort control (a labelled `<select>`, "Sort by": `Most recent`/`Name A–Z`). Search matches a trimmed, case- and diacritic-insensitive substring anywhere in a route's name only — never fuzzy, never tags/folders/route-content — via Unicode NFD normalisation (e.g. a query of "hutte" matches "Hütte"). Both are computed by a new pure `src/ui/library/routeLibraryView.ts` (`normalizeSearchText`/`filterRoutesByName`/`sortRoutesForLibrary`/`selectRouteLibraryView`), applied to whatever `listRoutes()`'s own liveQuery returns, never assumed to already match — the pipeline is always normalise query → filter → sort → render, and neither function mutates its input array. `Most recent` preserves `PlannedRoute.createdAt` descending exactly as before (opening/exporting/renaming a route never makes it "recent"; no `updatedAt`/last-opened field was added); `Name A–Z` uses a module-level `Intl.Collator("en-GB", { sensitivity: "base", numeric: true })` (a pinned locale, matching `providerKeyStatus.ts`'s existing `DATE_TIME_FORMATTER` convention, so ordering is deterministic across machines) so e.g. "Route 2" sorts before "Route 10". Both orders are tie-broken deterministically by route id.
    - Sort choice is the first Route-library-specific durable preference: a new singleton `routeLibraryPreferences` table (`id: "route-library"`, schema `version(4)`, purely additive like v1→v2→v3, no `.upgrade()` needed) holds a plain `sortOrder` string; `src/storage/mapping.ts`'s `RouteLibrarySortOrder`/`isRouteLibrarySortOrder`/`fromStoredRouteLibraryPreferences` guard-validate it on read (mirroring `fromStoredPlanningDraft`'s profile guard, not `fromStoredPlanningPreferences`'s bare `??`, since this is a closed enum) and default an absent or corrupt value to `most-recent`. `src/storage/routeLibraryPreferencesRepository.ts` mirrors `planningPreferencesRepository.ts` exactly. `RouteLibrary` waits for both the routes liveQuery and this preference to resolve before rendering any list or the toolbar, avoiding a "Most recent, then jump to Name A–Z" flash; the `<select>`'s value is always bound directly to the live-query-resolved preference, never optimistic local state, so a failed or in-flight save can never look like it succeeded — on failure it keeps showing the last-persisted value alongside a `role="alert"` message, with a `role="status"` "Saving…" note only while a write is genuinely in flight.
    - The search query is deliberately not persisted to IndexedDB — it's transient view state that may clear on a full reload/relaunch. Within the current app session it survives navigating away from Routes to any other screen and back, via a new `routesSearchQueryRef` owned by `App.tsx` (a `RefObject<string>`, continuously synced on every keystroke rather than captured once like `restoreScrollYRef`, since — unlike the Riding-open path — there is no single "about to navigate away from Routes" call site covering every destination; `MainNavigation`'s `onNavigate` goes straight to `setScreen`). `RouteLibrary` hydrates its local `searchQuery` state from this ref exactly once per mount via a `useLayoutEffect` with an empty-ish dependency array (never a lazy `useState` initializer, for the same `react-hooks/refs` reason `restoreScrollYRef` documents), and never nulls the ref afterwards.
    - The existing scroll-restoration effect and `computeFocusRouteIdAfterDelete` (`src/ui/library/routeDeleteFocus.ts`, itself unchanged — it was already order-agnostic) now both operate on the filtered/sorted array actually on screen rather than the raw liveQuery result. Deleting the last visible route under an active filter focuses the search field rather than the Routes heading. A rename that causes the renamed route to drop out of the active filtered view (and so unmount its own row, stranding any focus inside it) is handled by a small, explicitly-scoped effect — tracking the just-renamed id and comparing the previous rendered view to the new one — rather than a generic "was focus just here" check, since by the time any effect can observe a removed row the browser has already moved focus to `<body>`, making rename-caused and delete-caused disappearance indistinguishable at that point; a `stillExists` guard defers to the delete flow's own focus handling if the same route was deleted before the rename's write round-tripped. Reordering under `Name A–Z` needs no equivalent code: `RouteListItem` stays keyed by `route.id`, so React's key-based reconciliation moves the existing DOM node (and any focus within it) rather than unmounting it.
    - No fuzzy search, tags, folders, route-content search, remote search, analytics or new dependency; `Intl.Collator`/Unicode `normalize("NFD")` are Web-platform-native. No change to import, export, inline rename/delete, route selection or GPX/Riding behaviour. Automated tests (storage mapping/repository/migration, pure view-logic unit tests, `RouteLibrary`/`App` component tests, and a phone-viewport `e2e/routeLibrarySearchSort.spec.ts`) pass; real-device iPhone verification of search, reload persistence, narrow-viewport layout and returning from a searched/sorted route is still outstanding — do not treat this item as manually verified until that check has actually been done.

20. **Route pinning — done**
    - Persistent Route Library pinning, exactly as recorded for continuity above: pinned routes form one group, `Pinned`, always shown above unpinned routes; within `Pinned` the most recently pinned route is always first, ordered purely by pin recency; the existing persisted `Most recent`/`Name A–Z` preference applies only within the unpinned group (`Other routes`) and never reorders `Pinned`. `PlannedRoute` gained one new optional field, `pinnedAt?: string | null` (`src/domain/types.ts`) — an ISO timestamp when pinned, `null`/absent when not, documented as local Route Library metadata rather than route geometry or provenance, never read by `gpx/`, `routing/` or `navigation/`. Because it is optional and never indexed, it needed no Dexie schema version bump — the exact same precedent already established for `manoeuvreProvenance`/`surfaceSummary` on `PlannedRoute` and the camera/wake-lock fields on `StoredRideState`: Dexie's `version().stores()` only lists indexes, so a plain new field on an existing row is handled entirely by explicit defaulting in application code, not a schema migration.
    - `src/storage/routesRepository.ts` gained `pinRoute(id, clock: Clock = systemClock)` (writes `pinnedAt: new Date(clock.now()).toISOString()`) and `unpinRoute(id)` (writes `pinnedAt: null`, not `undefined`, the correct member of the `string | null` union for Dexie's `update()`), mirroring `renameRoute`'s own `db.routes.update(id, {...})` shape and `src/gpx/importGpx.ts`'s existing `(file, clock: Clock = systemClock)` convention so pin timestamps are testable without `vi.setSystemTime`. Deleting a route naturally deletes its pin metadata with it — there is no separate pin table to leave an orphan row in. GPX export/import were confirmed structurally incapable of touching `pinnedAt` (`exportGpx.ts`/`normalizeGpx.ts` both name every field explicitly, with no `{...route}` spread anywhere in either file) and needed no code change, only regression tests: a pinned route exports with no pin metadata in the GPX, and any reimported route — including a re-export/reimport of a previously pinned one — is unpinned. Pin state is specific to this device's local library, exactly like sort order and search.
    - `src/ui/library/routeLibraryView.ts` gained `isPinnedRoute` (a route counts as pinned only when `pinnedAt` is a string that parses to a finite timestamp — missing, `null`, or malformed local data is treated as unpinned rather than destabilising ordering) and `selectRouteLibraryGroups(routes, query, sortOrder): { pinned, unpinned }`, superseding the previous flat `selectRouteLibraryView`: normalise the query → filter by name (the existing, unchanged `filterRoutesByName`) → partition into pinned/unpinned by `isPinnedRoute` → sort pinned by `pinnedAt` descending (id-tiebroken, never consulting `sortOrder` at all, so a sort-order change can never touch `Pinned` even in principle) → sort unpinned via the existing, unchanged `sortRoutesForLibrary`. The partition is exhaustive and disjoint by construction (a plain filter/predicate split), proven directly by a dedicated unit test rather than left implicit.
    - `RouteLibrary.tsx` derives `groups = selectRouteLibraryGroups(...)` and a flattened `viewRoutes = [...groups.pinned, ...groups.unpinned]` kept feeding exactly what it already fed before this slice — `computeFocusRouteIdAfterDelete`, the rename-orphan-focus effect, and scroll-restoration gating — so deletion focus continues to use the full displayed pinned-then-unpinned order with no change to `routeDeleteFocus.ts` itself. Rendering splits into up to two `<h2>`-headed `<ul className="route-list">` groups — `Pinned` shown only when non-empty, `Other routes` shown only when both groups are non-empty — so with zero pinned routes the DOM is byte-for-byte identical to before this slice. A new optional `clock?: Clock` prop (default `systemClock`), mirroring `PlanningScreen`'s own existing convention, lets tests assert deterministic newest-pinned-first ordering without depending on real clicks landing in different milliseconds.
    - Pin/unpin writes are never optimistic: because `routes` is a Dexie live query, a card only ever visually moves once the write has actually committed and the query re-fires, so "never move a card before the write succeeds" and "keep the previous group/order on a failed write" both fall out of the existing architecture with no extra bookkeeping — a rejected write simply never touches `routes`. A per-route `pinPendingIds` set disables that route's own toggle and blocks a duplicate submission while its write is in flight; a per-route `pinErrors` map shows a concise `role="alert"` message on failure. Because pinned and unpinned render as two separate `.map()`-produced arrays under two separate `<ul>` parents, a route moving between them is an unmount-then-mount pair even though its `key` is unchanged — React does not carry a component's DOM identity, and therefore focus, across that move the way it would for a plain reorder within one array. A `pendingPinFocusIdRef` set on a successful write, consumed by an effect keyed on `groups`, explicitly refocuses the route's own toggle at its new position (an accepted, narrow race — pinning a second route before the first one's effect has fired overwrites the pending target — documented the same way as the pre-existing `lastRenamedIdRef` rename-orphan-focus race, and equally sub-perceptible in practice).
    - `RouteListItem.tsx` gained one compact, icon-only pin toggle in the title row's upper-right, via a new `.route-card-title-row` (`display: grid; grid-template-columns: minmax(0, 1fr) auto`, plus `min-width: 0` added to the existing `.route-card-title` rule) so a long/wrapping route name never collides with or displaces the fixed-size toggle. A new project-owned, dependency-free `src/ui/library/PinIcon.tsx` (mirroring `ManoeuvreIcon.tsx`'s exact convention — `aria-hidden`, all styling inline since Vitest's `test: { css: false }` never loads `index.css`) renders an outline pushpin when unpinned and a filled one when pinned, never rotated; `.route-pin-toggle`/`.route-pin-toggle.is-pinned` add only the real CSS deltas on top of the global `button` rule's own inherited 44px touch target, mirroring `.waypoint-row-icon-button`'s override-only-the-delta convention — the pinned state additionally gets a pale `--colour-accent-soft` surface and a stronger accent ring, mirroring `.main-nav-button[aria-current="page"]`'s soft-surface-plus-ring convention, so pinned state is never colour alone. `aria-pressed`/a full route-scoped accessible label (`Pin Evening loop`/`Unpin Evening loop`) carry the state explicitly; no `Pin`/`Unpin` text is ever shown inside the button itself. The toggle is deliberately **not** disabled merely because that row's delete confirmation is open (only while a pin write is genuinely in flight, or while deleting) — clicking it while a confirmation is open cancels that confirmation first via a local `handlePinClick`, mirroring exactly where the equivalent Rename-cancels-delete logic already lived in this same file (`openRename`), rather than lifting that per-row concern into `RouteLibrary`. The toggle is unavailable during inline rename with no extra logic at all, since it lives inside the same "not renaming" fragment as the rest of the card's normal-state markup. The per-card delete-confirmation heading was downgraded from `h2` to `h3` (and its CSS selector updated to match) so it sits one level beneath the Route Library's own new `Pinned`/`Other routes` `h2` group headings rather than as a same-level sibling.
    - No drag-and-drop or manual ordering, folders, tags, favourites separate from pins, cloud synchronisation, route duplication or new search modes were added, matching this item's own original scope note. Automated tests (pure grouping/partition unit tests including an explicit disjointness check, repository tests for `pinRoute`/`unpinRoute` with a literal fixed `Clock`, a migration-test assertion that a legacy route survives with `pinnedAt` absent, GPX export/reimport regression tests, `RouteListItem`/`RouteLibrary` component tests covering the toggle's states/labels/focus/error/duplicate-click/delete-cancel-first behaviour, and a phone-viewport `e2e/routeLibraryPinning.spec.ts`) pass; real-device iPhone verification of pin order, reload persistence, search/sort interaction, long route names, touch targets and returning from an opened pinned route is still outstanding — do not treat this item as manually verified until that check has actually been done.

21. **`e2e/planning.spec.ts`'s "pressing Northwards twice" test's manual-rotation precondition — done**
    - Following the v0.3.10 Route Library search/sort push, a GitHub Actions run of this desktop-viewport test failed at its own precondition — `data-camera-bearing` never left `"0"` after the synthetic right-button drag, so the second Northwards press was never actually exercised — the exact `moveend`/`rotateend`-never-fires signature already documented for the fractional-height MapLibre drag-rotate bug elsewhere in this file. A same-commit re-run of the identical job passed, and nothing in that push touched Planning, its map, or camera code (confirmed by diff inspection before the re-run), so this is treated as a further occurrence of that same known, narrow, CI-environment-sensitive MapLibre quirk — not a Northwards regression — and no production camera-logic change was made on the strength of a single failure.
    - Hardened in both the desktop test and its phone-viewport twin (`e2e/planning.spec.ts`, "pressing Northwards twice on a phone viewport…"): the synthetic right-button pointer drag through MapLibre's `DragRotateHandler` — the genuinely non-deterministic step, since a drag/inertia gesture can in principle finish without the handler ever firing `moveend`/`rotateend` — is replaced by MapLibre's own built-in `KeyboardHandler` (confirmed by reading the installed v6.0.0 source directly: `keyboard.ts`, `handler_manager.ts`, `camera.ts`, `map.ts`). Both tests now focus the actual `<canvas class="maplibregl-canvas">` inside `[data-testid="map-container"]` — MapLibre sets `tabindex="0"` on the canvas itself, not the container div (`map.ts`'s `_setupContainer`) — and send `page.keyboard.press("Shift+ArrowRight")` then, once that settles, `"Shift+ArrowUp"`: each is one discrete trusted keydown+keyup that `KeyboardHandler.keydown()` turns into a single fixed +15° bearing or +10° pitch `map.easeTo()` call through MapLibre's own ordinary camera-animation pipeline (`_prepareEase` → `movestart`/`rotatestart` → per-frame `move`/`rotate` → `_afterEase` → `rotateend` → `moveend`) — the exact pipeline a real drag, or this app's own North-up reset (`mapAdapter.ts`'s `setCamera`), already uses, so `MapView.tsx`'s `onCameraSettled`-driven `data-camera-*` attributes are republished through the real production code path, never set directly. Because there is no drag/inertia state machine in this path at all, the mechanism cannot reproduce the stuck-gesture failure mode by construction, not merely retry past it faster; the two key presses are sent sequentially, only once the first has fully settled, since both share MapLibre's fixed `easeId: "keyboardHandler"` and an in-flight same-id ease would otherwise suppress the earlier one's own end events. Both tests also gained an explicit `aria-pressed` assertion of the North-up control around the rotation and each press — real-browser coverage of the same contract `PlanningScreen.test.tsx`'s "a manual rotation unpresses the control" already proves at the mock level — and the desktop test alone gained new coverage this test never had before: a numeric-tolerance check (matching the existing "Locate me recentres…" test's own `CENTRE_CHANGE_TOLERANCE_DEGREES`-style pattern) that centre and zoom, captured once settled after the first press, are still preserved after the second, proving `setCamera(null, null, 0, 0, …)`'s contract across both presses at runtime rather than only by reading the source. Independent real pointer-drag-rotate coverage of MapLibre's `DragRotateHandler` itself remains intact and untouched: `e2e/directionArrows.spec.ts`'s "arrows remain attached and correctly oriented after the map is rotated" test still performs a real right-button drag and asserts, via decoded canvas pixel angle detection, that the rendered route line's on-screen angle genuinely changed — this is test hardening only, since no production code changed at all (`mapAdapter.ts`/`MapView.tsx`/`PlanningScreen.tsx` are untouched, and the `requestId`-dedup fix itself already has full unit coverage in `MapView.test.tsx`'s "Northwards-pressed-twice regression" test and `PlanningScreen.test.tsx`'s north-up `aria-pressed` suite) — this slice only changes how the e2e test establishes its precondition. Verified via `npx playwright test e2e/planning.spec.ts --grep "pressing Northwards twice" --repeat-each=20 --workers=1`, both locally (40/40 passed) and again with `CI=1` set, which forces a fresh preview-server boot each run (40/40 passed), plus a full `npm run e2e` run (62/62 passed, including the independent `directionArrows.spec.ts` drag-rotate test). This is not a claim that every MapLibre pointer gesture is universally deterministic in CI generally — only that this specific regression test no longer depends on one.

### Planning waypoint interaction

22. **Deselect a waypoint on a repeat tap — done**
    - Amends the existing waypoint-selection and relocation behaviour rather than introducing a new movement architecture: placing, dragging, moving, inserting, reordering and deleting waypoints (see "## Planning behaviour" above and Future-backlog item 17's `WaypointList` work) are already implemented and unchanged by this item. Before this item, `waypointHistory.ts`'s `"select"` action always set `selectedWaypointId` to the tapped waypoint, with no toggle-off case.
    - In the normal selected state, tapping the already-selected waypoint again deselects it (`selectedWaypointId` returns to `null`). Selecting a different waypoint continues to transfer selection to that waypoint, exactly as today.
    - Once an active relocation (Move/Insert-after) operation has begun, tapping the waypoint again must not silently cancel or commit the move — the explicit Cancel action remains the only way to cancel a relocation in progress.
    - Preserve the existing route recalculation, undo/redo and stale-result (`isStale`/`canSaveOrExportPlan`) protections unchanged.
    - Implemented as a single-call-site change: `PlanningScreen.tsx`'s `onSelect` handler passed to `WaypointList` now dispatches `{ type: "select", waypointId: null }` instead of the tapped id when the tapped waypoint is already `state.selectedWaypointId` **and** no Move/Insert-after relocation is currently pending for it (`effectivePendingAction === null`) — otherwise it dispatches the tapped id unchanged, exactly as before. `waypointHistory.ts`'s `"select"` reducer case already accepted a `null` id, already excluded selection from undo/redo, and `usePlanningRoute` already never read `selectedWaypointId`, so no reducer, staleness, `WaypointList`, `MapView` or map-marker code changed — list-row `aria-pressed`/`.is-selected` and the map marker's `--selected` ring already reacted correctly to a cleared selection via existing plumbing. Real-device iPhone verification is still outstanding — do not treat this item as manually verified until that check has actually been done.

### Map zoom-dependent route and waypoint presentation

23. **Low-zoom route-colour and waypoint-marker legibility — done**
    - Confirmed visual bug, evidenced on the user's phone: at low zoom, the geographically drawn route in Planning, the pre-ride overview and active Riding can appear predominantly blue even though the underlying recognised climb/descent classification (`src/navigation/routeFeatures.ts`, `src/map/routeFeatureLayer.ts`, `src/map/gradientRouteLayer.ts`) has not changed. The elevation chart is unaffected by map zoom and is out of scope for this item. Planning is additionally obscured by the existing fixed-size numbered waypoint markers (Future-backlog item 17) once many waypoints are visible together.
    - Required outcome: preserve the existing climb/descent detection, category boundaries and colour meanings exactly as they are; make the map's route presentation responsive to zoom so a full-route overview stays legible and no single colour/layer visually overwhelms nearby route sections purely because the view has been zoomed out; restore the existing close-range widths as the rider zooms in so active Riding stays as easy to read as it is today; apply the resulting styling consistently across Planning, the pre-ride overview and active Riding.
    - Implementation direction to validate, not yet a proven fix: investigate the existing MapLibre sources, layers, draw order, and line caps/joins before changing anything; the likely direction is a progressively thinner route at lower zoom, with a thin neutral route casing/base beneath the coloured climb/descent/gradient/warning overlays, the overlays themselves staying narrower at overview zoom than at close range. Do not record a fixed line width as an already-proven sole root cause. Separately verify that layer draw order does not systematically paint descent segments over climb segments where the route geographically overlaps itself.
    - For Planning's waypoint markers: reduce their _visual_ size at low zoom while keeping at least a 44 × 44 px effective touch target; keep the start marker, the selected waypoint and waypoint ordinal numbers individually identifiable; do not cluster waypoints or discard their individual identities.
    - Add focused unit/integration coverage of the zoom-derived presentation logic, plus visual/e2e coverage at representative close, regional and full-route zoom levels, proving the underlying route data and classifications are unchanged — only their presentation is.
    - **Evidence-backed root cause**, confirmed by direct source inspection: `src/map/mapAdapter.ts`'s `LineLayerPaint.lineWidth`/`CircleLayerPaint.circleRadius` were plain numbers, and MapLibre renders `line-width` in fixed _screen_ pixels regardless of zoom — no zoom expression existed anywhere in the codebase, so the same pixel width covers ever more geographic distance as the map zooms out, and nearby/parallel/self-crossing route sections visually merge. This was compounded, not caused, by a second fact: `MapView.tsx`'s `addRouteAndPositionLayers` gave the neutral base route (`acn-route-remaining-line`/`acn-route-completed-line`, width 5) and the always-on macro climb/descent overlay (`acn-route-feature-line`, width 5, populated for _every_ recognised climb/descent along the whole route, not just a selection) the exact same width at every zoom, so the coloured overlay always fully covered the neutral base with no visible casing — a route with a long recognised descent therefore read as solid blue regardless of zoom. Separately verified, via a new geographic self-overlap fixture in `routeFeatureLayer.test.ts` (an out-and-back where a climb and a coincident, reversed-direction descent occupy identical geometry): `detectRouteFeatures` (`routeFeatures.ts`) pushes features in plain route-distance order with no kind-based sort, and `buildRouteFeatureFeatureCollection` preserves that order into the one shared GeoJSON layer — there is **no** category-based "descents always drawn over climbs" bug; whichever feature is later by route-distance simply happens to be later in paint order, which is a property of route shape, not category. No feature-ordering change was made.
    - **Route-width policy**: a new pure, MapLibre-free module, `src/map/routeWidthPolicy.ts`, exports three shared zoom bands (`ROUTE_WIDTH_OVERVIEW_ZOOM = 6`, `ROUTE_WIDTH_REGIONAL_ZOOM = 11`, `ROUTE_WIDTH_CLOSE_ZOOM = 15` — the last deliberately equal to `distanceBadgeLayer.ts`'s own `DISTANCE_BADGE_STREET_ZOOM_MIN`, one shared "close navigation" boundary) and two pure functions, `recedingWidthStops(closeWidthPx)`/`legibleWidthStops(closeWidthPx)`, each building a `{ stops: [{zoom, width}, …] }` value from a layer's own unchanged close-zoom width via one multiplier family (recede-faster for the always-on macro/micro colour overlays; recede-gentler for the neutral base/casing, selection halos and warning casings). Both families resolve to exactly the input width at `ROUTE_WIDTH_CLOSE_ZOOM` — today's appearance is unchanged at close zoom, by construction — and the gentler "legible" family stays strictly wider than the "receding" family below that, so the neutral base begins to peek through as a visible casing ring around the narrower coloured overlay once zoomed out, without adding a second route-colour pipeline or a new layer. These zoom bands and multipliers are first-pass, reasoned starting points, explicitly **not yet verified on a real iPhone viewport** — mirroring `distanceBadgeLayer.ts`'s own documented humility for its zoom bands.
    - **MapLibre plumbing**: `mapAdapter.ts` gained `ZoomWidthStop`/`ZoomInterpolatedLineWidth` types (mirroring `DataDrivenLineColor`'s existing narrow, project-owned-abstraction convention rather than exposing a raw MapLibre expression), widening `LineLayerPaint.lineWidth` to `number | ZoomInterpolatedLineWidth`; `addLineLayer` translates a non-number width into a native `["interpolate", ["linear"], ["zoom"], …]` expression at the same single-field-cast site its existing `lineColor` handling already uses. `CircleLayerPaint.circleRadius` (position/start/finish circles) was deliberately left untouched — out of this item's own scope. Because MapLibre itself evaluates the expression per render frame, `addLineLayer` is still called exactly once per layer at style-load — **no new React state or effect drives route width at all**, satisfying the "must not cause a React state update on every zoom frame" constraint by construction. `MapView.tsx`'s `addRouteAndPositionLayers` now wraps every route/warning/preview layer's width in the appropriate policy call, with its existing "nested-ring" draw-order comment left unchanged (draw order still governs which layer wins where they overlap; width now separately governs how visible that relationship is at a given zoom) plus one added paragraph recording this. `addRouteAndPositionLayers` has no Planning/Riding mode branch and none was added, so the two screens resolve identical widths for the same zoom by construction, proven by a dedicated regression test rather than left implicit.
    - **Waypoint-marker zoom scaling**: implemented with zero changes to `MapMarkerSpec`, `setMarkers`, or `waypointMarkerElement.ts` (so the tested "never `element.className =`" hazard in that file is never touched). The map's own container div (`data-testid="map-container"`, an ancestor of every MapLibre `Marker` DOM node) gained a stable `map-canvas-host` class and a new `data-marker-zoom-band` attribute (`"close" | "regional" | "overview"`), populated from the existing settle-quantised `distanceBadgeZoom` state (reused unrenamed, not duplicated into a second zoom-tracking state) via a new pure `deriveMarkerZoomBand(zoom)` in `planningLayer.ts` (mirroring `distanceBadgeLayer.ts`'s own `zoomToBandIndex` band-lookup shape; a non-finite zoom, i.e. before the first camera settle, resolves to `"close"`, matching the marker's current unchanged full-size appearance). New CSS-only descendant rules in `src/index.css` (`.map-canvas-host[data-marker-zoom-band="…"] .planning-waypoint-marker`) cascade the size reduction (~26px close → ~20px regional → ~16px overview, plus a narrower selection ring at the smallest band) to every marker with zero per-marker JS work and no marker rebuild — `setMarkers`'s own effect is untouched and never re-runs on a zoom-only camera settle, proven by a dedicated test.
    - **Touch-target decision** (confirmed with the user before implementation): `.planning-waypoint-marker` was, and remains, `pointer-events: none` — it is not, and has never been, an interactive tap target; waypoint selection is exclusively `WaypointList`'s own rows, which already satisfy the app's global `min-width/height: var(--touch-target-min)` (44px) at every zoom, unaffected by any of this. Rather than restructuring the marker's DOM into a fixed 44×44 invisible hit-box around a smaller visual glyph (which would have no functional effect today, since the element still wouldn't be clickable), this slice does a **visual-only CSS shrink** and documents, in both `index.css`'s own comment and here, that `WaypointList` remains the sole ≥44×44px waypoint-selection surface at every zoom — satisfying the requirement's substance without an interaction-model change. Selection, deselection-on-repeat-tap (item 22) and active-relocation behaviour are all unaffected; this was proven, not merely asserted, by e2e coverage that selects then deselects a waypoint at overview zoom.
    - **Tests**: new `src/map/routeWidthPolicy.test.ts` (monotonicity, close-zoom-unchanged, casing-wider-than-overlay); new cases in `mapAdapter.test.ts` (the generated `interpolate` expression shape) and `MapView.test.tsx` (every layer's real policy-derived paint, Planning/Riding parity, zoom-invariant classified-feature source data, a climb+descent fixture retaining both classes at overview zoom, the `data-marker-zoom-band` transition, no marker rebuild on a zoom-only settle, and fallback-style regression); new `deriveMarkerZoomBand` boundary tests in `planningLayer.test.ts`; the out-and-back self-overlap fixture in `routeFeatureLayer.test.ts`. A new e2e spec, `e2e/lowZoomLegibility.spec.ts`, uses MapLibre's own real, deterministic `KeyboardHandler` (`Shift+=`/`Shift+-`, the same class of mechanism `planning.spec.ts`'s "pressing Northwards twice" test already uses for bearing/pitch, chosen for the same reason — no drag/inertia state machine to reproduce CI's documented `DragRotateHandler` flakiness) rather than a synthetic pointer/wheel gesture, and proves: a dense ~14-waypoint Planning fixture's markers visibly shrink at overview zoom and restore exactly at close zoom, while staying individually identifiable (start/ordinary/selected) and the `WaypointList` touch target stays ≥44×44px throughout; the same holds under the local fallback style; and a calculated route keeps rendering (`data-route-loaded` stays `"true"`, the canvas stays visible) across a full zoom-out-and-back cycle in both Planning and the saved route's pre-ride Riding overview. This exposed one real, narrow regression in the _pre-existing_ `planning.spec.ts` "waypoint list badges visually match…" test (item 17): its implicit assumption that a badge and its map marker match at _any_ zoom no longer holds, since the list badge is deliberately not zoom-responsive while the map marker's size now is — fixed by a small local `zoomToCloseRange` helper (mirroring the new spec's own `zoomToBand`, duplicated per this file's established no-shared-e2e-helpers-across-specs convention) that deterministically zooms to the close band before that test's own style comparisons, preserving its original claim exactly rather than loosening it. Real-device iPhone verification is still outstanding — do not treat this item as manually verified until that check has actually been done.

### Navigation and library interface

24. **Always-sticky compact primary navigation, except during active Riding — done**
    - `MainNavigation` (Future-backlog item 6) is sticky on every screen — Routes, Planning, Diagnostics, Settings, the pre-ride briefing and paused/awaiting-Resume riding — except while a ride is genuinely being GPS-tracked, where it stays in normal document flow to maximise dashboard space; it never auto-hides. The state predicate is `nav.geolocationStatus !== "idle"` (`useRideNavigation.ts`) — the app's own authoritative ride-tracking state, never inferred from button text, GPS freshness, route presence or CSS, and identical to the predicate `RidingScreen.tsx` already used for the map's active/overview class, the wake-lock gate and the next-manoeuvre panel. A transient GPS error mid-ride (`"error"`) deliberately still counts as active, since the underlying watch is never torn down for it, matching this file's own existing "Try again preserves progress" philosophy.
    - A new pure module, `src/ui/shared/navPositionMode.ts`, exports `NavPositionMode` (`"sticky" | "static"`) and `deriveNavPositionMode(screen, isRidingActive): NavPositionMode` — returning `"static"` only for `screen === "riding" && isRidingActive`. `App.tsx` computes `positionMode` from this and applies it as a state-dependent modifier class directly to its own `<header>` element (`className={positionMode === "sticky" ? "app-header--sticky" : undefined}`) — `MainNavigation`/`<nav>` itself carries no positioning class and has no opinion about sticky/static at all.
    - **A real, confirmed field bug, found via the deployed iPhone PWA at build 0.3.14**: the first implementation of this item put `position: sticky` directly on `.main-nav` (`<nav>`), nested inside a bare `<header>` that only ever wraps that one nav and so is barely taller than it. A sticky element's stuck range is bounded by its own containing block — with `<header>` as `<nav>`'s containing block and almost no height beyond the nav itself, the nav had essentially no room to remain stuck before scrolling away with that too-short header regardless of the sticky declaration being otherwise correct. Real iOS Safari confirmed the nav was not sticky on Diagnostics (and everywhere else). This was root-caused precisely (not merely worked around) by moving the sticky declaration one level up, from `.main-nav` to `<header>` itself: `<header>`'s own containing block is `.app-shell`, which spans the full page height, giving it the room a sticky element actually needs. `App.tsx`'s `<header>` now carries the `positionMode`-derived `app-header--sticky` class described above; `MainNavigation`/`<nav>` reverted to a plain, unconditional `main-nav` class with no `positionMode` prop at all.
