An earlier, incorrect diagnosis briefly attributed the automated test failures this bug produced to a headless-Chromium/root-scroller rendering limitation rather than this real containing-block defect — that diagnosis was wrong and has been removed from both this file and `e2e/stickyNavigation.spec.ts`; genuine bounding-box scroll geometry against the real app renders correctly here once the sticky declaration sits on the correct element.
    - `RidingScreen` gained a new optional `onRidingActiveChange?: (active: boolean) => void` prop, synced by a small effect keyed on `[nav.geolocationStatus, onRidingActiveChange]` that calls it on every genuine `idle ⇄ watching ⇄ error` transition, and again via its own cleanup on unmount — the latter is what resets `App`'s copy to `false` the instant the rider navigates away from Riding entirely, since this screen always fully unmounts on every screen switch (no `key` anywhere in `App.tsx`'s conditional rendering), needing no separate reset logic. This is the first instance in this codebase of a screen reporting state back up to `App` via a callback prop — mirrors the existing `onOpenRoute`/`onRouteSaved`/`onNavigateToSettings` convention rather than introducing React context, which remains unused anywhere in `src/`. `App.tsx` wires it with a bare `onRidingActiveChange={setIsRidingActive}`, mirroring its own existing `onNavigate={setScreen}` convention of passing a raw `useState` setter directly.
    - `.app-header--sticky` (`src/index.css`, directly after the existing `header` padding rule): `position: sticky`, `top: env(safe-area-inset-top, 0px)` (the exact same safe-area idiom `.app-shell`'s own top padding already establishes, so the resting and stuck positions agree exactly and the transition is continuous by construction — no jump); `z-index: 10` (comfortably above every other z-index in this file, none of which exceeds 5, and none of which ever overlaps the header's own screen region under normal use); an opaque `background: var(--colour-bg)` (`header` had no background before, needed so scrolling content doesn't show through once pinned); and `border-bottom: 1px solid var(--colour-border)` (the same token `.main-nav`'s own `border-top` already uses) to separate the pinned bar from content beneath it. No `@supports` gate needed for `position: sticky` itself, unlike this file's `dvh`/`round()` fallbacks elsewhere. `header`'s own top padding/wake-lock-popover dead zone, `.main-nav`, `.main-nav-button`, and `.main-nav-button[aria-current="page"]` are all unchanged.
    - Automated e2e coverage (`e2e/stickyNavigation.spec.ts`) asserts genuine bounding-box geometry against the real, running app for Routes, Diagnostics and the pre-ride/Resume screen (scrolled `<header>` stays within ~2px of its unscrolled position), plus real geometry proving active Riding's `<header>` scrolls fully out of view and returns once scrolled back to the top, and computed-style checks that every other top-level screen renders `<header>` sticky and that navigating away from an active ride restores it. `App.test.tsx`/`MainNavigation.test.tsx`/`navPositionMode.test.ts`/`RidingScreen.test.tsx` cover the state-derivation and callback contract at the unit level; `App.test.tsx` asserts the sticky class on the `<header>` ancestor of the rendered `<nav>`, since `MainNavigation` itself no longer carries any positioning class.
    - Files changed: `src/ui/shared/MainNavigation.tsx`, `src/ui/shared/navPositionMode.ts`, `src/ui/riding/RidingScreen.tsx`, `src/App.tsx`, `src/index.css`; tests: `src/ui/shared/MainNavigation.test.tsx`, `src/ui/shared/navPositionMode.test.ts`, `src/ui/riding/RidingScreen.test.tsx`, `src/App.test.tsx`, `e2e/stickyNavigation.spec.ts`.
    - Real-device iPhone verification of the corrected, `<header>`-based fix has confirmed that navigation remains sticky in Planning while scrolling. Routes, Diagnostics, Settings, the pre-ride briefing, paused/Resume Riding, and the active-Riding scroll-away/return transition have not yet been separately confirmed on a real device — do not treat any of those as manually verified until each has actually been checked. Android Chrome verification is outstanding entirely.

### Navigation and library interface

28. **Optional adaptive compact navigation while scrolling**
    - A possible later visual refinement to the always-sticky primary navigation delivered in item 24 above. The user is currently unsure whether they want this at all — it is a candidate direction only, not approved or scheduled work, and must not be started without explicit confirmation.
    - Motivation: the full sticky icon-and-label row occupies noticeable vertical space on long screens such as Planning.
    - Candidate behaviour, all subject to later confirmation and none of it settled: at the top of a sticky screen, show the current full icons-and-labels navigation; after a meaningful downward scroll, collapse it into a shorter icon-only sticky row; after a meaningful upward scroll, expand it again before the user reaches the top; at the top of the page, always use the expanded presentation; retain accessible names for every destination while text labels are visually hidden; preserve the current non-sticky behaviour during active Riding rather than adding any compact/sticky scroll handling there.
    - Any implementation would need scroll-direction state plus hysteresis/thresholds so small touch movements and scroll bounce do not cause rapid toggling; would need to expand when keyboard focus enters the navigation; would need to respect `prefers-reduced-motion` if a size transition is used; and must not create a second navigation element, change current-page semantics, or reduce effective touch targets below the existing minimum.
    - Decisions that remain open and must be settled with the user before any implementation prompt is written, not invented here: whether to build this at all; which sticky screens should use it, or whether all sticky screens should behave consistently; the downward-collapse and upward-expand thresholds; whether any upward movement expands it immediately or a separate upward hysteresis distance is required; the compact bar's exact height, icon size, padding and visual separation from scrolling content; whether expansion should also occur on navigation focus, destination change or orientation change; whether the compact-to-expanded transition should animate, and its reduced-motion behaviour; and real iOS Safari and Android Chrome acceptance criteria.
    - Keep this item outside the immediate ordered implementation sequence. It must not displace Android compatibility acceptance (item 25), editable GPX conversion (item 26), route reversal (item 27), weather (item 11), offline-map feasibility (item 12), the optional desktop Planning layout (item 16), or the still-pending bicycle field acceptance items recorded under "Manual acceptance status" above. It must not be merged into active Riding's navigation work — active Riding's non-sticky contract (item 24 above) is settled and unaffected by this idea. Implementing this item does not reopen or mark incomplete the already-delivered item 24.

### Platform compatibility

25. **Explicit Android/Chrome PWA compatibility — done**
    - A compatibility and acceptance-testing commitment, not a native-app project: ACN remains one PWA codebase. No APK, Android Studio project, Play Store packaging or Android-specific application architecture was introduced.
    - Target: Android 10 or later running the current stable release of Chrome for Android. No hard-coded Chrome version exists anywhere in product behaviour. Other Android browsers are not formally supported at this stage.
    - An audit of the manifest/service-worker configuration, GPX import/export, and geolocation/Wake Lock/persistence/map-fallback code (`vite.config.ts`, `vite.pwa.workbox.ts`, `src/platform/wakeLock.ts`, `src/ui/riding/useRideNavigation.ts`, `src/ui/riding/useScreenWakeLock.ts`, `src/map/MapView.tsx`, `src/storage/`) found **no production-code defect**: `grep -rniE "useragent|isAndroid|navigator\.platform" src/` returns nothing, confirming capability detection is used throughout with no UA-sniffing anywhere; diffing the built `dist/index.html`/`dist/manifest.webmanifest` against source confirmed Vite's `base` rewriting already correctly prefixes every asset reference, the manifest's `start_url`/`scope`/`display: standalone` are already correct, and `vite.pwa.workbox.ts` already precaches only the app shell with no `runtimeCaching` (independently proven by the pre-existing `src/routing/serviceWorkerExclusion.test.ts`). This slice is therefore test-coverage-and-documentation only — no application logic changed.
    - A new second Playwright project, `android-chrome` (`playwright.config.ts`), uses `devices["Pixel 7"]` — a current, mainstream Android reference device (Android 14, satisfying the "10+" target) — split from the existing `chromium` project via `testMatch`/`testIgnore` on an `android*.spec.ts` filename convention, so each spec runs exactly once, never duplicated across both projects. This is still Chromium underneath (mobile viewport/UA/touch/device-scale-factor emulation only), never real Android Chrome or WebView — `docs/android-chrome-acceptance.md` states this plainly and tags every checklist item as automatically verified, real-device-only, or already-verified-on-iPhone-needs-Android-recheck, per this file's own "never mark real-device-only behaviour as verified" discipline.
    - New coverage: `e2e/pwaManifestAndScope.spec.ts` (manifest/base-path/service-worker-scope against the real built/served artefacts, run under the existing desktop `chromium` project since it doesn't depend on device emulation); `e2e/androidMobileLayout.spec.ts` (no horizontal overflow, ≥44px touch targets, the sticky-header-except-active-riding contract); `e2e/androidGpxImportExport.spec.ts` (real file-input import, a real download export with exact-filename and ACN-manoeuvre-content assertions, then an entirely offline re-import); `e2e/androidPlanning.spec.ts` (mocked-provider calculate/save/reopen on the default Road bike profile only — General cycling is already proven by `e2e/planning.spec.ts` and PlanningScreen's own unit/component tests, and is not duplicated here); `e2e/androidRiding.spec.ts` (mocked-fix Riding, camera controls, the non-sticky-during-riding contract, and three lighter-touch Wake Lock checks — visible-and-requests-when-supported, absent-when-unsupported, a rejecting request surfaces the existing retry state without crashing Riding — since the full Wake Lock state machine is already proven at the hook level and in `e2e/ridingWakeLock.spec.ts`); `e2e/androidPersistenceAndOffline.spec.ts` (three scenarios: a genuine `page.reload()`-then-reopen recovery flow, tile/style-blocked fallback rendering combined with Riding for the first time, and a real `context.setOffline(true)` proving the app's existing "Offline —" banner and continued route/progress/elevation usability); and `e2e/androidOfflineAppShell.spec.ts`, the one spec in the whole suite that deliberately does not block service workers, proving the real installed service worker serves the app shell after a reload with the network fully down. This last one was the slice's one identified flake risk (real service-worker activation/control timing); it passed 8/8 in the full suite run plus a further 8/8 under `--repeat-each=8`, so it shipped rather than being dropped to the real-device-only checklist.
    - A genuine, previously-undocumented architectural finding surfaced while writing the reload-recovery test, confirmed by reading `src/App.tsx` directly: `screen`/`selectedRoute` are plain, unpersisted `useState`, with no mount-time read of the stored active-ride row anywhere in `App.tsx`. A real page reload during a ride therefore returns the rider to the Routes screen, not back into Riding automatically — recovery happens only once the rider reopens the _same_ route (matched by `routeId` in `useRideNavigation.ts`), at which point the persisted fix/progress restore and the pre-ride panel offers "Resume riding" rather than "Start riding". `e2e/androidPersistenceAndOffline.spec.ts`'s first test asserts this precisely, through an actual reload — no test at any level (hook, component or e2e) previously did this.
    - Version bumped to 0.3.16 (`package.json`, and `package-lock.json`'s two matching `version` fields, hand-patched rather than regenerated). Full verification (format, lint, typecheck, the complete unit/component suite, production build, and the complete e2e suite across both Playwright projects — 84 tests, zero failures) passed; a single unrelated `RouteLibrary.test.tsx` focus-timing flake was observed under full-suite parallel load and confirmed pre-existing (44/44 pass in isolation; that file was untouched by this slice).
    - Real-device Android verification has not happened in this slice at all — every check above is Chromium-emulated. See `docs/android-chrome-acceptance.md` for the full, tagged checklist and an ordered manual test procedure for the user's next Android-phone session.
    - A follow-up fixes a genuinely flaky instance of this file's own reload-recovery test (the first test above), found via a real CI failure, not a hypothetical: the test gated its `page.reload()` on a fixed `page.waitForTimeout(300)`, mirroring the same fixed-sleep idiom used at 4 other reload-precondition sites across `e2e/ridingNextManoeuvre.spec.ts`/`e2e/ridingFinishAndEnd.spec.ts` (left unchanged by this fix — a known, still-open instance of the identical pattern, out of this fix's own scope). Root cause, confirmed by direct reading of `useRideNavigation.ts`'s persistence effect: the `rideState` write (`setActiveRideState` → a plain, un-throttled `db.rideState.put(...)`) fires once per accepted GPS fix with no debounce or timer, so its actual IndexedDB commit time depends on the browser's transaction queue and machine load — a fixed delay can never be provably sufficient. Directly reproduced locally, not just inferred: under calibrated CPU contention (`taskset -c 0-3`, 4-6 concurrent workers), the unmodified test failed with the exact reported symptom — `getByRole('button', { name: 'Resume riding' })` resolving to `undefined` after reload — confirming the row genuinely hadn't committed in time, not a Playwright auto-retry gap (CDP `Emulation.setCPUThrottlingRate` was also tried and rejected for this diagnosis: it throttles only the renderer's own JS execution, not the browser's storage-service write path, so it failed to reproduce the delay at all).
    - Fixed by replacing the sleep with two small local helpers, `readSavedRouteId`/`readActiveRideStateRow` — the repo's first e2e use of raw `indexedDB.open(...)` from inside `page.evaluate`, kept in the spec file itself rather than `e2e/support/`, per this file's own established no-shared-e2e-helpers-across-specs convention (see item 23's `zoomToCloseRange`). The test now waits for the on-route/off-route status text (proves a fix was accepted and classified) and then `expect.poll`s the real committed `rideState` row — `routeId` matching the just-saved route (looked up from the `routes` store), a non-null `lastFix`, numeric matched-progress fields, and `completionArmed: false` (provably correct here, since the injected fix never leaves the route's own start coordinate and so can never satisfy `rideCompletion.ts`'s arming departure-radius condition) — before reloading. Re-run under the same calibrated contention that reproduced the original failure (60+ repeats total across light, moderate-contention and `CI=1` fresh-server-boot conditions): zero occurrences of the original failure signature.
    - No production code changed — the persistence and restoration paths were already correct and already unit-tested (`useRideNavigation.test.ts`'s own `finish()` suite already flushes a microtask before asserting against `getActiveRideState()`); the gap was purely in this one e2e test's own precondition. No version bump, matching item 21's own precedent for a test-only hardening fix.
    - A second follow-up hardens the 4 remaining `page.waitForTimeout(300)` reload-precondition sites the paragraph above explicitly left open (2 in `e2e/ridingNextManoeuvre.spec.ts`, 2 in `e2e/ridingFinishAndEnd.spec.ts`) — all four are now removed. The two `ridingNextManoeuvre.spec.ts` sites (each already preceded by an existing UI assertion proving the intended fix was accepted — `"50 m"` visible, or `"Arrive at your destination"` visible) poll the committed `rideState` row's own `lastReliableMatchedDistanceFromStartMetres` — the exact frozen/presentation field `nextManoeuvre.ts`'s `selectNextManoeuvre` reads, confirmed by direct reading of its signature, never the live `matchedDistanceFromStartMetres` — reaching a threshold with a wide (300m+) margin against the fixture's own earlier fixes, before reloading; `matchedDistanceFromStartMetres` was deliberately left unchecked here (unlike the paragraph above's own precedent), since `rideNavigationCore.ts` shows the reliable and raw fields only ever diverge on an `"off-route"` classification, which these dead-straight, always-on-route fixtures never produce — checking the live field would add no real coverage and isn't the field that's actually load-bearing for the manoeuvre panel's restored content. The two `ridingFinishAndEnd.spec.ts` sites instead poll for the row's outright absence: `finish()` (`useRideNavigation.ts`, the sole caller of `clearActiveRideState()`, shared by both End ride and Finish ride) awaits the storage delete first, before any in-memory reset, making absence a race-free, strictly-stronger signal than the clean-pre-ride-UI assertions already present just above each site. The earlier "kept in the spec file itself rather than `e2e/support/`... per this file's own established no-shared-e2e-helpers-across-specs convention" decision two paragraphs above, for these same two reader functions, is now **explicitly superseded**: with two more spec files needing the identical raw-database-read mechanism (a third and fourth consumer beyond the original one), `readSavedRouteId`/`readActiveRideStateRow`/`toIndexedDbError`/`INDEXED_DB_NAME` were extracted into a new `e2e/support/rideStateDb.ts`, mirroring `localMapStyle.ts`'s own precedent for a genuinely shared, `Page`-coupled e2e support module. The extracted module stays deliberately narrow — IndexedDB reads only, no assertions, no polling policy, no scenario-specific expectations — so each spec still states and polls its own exact postcondition locally via a small per-file helper (`waitForCommittedRideProgress` in `ridingNextManoeuvre.spec.ts`; `waitForClearedRideState` in `ridingFinishAndEnd.spec.ts`), never a shared assertion. `androidPersistenceAndOffline.spec.ts` now imports the same two functions rather than defining them, with its own test bodies and `expect.poll(...).toMatchObject({...})` assertion byte-identical to before. No production persistence, restoration, ride-completion, End/Finish-ride, route-matching, manoeuvre-selection or PWA/service-worker behaviour changed; direct re-reading of `useRideNavigation.ts`/`rideStateRepository.ts`/`mapping.ts` for this slice found no defect making any of the four postconditions unreachable. No retries, enlarged timeouts or weakened assertions were introduced; no version bump, matching this same entry's own established precedent for test-only hardening.

### Editable route copies and GPX round-trip

26. **Edit an existing route as a new Planning draft — done**
    - A route opened in the pre-ride overview (Riding's `.ride-start-panel`, idle state only — never every Routes card) shows an **Edit copy in Planning** button whenever `domain/editableWaypoints.ts`'s `canDeriveEditableWaypoints` finds at least two points to work from; otherwise the button is disabled with an inline explanation. Pressing it never mutates the source route: it seeds the app's single Planning draft row with independent waypoint objects (fresh `createWaypointId()`s), the source route's own name as the suggested route name, and either the source route's recovered routing profile/avoid-ferries or (when nothing is recoverable) `DEFAULT_ROUTING_PROFILE` plus the current Settings avoid-ferries default — then hands off navigation to `App.tsx` via a new `onNavigateToPlanning` prop, mirroring the existing `onNavigateToSettings` convention. If the singleton draft already holds a meaningful (non-empty) unrelated plan, the shared `ConfirmDialog` (see below) interrupts first; Cancel leaves that draft untouched. Opening the copy issues zero routing requests — Planning's own existing "no auto-calculate, only `calculateNow()`" behaviour already guarantees this, since a freshly seeded draft never has a `"routed"` result.
    - Waypoint recovery follows a two-tier priority, implemented as a single new domain type, `PlanningProvenance` (`domain/types.ts`, mirroring `ManoeuvreProvenance`'s exact two-kind shape): `{kind: "planning-session", waypoints, profile, avoidFerries}` (stamped by `PlanningScreen.tsx` on **every** successful `handleSave`/`handleExport`, from live `state.present`/`profile`/`avoidFerries` — not scoped to edit-copies only, since any future Planning save should retain its own authored waypoints) or `{kind: "acn-gpx-extension", version: 1, ...}` (recovered from a validated GPX reimport). Both resolve to `origin: "exact"` in `resolveEditableWaypoints`; when neither is present, `navigation/deriveWaypointsFromRoute.ts` derives waypoints from the route's own geometry instead (`origin: "derived"`). PlanningScreen shows a small, persistent informational notice matching the origin ("Editable copy created from the route's original planning waypoints..." vs "Editable waypoints were estimated from this route. Recalculation may follow different roads..."), threaded through the existing draft-hydration effect and — critically — through _every_ run of the debounced autosave effect (not just hydration), since `saveDraft` fully replaces the stored row and would otherwise silently drop the notice's backing fields on the first post-open edit.
    - `deriveWaypointsFromRoute` is a pure, dependency-free, hand-rolled adaptive Ramer-Douglas-Peucker simplification (local equirectangular metric projection, not raw lat/lon degrees) over the route's `RoutePoint[]`: an epsilon-doubling search (starting at 0m) guarantees convergence to at most `MAX_DERIVED_WAYPOINTS = 20` waypoints (RDP's own point-count monotonicity in epsilon makes a separate truncation step both unnecessary and unsafe — it could otherwise drop the finish waypoint), followed by gap-filling any remaining capacity with real route points nearest the midpoint of oversized (>8km, the documented midpoint of the spec's "5–10km" guidance) gaps, then near-duplicate removal (<20m, never touching the first or last waypoint). A detected closed loop (start/finish within 50m) has its final waypoint forced to _exactly_ the first waypoint's coordinate, mirroring Planning's own "return to start" action. Always preserves route order and (subject to the above) the original endpoints; never shares coordinate tuple references with the source route.
    - The GPX side extends the existing `<acn:navigation>`/`<acn:source>` namespace with a new, geometry-digest-bound `<acn:planning version="1" profile="..." avoidFerries="..." waypointCount="N" geometrySha256="...">` element (`acnNamespace.ts`'s `ACN_PLANNING_EXTENSION_VERSION`), containing one `<acn:waypoint lon="" lat=""/>` per waypoint — written by `exportGpx.ts` whenever `route.planningProvenance` is present (independent of, but sharing one already-computed geometry digest with, the existing `<acn:navigation>` block when both apply), and read by a new `parseAcnExtension.ts` function, `readAcnPlanningExtension`, using the _same_ strict, all-or-nothing, digest-verified rejection tier as `readAcnNavigationExtension` (not the weaker best-effort tier `readAcnSourceProfile` uses) — version/profile/avoidFerries/waypoint-count/coordinate-range/digest all validated, any single failure discards the whole envelope. A rejected (but present) envelope surfaces a new non-blocking `GpxImportNotice` (`"acn-planning-extension-rejected"`) and the import still succeeds, falling back to geometry-derived waypoints. `MAX_ACN_PLANNING_WAYPOINTS = 300` bounds a validated import — independent of, and much larger than, the unrelated 20-waypoint derivation cap, which only bounds the geometry-fallback path.
    - `StoredPlanningDraft`/`PlanningDraftContent` gained two new optional fields, `editCopySourceRouteId`/`editCopyWaypointsOrigin` (`storage/db.ts`/`storage/mapping.ts`), following the exact `profile`/`avoidFerries`/`routeName` precedent — plain, non-indexed fields needing no Dexie version bump (schema stays at v4), guarded on read via a real `"exact" | "derived"` membership check rather than a bare `??`, since a corrupt value should suppress the notice, not show a wrong one.
    - The shared `src/ui/shared/ConfirmDialog.tsx` (previously bare, with no focus/keyboard handling at all — its only prior caller was Settings' key-deletion confirmation) gained `autoFocus` on Cancel and an Escape-anywhere-inside-cancels `onKeyDown`, mirroring `RouteListItem.tsx`'s own already-proven inline delete-confirmation pattern exactly — purely additive, so Settings' existing call site needed no changes and gained the same accessibility improvement for free. `RidingScreen.tsx` is the dialog's second caller, owning its own trigger-button ref for focus-restore on Cancel (the dialog itself has no notion of what opened it).
    - Saving the edited copy always creates a new route: `usePlanningRoute.ts` already mints a fresh `createRouteId()` on every calculation, so Save/Export's existing copy semantics needed no change at all — proven by e2e coverage that the source route remains present, unchanged, and independently reopenable after saving an edited copy under a different name.
    - Automated coverage: `deriveWaypointsFromRoute.test.ts` (straight/bends/dense-thousands/loop/very-short/near-duplicate/deterministic/order-preserving/20-cap fixtures), `editableWaypoints.test.ts`, GPX export/import/round-trip tests (including a tampered-digest-falls-back-to-derivation case), storage mapping/migration tests, `PlanningScreen.test.tsx` (notice text per origin, the notice surviving an unrelated waypoint edit — the autosave-threading fix above — and provenance stamping on both Save and Export), `ConfirmDialog.test.tsx` (autoFocus/Escape), `RidingScreen.test.tsx` (button state, both no-existing-draft and meaningful-draft-confirmation paths, storage-failure handling), and a new `e2e/editRouteAsPlanningCopy.spec.ts` covering exact-provenance recovery with zero routing requests until Calculate, a capped derived-waypoint arbitrary GPX, the meaningful-draft confirmation (Cancel/Confirm), a full export→offline-reimport→edit-copy-again round trip proving exact (not re-derived) recovery, and a tampered-digest-falls-back-to-derivation scenario. Real-device iPhone verification has not happened — do not treat this item as manually verified until that check has actually been done.
    - This item's `PlanningProvenance`/`resolveEditableWaypoints`/`deriveWaypointsFromRoute` foundation was built so item 27 below could reuse the same editable-waypoint representation, as originally scoped; item 27 is now also done.

27. **Reverse a route by rerouting reversed waypoints — done**
    - A **Reverse route** button sits directly beside **Edit copy in Planning** in the pre-ride overview (`.ride-start-panel`, idle state only), sharing its exact eligibility rule (`canDeriveEditableWaypoints`) and disabled-hint text verbatim — the two buttons are always simultaneously enabled or disabled, so a single shared `field-hint` paragraph covers both rather than duplicating identical text. Pressing it never mutates the source route: it resolves editable waypoints via the unchanged v0.3.17 `resolveEditableWaypoints` (exact `planningProvenance` first, `deriveWaypointsFromRoute` fallback otherwise), reverses their order, and seeds the singleton Planning draft with a suggested `<source name> (reversed)` name. Opening the reverse copy issues zero OpenRouteService requests — the draft has no `"routed"` result until the rider explicitly presses Calculate route.
    - **Shared architecture**: `RidingScreen.tsx`'s previous single-purpose `performEditCopy`/`handleEditCopyClick`/`handleEditCopyCancel`/`handleEditCopyConfirm` became one parameterised `performCopyOperation`/`handleCopyOperationClick`/`handleCopyOperationCancel`, driven by a small `CopyOperationConfig` object (button/dialog copy, error messages, log tags, a `transformWaypoints` function and a `suggestedName` function) with two module-level instances, `EDIT_COPY_CONFIG` (identity transform, unchanged copy) and `REVERSE_ROUTE_CONFIG` (`reverseEditableWaypoints`, `suggestReversedRouteName`). Both actions read and write the _same_ singleton `planningDrafts` row, so they deliberately share one guard pair (`pendingConfirmKind`/`activeOperationKind`/`isActionPendingRef`) rather than independent per-action state: `handleCopyOperationClick`'s existing `pendingConfirmKind !== null || isActionPendingRef.current` guard is what serialises each action against itself (a rapid double click) _and_ against the other action (clicking Reverse route while Edit copy's own confirmation is open is a no-op) with no extra coupling code, since both conditions collapse to the identical check. `ConfirmDialog` is reused unchanged with reverse-specific title/message/confirm-label text; Cancel and a persistence failure both restore focus to whichever button (`editCopyButtonRef`/`reverseButtonRef`) triggered the pending operation, mirroring the v0.3.17 behaviour exactly.
    - **Reversal semantics**: `domain/editableWaypoints.ts`'s new `reverseEditableWaypoints(waypoints)` is `waypoints.map(clone).reverse()` — a plain positional reversal with no loop-aware special-casing, which is already exactly correct for a closed loop: `deriveWaypointsFromRoute`'s own loop-closure step produces a _value_-equal (not reference-equal) final waypoint when it detects one, so for `[A, B, C, A]` a positional reversal (swap index 0↔3, 1↔2) yields `[A, C, B, A]` exactly, understood the only way any code in this app ever compares a coordinate — by value, never by object identity. Every output tuple is freshly constructed via `.map`, so the input array and its coordinate tuples are never mutated or shared. `domain/routeNaming.ts` (new file, the first naming helper anywhere in this codebase) holds `suggestReversedRouteName(sourceName) => \`${sourceName} (reversed)\``: preserves the complete source name unmutated, no trimming or language-aware suffix logic (Planning's own existing `routeName.trim() || "Planned route"` already covers whitespace at Save/Export time), and no dedupe against existing Route Library names, since duplicate names are already an accepted, pre-existing condition.
    - **Draft metadata compatibility**: one new additive, non-indexed field, `editCopyOperation?: string` on `StoredPlanningDraft` (`storage/db.ts`) and `EditCopyOperation = "forward" | "reverse"` on `PlanningDraftContent` (`storage/mapping.ts`, guarded by a real `isEditCopyOperation` membership check, mirroring `isEditCopyWaypointsOrigin` exactly) — no Dexie version bump needed, following the identical precedent the two v0.3.17 fields already established. Unlike `editCopyWaypointsOrigin` (which stays `undefined` when absent, since that `undefined` state is what lets the unchanged two-field hydration gate suppress the notice for an ordinary draft), `fromStoredPlanningDraft` resolves `editCopyOperation` to a **concrete default of `"forward"`** whenever absent or corrupt: this field is only ever consulted once the two-field gate has already passed, at which point "forward" is the objectively correct reading of "no marker recorded" for any pre-Reverse-route draft — so a v0.3.17-written draft (which has `editCopySourceRouteId`/`editCopyWaypointsOrigin` but no `editCopyOperation` at all) continues to hydrate as an ordinary forward edit copy, showing the unchanged forward notice, with no special-casing in `PlanningScreen.tsx`. `PlanningScreen`'s `editCopyMeta` state gained one field, `operation: EditCopyOperation`, threaded through the existing hydration effect (gate condition unchanged) and the existing debounced-autosave effect's conditional spread (a third line alongside the two v0.3.17 fields, required for the same "saveDraft fully replaces the row" reason). A new pure `describeEditCopyNotice(meta)` replaces the previous inline ternary with the full 2×2 (operation × origin) matrix: the two forward strings are byte-identical to v0.3.17; the two new reverse strings read "Reversed editable copy created. Recalculate before saving; one-way restrictions may make the new route differ from the original. The saved route remains unchanged." (exact) and "Reversed waypoints were estimated from this route. Recalculation may follow different roads, especially around one-way restrictions. The saved route remains unchanged." (derived).
    - **Zero changes needed, confirmed by direct reading rather than assumed**: the GPX `<acn:planning>` export/import layer (`exportGpx.ts`/`parseAcnExtension.ts`/`importGpx.ts`) never branches on how `planningProvenance.waypoints` were produced, so a reversed-then-saved route gets a correct, fresh `planningProvenance` for free via the unchanged `buildPlanningProvenance` (which always stamps from live editor state at Save/Export time); `usePlanningRoute.ts` already mints a fresh `createRouteId()` on every calculation, so Save always creates a new route regardless of whether the draft originated as a reverse copy; `canSaveOrExportPlan`'s existing `state.kind === "routed"` gate already keeps Save/Export unavailable for any freshly-seeded, uncalculated draft. None of `resolveEditableWaypoints`, `canDeriveEditableWaypoints`, `deriveWaypointsFromRoute` or `PlanningProvenance` itself changed at all.
    - CLAUDE.md backlog item 30 (the pre-existing Planning autosave race) was explicitly checked, not fixed: `editCopyOperation` is threaded through the autosave effect using the exact same conditional-spread pattern as the two v0.3.17 fields, so it inherits the already-documented race identically without making it worse or different in kind; no evidence was found that this slice's metadata cannot persist correctly without touching item 30, so it was left alone per this file's own change-discipline.
    - Automated coverage: `editableWaypoints.test.ts` (two-point/multi-point/closed-loop reversal, no input mutation, no tuple reference-sharing, permutation-only length preservation including an already-20-capped list), `routeNaming.test.ts` (suffix behaviour, unmutated source name, determinism), `mapping.test.ts`/`planningDraftRepository.test.ts` (round-trip, v0.3.17-legacy-row defaults to `"forward"`, corrupt-value defaults to `"forward"`, an ordinary draft's resolved default never leaks a false notice), `RidingScreen.test.tsx` (a full "Reverse route" suite mirroring every "Edit copy in Planning" case plus a new cross-action guard suite proving the two actions' shared in-flight state), `PlanningScreen.test.tsx` (reverse+exact/derived notices, legacy-draft compatibility, autosave survival, no-notice-for-ordinary-draft), and a new `e2e/reverseRoute.spec.ts` covering: zero requests until Calculate, exact-provenance reversal with reversed leg coordinates sent to the mocked provider, Save creating an independent second route with the source route left unchanged and reopenable, export→offline-reimport→Edit-copy-in-Planning recovering the reversed waypoints exactly, a capped derived-waypoint arbitrary GPX reversal, a closed-loop reversal (proved both at the UI level — the combined start/finish marker persists — and at the routing-request level, where the three reversed legs are shown to be the three original legs in reverse order with each leg's own endpoints swapped), the meaningful-draft confirmation (Cancel/Confirm, focus restored to Reverse route specifically), and reload recovery with no automatic routing request. Real-device iPhone verification, and manual verification once the currently-observed live OpenRouteService outage clears, have not happened — do not treat this item as manually verified until each has actually been done.
    - Version bumped to 0.3.18 (`package.json` and `package-lock.json`'s two matching `version` fields, hand-patched rather than regenerated, following the 0.3.16→0.3.17 precedent). Files changed: `src/domain/editableWaypoints.ts`, `src/domain/routeNaming.ts` (new), `src/storage/db.ts`, `src/storage/mapping.ts`, `src/ui/riding/RidingScreen.tsx`, `src/ui/planning/PlanningScreen.tsx`; tests: `src/domain/editableWaypoints.test.ts`, `src/domain/routeNaming.test.ts` (new), `src/storage/mapping.test.ts`, `src/storage/planningDraftRepository.test.ts`, `src/ui/riding/RidingScreen.test.tsx`, `src/ui/planning/PlanningScreen.test.tsx`, `e2e/reverseRoute.spec.ts` (new).

### Ride lifecycle

29. **A finished ride's persisted state is never cleared — done**
    - Found during the Android/Chrome compatibility audit (item 25 above), not fixed there: `src/storage/rideStateRepository.ts`'s `clearActiveRideState()` is implemented and unit-tested but never called from any application code — no "finished ride" flow exists anywhere in the UI, so a completed or abandoned ride's persisted `rideState` row lingers in IndexedDB indefinitely rather than being cleared. This is a general product question (what should "finishing a ride" mean — an explicit action, reaching the route's end, a time-based expiry?), not an Android-compatibility defect, and was deliberately left unimplemented in that slice per its own non-goal of unrelated backlog work.
    - Before implementing, decide the intended trigger and UX for clearing ride state, and confirm it should not interfere with the existing stale-fix/"Resume riding" restoration contract for a ride that was merely paused or interrupted, not genuinely finished.
    - This slice answers the questions the entry above left open with two entry points converging on one authoritative path: a manual, always-available **End ride** action, and a conservatively-detected, rider-confirmed **Finish ride** action. Neither records a ride — ACN still has no ride-history feature. `useRideNavigation.ts` gained a new exported `finish(): Promise<void>` — the sole caller of `clearActiveRideState()` anywhere in the app (previously zero call sites, per the entry above). It calls `clearActiveRideState()` first, before touching anything else in memory; only on success does it reuse the existing `stop()` (bumps `watchGenerationRef`, disposes the watch, `setStatus("idle")`) and reset `startedAtRef`/`currentFix`/`coreState`/`isStale`/`geolocationError`/`elevationViewMode`/`wakeLockDesired`/`dismissedClimbFeatureId`/`restoredCameraState` to fresh-ride defaults. `currentFix` returning to `null` is what makes `RidingScreen.tsx`'s pre-existing "Resume riding"-vs-"Start riding" label (already keyed off `currentFix`, not a new flag) correct for free. No IndexedDB schema change was needed: deleting the singleton `active` row outright is sufficient, confirmed by direct inspection of `storage/mapping.ts`/`storage/db.ts` — nothing about a finished ride needs remembering once its progress is exactly what must not persist.
    - **Storage-write ordering and obsolete-callback protection.** A new `isFinalizingRef` closes the one genuine race this entry's own text warned about: a persistence write racing `clearActiveRideState()` and completing after it, recreating the just-cleared row. `finish()` sets this ref synchronously, in the same synchronous block as its own call to `clearActiveRideState()` (before any `await`); the existing per-fix persistence effect (previously unconditional) now checks it as its literal first line and skips writing while a finalisation is in flight — closing the window where a genuine GPS fix arriving while the delete's own IndexedDB transaction is still pending would otherwise `put()` the row again after the `delete()` transaction, recreating it (same-object-store Dexie transactions commit in creation order, per the mechanism item 31 above already characterised and proved against this repository's real Dexie/fake-indexeddb stack — a new `useRideNavigation.test.ts` case proves this exact race directly, with no mock needed: `finish()`'s own real `await` already creates the window a synchronous test can drive a late fix into deterministically). The ref is deliberately **not** checked inside `handleFix`/`handleError`: a genuine fix arriving mid-finalisation still updates in-memory ride state normally — harmless on success (overwritten by `finish()`'s own reset) and exactly the correct live state to show on failure, since a real GPS fix doesn't become invalid merely because a background storage clear is in flight; only the persistence _write_ itself is suppressed. On failure the ref is cleared and the original error re-thrown untouched, leaving every other piece of state exactly as it was — the UI never claims success before the persisted state is actually proven cleared, and no rollback logic is needed anywhere since nothing else was ever touched. `finish()` also guards its own re-entrancy at the hook level (a silent no-op on a redundant call), a belt-and-braces layer beneath `RidingScreen.tsx`'s own primary `isFinalizeActionPendingRef` guard, mirroring the existing `isActionPendingRef` idiom `performCopyOperation` already established. A late callback from an already-disposed watch is rejected by the pre-existing `watchGenerationRef` discipline unchanged.
    - **Wake lock and camera** needed no new release logic and one small addition respectively. `RidingWakeLockControl`/`useScreenWakeLock` are already mounted only while `nav.geolocationStatus !== "idle"`, so `finish()` flipping status to `"idle"` unmounts that subtree and its own existing unmount-cleanup effect releases the sentinel, exactly per `useScreenWakeLock.ts`'s own doc comment — zero new wake-lock code. The camera needed one addition: `useRideCamera.ts` already had exactly the right reset transition (`rideCameraReducer`'s `"route-opened"` case, previously only ever dispatched automatically on a genuine route-id change) — a new `resetCamera(): void` on `UseRideCameraResult` just dispatches that same event, reusing the existing reducer branch rather than adding a second reset path; `RidingScreen.tsx`'s finalisation callback calls it explicitly, since ending a ride never changes `routeId` and so would never trigger the existing automatic route-change reset on its own. One accepted, documented, unfixed residual: `showPausedToast` (a separate `useState`, not part of the reducer's own `HookState`) isn't touched by `"route-opened"`, so a "Map follow paused" toast already showing at the exact instant of finalize lingers until its own pre-existing 3 s timeout — negligible, self-heals, and the message isn't wrong, just momentarily stale after a screen transition that already shows an entirely different panel.
    - **Route-completion detection** (Finish ride's trigger) is a new, deliberately conservative pure module, `src/navigation/rideCompletion.ts`. `isRouteCompletionCandidateEligible` requires, together: an actively-tracking ride; a fresh (non-stale) fix; an off-route level below the strong `"off-route"` threshold; a trusted fix accuracy (reusing `offRoute.ts`'s own `MAX_TRUSTED_ACCURACY_METRES`, 100 m — a fix already too poor to trust for route-matching is too poor to trust for completion either); reliable progress — the same frozen/reliable `presentationDistanceFromStartMetres` every other Riding presentation value already uses, per this file's own stated preference, never the live matched distance — within `ROUTE_COMPLETION_REMAINING_DISTANCE_THRESHOLD_METRES` (50 m, matching `offRoute.ts`'s own `OFF_ROUTE_BASE_METRES` order of magnitude) of the route's total distance; and the raw GPS coordinate within an accuracy-inflated radius of the route's own final point (`route.points.at(-1)`) — `ROUTE_COMPLETION_ENDPOINT_BASE_RADIUS_METRES` (25 m, comparable to `offRoute.ts`'s `POSSIBLY_OFF_ROUTE_BASE_METRES`) plus the fix's own accuracy times `ROUTE_COMPLETION_ENDPOINT_ACCURACY_MULTIPLIER` (1.0, mirroring `offRoute.ts`'s identical multiplier), capped at `ROUTE_COMPLETION_ENDPOINT_MAX_ACCURACY_ALLOWANCE_METRES` (40 m) so one very inaccurate fix can never qualify from an unreasonable distance. A closed loop or an out-and-back/self-crossing route needs no special-casing: the remaining-progress gate alone already rejects an early pass near the shared start/finish, or a geographically-close-but-early crossing, since reliable progress there is far from the route total regardless of raw proximity — proved directly against this project's existing `outAndBackRoute.ts`/`selfIntersectingRoute.ts` test fixtures. These are first-pass, reasoned starting values, compared against this codebase's own existing accuracy/off-route/route-matching thresholds (`offRoute.ts`; `projection.ts`'s `WINDOW_RADIUS_METRES`/`MAX_ACCEPTABLE_LATERAL_METRES`, which bound map-matching search rather than completion but confirm the chosen ~65 m worst-case endpoint radius is comfortably tighter than the app's existing matching tolerance), not yet field-verified on a moving bicycle — matching this file's own established humility about exact metre-scale tuning elsewhere (`routeWidthPolicy.ts`, `distanceBadgeLayer.ts`). A narrow `routeCompletionTrackerReducer` state machine (`consecutiveEligibleCount`/`isDismissed`) requires `ROUTE_COMPLETION_CONSECUTIVE_FIXES_REQUIRED` (2, matching `offRoute.ts`'s own `CONSECUTIVE_TO_DEESCALATE`) consecutive eligible fixes before `isRouteCompletionConfirmed` reports true, deliberately non-latching: an ineligible fix resets the count, per this item's own required test list. Completion detection never clears anything automatically — it only ever surfaces `RidingRouteCompletionPanel`; the rider must press Finish ride. `src/ui/riding/useRouteCompletionCandidate.ts` wires this into React using the same "conditional dispatch during render" idiom `RidingScreen.tsx` already established for `reachedManoeuvreIndex` (not a `useEffect`), tagging each evaluated fix by object identity so neither an unrelated re-render nor React 18 StrictMode's double-invoked render can double-count a fix — proved directly by a dedicated `{ wrapper: StrictMode }` test mirroring `useScreenWakeLock.test.ts`'s own existing convention. An explicit `reset()` (called from the finalisation callback, alongside the hook's own automatic route-id-keyed reset) is needed because Finish/End ride never changes `routeId`, so starting the same route fresh again does not inherit a stale dismissal or count.
    - **UI.** `RidingScreen.tsx` gained an `End ride` `.btn-danger` trigger — shown at the bottom of the pre-ride panel's button stack whenever a Resume-riding state exists (`geolocationStatus === "idle" && currentFix !== null`), and, in the same DOM slot `RidingClimbSelector` occupies (mutually exclusive with it, since that panel is idle-only), whenever a ride is genuinely active — never in a clean, never-started pre-ride state. It opens the shared `ConfirmDialog` ("End this ride?" / "Navigation progress for this ride will be cleared. The saved route will remain in your library." / Cancel / End ride) as a single top-level element reachable from either trigger site, not `window.confirm()`. A new `RidingRouteCompletionPanel` ("Route complete" status paragraph / Finish ride primary / Keep riding secondary) renders directly after `RidingNextManoeuvrePanel`, so it never competes with the map or manoeuvre panel — shown only once `useRouteCompletionCandidate` reports confirmed, and never auto-clearing anything itself. Both End ride and Finish ride funnel into one `performFinalizeRide(source)`, guarded by a single `isFinalizeActionPendingRef`/`activeFinalizeSource` pair mirroring `performCopyOperation`'s own existing catch-block shape (`logError`, a source-tagged accessible `.field-error role="alert"` message) — a storage-clear failure therefore always leaves the ride active/resumable with a concrete retry path, never silently discards in-memory state. `ConfirmDialog.tsx` gained a small, additive `confirmDisabled`/`cancelDisabled` prop pair (unused by its two existing callers, so their behaviour is unchanged), used by End ride's dialog to disable both buttons while a finalisation is in flight — mirroring `RouteListItem.tsx`'s own already-documented "both actions disabled while a deletion is in flight, so Cancel can never appear to undo work storage is still carrying out" precedent (item 7 above). A genuine focus-restoration bug was found and fixed during this slice's own test-writing, not merely theorised: calling `.focus()` directly inside `performFinalizeRide`'s `catch` block targets a button that is _still_ disabled in the DOM at that exact point (`activeFinalizeSource` only resets to `null` in the `finally` block immediately after, and React does not commit that until this whole synchronous catch/finally sequence finishes — a disabled control cannot receive focus, so the call silently no-ops and focus falls to `<body>` once the confirmation dialog unmounts). Fixed by moving the failure-path focus-restoration into a `useEffect` keyed on `finalizeError`'s own object identity (fresh only for a genuinely new error), which React guarantees runs only after the DOM has actually committed the button's re-enabled state — proved directly by this slice's own `RidingScreen.finishEndRide.test.tsx`, and confirmed empirically against jsdom's real disabled/focus behaviour before settling on this fix. A second, smaller gap found by reading `RidingScreen.tsx` directly: `reachedManoeuvreIndex` (the monotonic furthest-reached-manoeuvre tracker) was never reset by the existing `handleStart` — correct for Resume riding, but not for a fresh ride starting after a finalisation. `performFinalizeRide`'s success path now resets it to 0 explicitly, alongside `camera.resetCamera()` and the completion tracker's `reset()`. `explicitFeatureSelection`/`selectedGradientSegment` needed no new reset of their own: `handleStart`'s existing `nav.geolocationStatus === "idle"` branch already clears both, and `finish()` always leaves status `"idle"`, so pressing Start again after a finalisation re-triggers that existing clear for free.
    - No routing endpoint/payload/profile/diagnostics, GPX behaviour, route-reversal/editable-copy behaviour, Planning draft hydration/autosave, camera follow/north-up behaviour beyond the one new explicit reset, elevation/climb detection, manoeuvre selection beyond the one `reachedManoeuvreIndex` fix above, map-layer styling, service-worker caching, or deployment configuration changed. Automated coverage: `src/navigation/rideCompletion.test.ts` (all required pure-eligibility and tracker-state cases, including closed-loop/out-and-back/self-intersecting fixtures reused from `src/test/fixtures/`); `src/ui/riding/useRouteCompletionCandidate.test.ts` (dispatch-during-render correctness, including the StrictMode case above); `src/ui/riding/RidingRouteCompletionPanel.test.tsx` (new, isolated); `src/ui/riding/useRideNavigation.test.ts` gained a `finish()` suite (success clears storage and resets every field; failure re-throws, leaves state untouched, and correctly re-arms persistence; a duplicate concurrent call is a no-op; and the late-write race itself, proved against this repository's real Dexie/fake-indexeddb stack); `src/ui/shared/ConfirmDialog.test.tsx` gained `confirmDisabled`/`cancelDisabled` cases; a new `src/ui/riding/RidingScreen.finishEndRide.test.tsx` (deliberately separate from the existing, much larger `RidingScreen.test.tsx` — pure file-size/organisation hygiene, using the exact same real-Dexie-backend/targeted-`vi.spyOn` approach that file already uses) covering all twelve required "Riding UI and finalisation" scenarios; and a new `e2e/ridingFinishAndEnd.spec.ts` covering the required End-ride reload-recovery proof and a deterministic-geolocation Finish-ride proof (both the remaining-distance and the two-consecutive-fix requirements exercised explicitly against a locally-imported GPX fixture, no OpenRouteService/OpenFreeMap dependency), mirroring `e2e/ridingNextManoeuvre.spec.ts`'s own established geolocation-injection/local-map-style conventions. Version bumped to 0.3.20 (`package.json` and `package-lock.json`'s two matching `version` fields, hand-patched, following the established precedent). Real-device iPhone/bicycle verification of the completion-detection distance/accuracy constants above has not happened — do not treat that part as field-verified until it has actually been ridden.
    - **A follow-up slice (v0.3.21) corrects the closed-loop claim above and adds a mandatory completion-arming gate.** The previous paragraph's "a closed loop or an out-and-back/self-crossing route needs no special-casing... the remaining-progress gate alone already rejects" claim is **too strong** and is superseded by this entry. Direct inspection of `src/navigation/projection.ts`'s `projectFixOntoRoute` found a real, reachable hole: the very first fix of a ride has `lastMatch === null`, so it runs a **whole-route** nearest-point search rather than a windowed one; `classifyFix`'s "reacquired ⇒ untrusted" handling leaves `offRouteMachineState` at its _initial_ `"on-route"` value (an untrusted raw classification never changes the debounced level, and the initial level is already on-route). A first fix that geometrically ties — or nearly ties — between a closed loop's shared start/finish coordinate is therefore never blocked by the off-route gate, and if that same fix's whole-route search happens to snap onto the route's _final_ segment (plausible whenever start/finish geometry overlaps or nearly overlaps), `reliableDistanceFromStartMetres` can misreport near-total progress before the rider has gone anywhere — confirmed directly, not assumed: `src/navigation/rideCompletion.test.ts`'s "a hostile closed-loop start projection" test constructs exactly this input and asserts `isRouteCompletionCandidateEligible` alone _would_ have returned `true` for it, the precise pre-existing hole this slice closes.
    - **The arming invariant**: every genuinely new ride starts `completionArmed = false`. No fix counts towards the existing completion counter, and `RidingRouteCompletionPanel` cannot show, until reliable accepted fixes have first demonstrated both genuine geographic departure from the finish and credible interior route progress, across `ROUTE_COMPLETION_ARMING_CONSECUTIVE_FIXES_REQUIRED` (2, matching the completion module's own consecutive requirement) consecutive arming-eligible fixes. `src/navigation/rideCompletion.ts` gained a new pure `isRouteArmingFixEligible`, requiring both, together: the raw GPS coordinate strictly outside `ROUTE_COMPLETION_ARMING_DEPARTURE_BASE_RADIUS_METRES` (75 m — deliberately much larger than completion's own 25 m endpoint radius, a ~2× hysteresis margin against completion's ~65 m worst case) plus an accuracy allowance capped at `ROUTE_COMPLETION_ARMING_DEPARTURE_MAX_ACCURACY_ALLOWANCE_METRES` (50 m) — this cap works in the _opposite_ sense from completion's own accuracy cap: completion's cap stops a poor-accuracy fix being wrongly _admitted_ from too far away, whereas this cap stops a poor-accuracy fix facing an unreasonably _inflated_ exclusion zone it could never practically clear; and reliable route progress within `[ROUTE_COMPLETION_ARMING_INTERIOR_MIN_FRACTION, ROUTE_COMPLETION_ARMING_INTERIOR_MAX_FRACTION]` (10%–80% of the route's own total distance, route-relative so both short and long routes stay practical) — deliberately well short of 100%, so a fix that misreports near-total progress (the exact hostile scenario above) can never itself count as interior progress. `RouteCompletionTrackerState` gained `isArmed`/`consecutiveArmingEligibleCount`; its `"fix-evaluated"` event now carries both `armingEligible` and `completionEligible` in one dispatch and branches on the _current_ `isArmed`: while unarmed, only arming evidence progresses and `completionEligible` is ignored outright regardless of its value (not merely an incidental consequence of the two ranges never overlapping — they mathematically can, on a route short enough that its remaining-distance completion threshold falls inside the interior fraction; the ignore-while-unarmed rule holds regardless); once armed, only the completion streak progresses, and arming fields are frozen, one-way, for the rest of that ride. `isRouteCompletionConfirmed` gained `state.isArmed &&` as a first, defensive condition.
    - **Ownership and persistence**: `completionArmed` is owned and persisted by `useRideNavigation.ts`, mirroring `wakeLockDesired`'s exact established pattern (a plain `useState`, restored from `getActiveRideState()` in the mount effect, included in the persistence effect's write call and dependency array, reset to `false` inside `finish()`'s success branch, exposed as `completionArmed`/`setCompletionArmed`) — `storage/db.ts`'s `StoredRideState` gained an optional, non-indexed `completionArmed?: boolean` (no Dexie `version()` bump; a legacy row without it, or a legacy row whose _other_ stored fields happen to describe near-total progress, both restore as `false` — never inferred `true` from stored progress, proved directly by `mapping.test.ts`). `src/ui/riding/useRouteCompletionCandidate.ts` receives this as a new `armed: boolean` option (the current, possibly-still-restoring value) and reports its own one-way `false → true` transition back as `isArmed`; an idempotent `"external-armed"` event lets it adopt a persisted `true` value that arrives asynchronously after mount (the restore effect resolving later) without needing a fresh fix. `RidingScreen.tsx` is the single integration point that observes `completion.isArmed` and persists it: `if (completion.isArmed && !nav.completionArmed) { nav.setCompletionArmed(true); }`, called conditionally during render — the same "adjust state during render" idiom already used for `reachedManoeuvreIndex`, just operating on a setter returned from a _different_ custom hook. This was verified safe, not merely assumed: `useRideNavigation(route, ...)` executes synchronously inside `RidingScreen`'s own render body, so the `setCompletionArmed` closure it returns is bound to `RidingScreen`'s own fiber — indistinguishable, at the React-internals level, from a `useState` called directly there. Explicitly _not_ justified by "it lints clean": `eslint-plugin-react-hooks`'s `set-state-in-render` rule only traces setters back to a `useState`/`useReducer` call in the _same_ function body, so it is structurally blind to a setter reached through a custom hook's return object, in both directions — its silence here is not evidence either way, only the fiber-identity argument is. No second completion detector, no second finalisation path, no competing copies of the flag: `rideCompletion.ts`'s pure reducer remains the sole authority on whether fresh evidence is sufficient; `useRideNavigation` remains the sole authority on what's durable.
    - Preserved unchanged: the manual End-ride action (available regardless of armed state, proved by a dedicated before-and-after-arming test pair), Finish ride's own confirmation-free single-click UX, `Keep riding`'s dismissal semantics, the shared `performFinalizeRide`/`useRideNavigation.finish()` path, and every existing off-route/route-matching/progress-projection calculation this slice only ever _reads_ the output of. Automated coverage: `src/navigation/rideCompletion.test.ts` gained a full `isRouteArmingFixEligible` unit suite (mirroring completion's own, including the inverted-direction accuracy-cap case) plus tracker-level integration tests for the hostile closed-loop start, stationary endpoint jitter, a single false-departure fix resetting the streak, legitimate arming that never un-arms, every existing completion gate still holding once armed, a legitimate closed-loop arm-then-complete sequence, and overlapping out-and-back/self-intersecting geometry (reusing the existing fixtures); `src/ui/riding/useRouteCompletionCandidate.test.ts` gained arming-seeded-from-mount, arming-adopted-via-rerender, hostile-input-via-hook-options, legitimate-arming-via-hook-options, route-change-resets-armed and arming-specific fix-dedup cases; a new `src/ui/riding/RidingScreen.completionArming.test.tsx` (mirroring `RidingScreen.finishEndRide.test.tsx`'s own split-for-file-size precedent) proves the hostile scenario end-to-end through real `processFix` matching (a fix placed exactly at the route's final coordinate as the _first ever_ fix, reproducing the whole-route-reacquire code path directly rather than a synthetic stand-in), a full legitimate arm-then-complete sequence, End ride working identically before and after arming, in-memory (not just storage) arming reset after a finalise-then-restart within the same mount, a resumed `completionArmed: true` row skipping re-arming, and a legacy resumed row with no `completionArmed` field restoring unarmed; `mapping.test.ts` gained round-trip and legacy-default cases mirroring `wakeLockDesired`'s own block. The existing `e2e/ridingFinishAndEnd.spec.ts`'s deterministic-geolocation test needed no structural changes: its existing progression (two consecutive interior fixes well outside the arming departure radius, before the finish-approach fixes) already happens to satisfy arming, so the same real geolocation-driven sequence now proves arming and completion together end-to-end — confirmed by re-running it against this slice's build, not assumed. Version bumped to 0.3.21 (`package.json`/`package-lock.json`, hand-patched). Real-device iPhone/bicycle verification remains outstanding for both the original completion thresholds and these new arming thresholds — do not treat either as field-verified until actually ridden.

30. **Planning's draft-autosave can race a just-completed Save, briefly resurrecting a cleared draft — done**
    - Found while hardening `e2e/editRouteAsPlanningCopy.spec.ts`'s own coverage (item 26 above): `PlanningScreen.tsx`'s debounced draft-autosave effect (900ms, rescheduled on every waypoint/routeName/avoidFerries/profile/editCopyMeta change) and `handleSave`'s own `saveRoute()` → `clearDraft()` → waypoint-reset promise chain both wrote to the same singleton `planningDrafts` row independently, with no explicit cancellation between them beyond the effect's ordinary dependency-array cleanup. If an autosave timer already pending from an earlier edit (e.g. typing the route name just before pressing Save) had not yet fired by the time `handleSave` ran, and it fired after `clearDraft()` resolved but before the post-save waypoint-reset re-render had caused React to run the effect's own cleanup, the stale timer's callback could write the pre-save waypoints back into the just-cleared draft row. Observed directly under heavy parallel e2e load — rare, never reproduced in an ordinary interactive session — and previously worked around, not fixed, by an explicit `page.waitForTimeout(1_200)` in `e2e/editRouteAsPlanningCopy.spec.ts` before every Save. Distinct from item 31 below, which this slice leaves entirely unmodified: item 31 protects autosave against a still-loading/failed hydration read; this item coordinates autosave against a concurrent explicit Save.
    - Fixed by four small coordination primitives added to `PlanningScreen.tsx`, declared together immediately before the autosave effect: `saveTimeoutRef` (`useRef<number | undefined>`, mirroring `usePlanningRoute.ts`'s own `debounceTimeoutRef` shape) is the one synchronously-reachable handle for whichever autosave timer is currently scheduled, written by the effect and read/cleared by `handleSave`; `saveGenerationRef` (`useRef(0)`, mirroring `hydrationGenerationRef`'s existing idiom) is bumped at the start of every `handleSave` attempt and in a new unmount-only cleanup effect, and is checked by both the autosave timer callback and `handleSave`'s own `.then`/`.catch` continuations before they touch any state; `isSavingRef` (`useRef(false)`) is a synchronous re-entrancy guard checked+set as the first lines of `handleSave`, mirroring this same file's existing `isLocatingRef` rationale that a React state check alone isn't synchronous enough to block a rapid second click; `isSaving` (`useState(false)`) is the user-visible counterpart, added to the autosave effect's own gate and dependency array (`if (hydrationStatus !== "ready" || isSaving) return;`) and used to disable/relabel the Save button.
    - `handleSave` now synchronously cancels the pending timer (`window.clearTimeout(saveTimeoutRef.current)`) and bumps+captures the generation before any `await`/`.then` begins — closing the race entirely rather than narrowing it, since the only timer that could ever race a Save is the one that existed at the exact moment Save was clicked (the autosave effect's own dependencies provably don't change during the async gap, and effects only run at commit time). On failure — either `saveRoute` or the explicit `clearDraft` rejects, a single shared `.catch()` covering both and preserving existing partial-failure semantics with no cross-store rollback attempted — nothing was reset, so `isSaving` flipping back to `false` re-arms the autosave effect against current, live render state with no snapshotting. On success, the post-reset render re-arms the effect with `state.present = []`, so any further "autosave" is a harmless, idempotent `clearDraft()` call, never a resurrection. The Save button gained `disabled={!canSaveOrExport || isSaving}` and an in-flight `"Saving…"` label, matching this codebase's existing "Saving…"/"Deleting…" button vocabulary (`RouteLibrary.tsx`, `SettingsScreen.tsx`, `RouteListItem.tsx`).
    - Automated coverage: a new, dedicated `src/ui/planning/PlanningScreen.saveAutosaveRace.test.tsx` (mirroring `PlanningScreen.draftHydration.test.tsx`'s own fake-timer/controlled-promise conventions, plus a new mock of `routesRepository.ts`'s `saveRoute`) proves, deterministically: a pending autosave timer is cancelled before it can fire when Save is clicked just ahead of it; no autosave write occurs for the whole successful in-flight window and the draft is never resurrected afterward; a rapid double Save click issues exactly one `saveRoute`/`clearDraft` pair; the button shows `"Saving…"` and disables while in flight; a rejected `saveRoute` resumes autosave with the current (unreset) in-memory plan; a rejected explicit `clearDraft` (with `saveRoute` resolved) does the same, documented as accepted partial-failure semantics; a Save attempt's continuation discards itself if it resolves after unmount; a pending autosave timer never fires after unmount; ordinary post-hydration autosave is unchanged; and item 31's failed-hydration no-write invariant still holds. `PlanningScreen.test.tsx`'s two existing real-Dexie Save-click tests (`"saving clears the draft…"` and `"clears the edit-copy notice once the copy is saved"`) were extended with an edit-the-route-name-then-immediately-click-Save sequence followed by a real 1,100ms wait, re-asserting `getDraft()` is still `undefined` after the wait — an integration-level proof alongside the mocked unit-level one.
    - `e2e/editRouteAsPlanningCopy.spec.ts`'s `planAndSaveTwoWaypointRoute()` no longer waits 1,200ms before Save; it clicks Save immediately after filling the route name and asserts, via a new `readPlanningDraftRow` export added to `e2e/support/rideStateDb.ts` (mirroring `readActiveRideStateRow` exactly, extended for a third store per this file's own established precedent — see item 25), that the singleton draft row stays `null` after saving. Passed 15/15 under `--repeat-each=3` against the same heavy-parallel-load conditions that originally surfaced the race. The unrelated second `page.waitForTimeout(1_200)` in that same spec file (protecting an intentionally-unsaved, unrelated draft before navigating away to test the replace-draft confirmation dialog) was deliberately left untouched.
    - Version bumped to 0.3.22 (`package.json` and `package-lock.json`'s two matching `version` fields, hand-patched, following the established precedent). Real-device iPhone verification of the new `"Saving…"` button state is still outstanding — do not treat it as manually verified until that check has actually been done.
    - A follow-up, test-only slice removes the four `1_200`ms pre-Save workaround waits `e2e/reverseRoute.spec.ts` had carried since before this fix (in `planAndSaveTwoWaypointRoute`/`planAndSaveClosedLoopRoute` and the two direct Save clicks on a reversed draft), superseding the "left untouched, out of this slice's scope" note above. Each site now clicks Save immediately and awaits a new shared `assertPlanningDraftStaysCleared(page)` helper. This is deliberately stronger than `editRouteAsPlanningCopy.spec.ts`'s own `expect.poll(() => readPlanningDraftRow(page), {...}).toBeNull()` proof: inspection found that pattern's poll runs after an assertion that the "route saved" heading is visible, and `handleSave`'s own promise chain resolves `clearDraft()` strictly before the callback that makes that heading visible fires — so that poll's first check already observes `null` and succeeds immediately, without ever waiting through the ~900ms window in which a _regressed_ (non-synchronous) timer cancellation would let a stale autosave fire and resurrect the row later. `assertPlanningDraftStaysCleared` therefore polls to `null` once and then keeps re-sampling the real row every 150ms across a 1,200ms margin (900ms debounce + 300ms headroom), so a resurrection landing anywhere in that window is still caught; `editRouteAsPlanningCopy.spec.ts` itself was left unmodified (out of this slice's own scope) and still carries the weaker, first-success-only form. A fifth, differently-purposed wait — before `page.reload()` in the "reloading after creating the reverse draft…" test, confirming the seeded draft's fields are genuinely persisted before reloading — was replaced too, since it was never actually a Save-race guard (no Save button is pressed near it): it now polls the real `planningDrafts` row directly (`expect.poll(() => readPlanningDraftRow(page), {...}).toMatchObject({ routeName: ... })`), a strictly stronger, equally small postcondition than the fixed wait it replaced. The one remaining `1_200`ms wait in `reverseRoute.spec.ts` (protecting an intentionally-unsaved, unrelated draft before navigating away to test the reverse-specific replace-confirmation dialog) is unrelated to the Save race and was left unchanged, mirroring `editRouteAsPlanningCopy.spec.ts`'s own identical, still-untouched wait. No production code changed and no version bump was needed, matching this same entry's own established precedent for test-only hardening.
    - A second follow-up, also test-only, supersedes the "still carries the weaker, first-success-only form" note above: `e2e/editRouteAsPlanningCopy.spec.ts`'s own `planAndSaveTwoWaypointRoute` helper now also awaits a locally-copied `assertPlanningDraftStaysCleared(page)` (identical mechanism to `reverseRoute.spec.ts`'s own — poll to `null`, then re-sample every 150ms across the same 1,200ms margin), so it too samples across the complete danger window rather than stopping at the first successful poll. This file's other two Save clicks — saving an edited three-waypoint copy, and saving a GPX-derived copy — were deliberately left unasserted: direct reading of `PlanningScreen.tsx` confirms `handleSave`'s cancellation sequence never branches on `editCopyMeta`, waypoint count or origin, so the one strengthened site already proves the same generic mechanism these other saves also exercise; duplicating full-window sampling there would add ~1.2s per site for no additional coverage. The unrelated `page.waitForTimeout(1_200)` protecting an intentionally-unsaved draft before the replace-confirmation-dialog test remains untouched, since no Save button is involved there. No production code changed and no version bump was needed, matching this same entry's own established precedent for test-only hardening.

31. **Planning draft hydration/autosave race — done**
    - A dedicated reliability slice, distinct from item 30 above. Evidence-backed root cause, confirmed by direct reading of the pre-fix `PlanningScreen.tsx`: the hydration effect's `getDraft().then(...).catch((error) => { logError(...); setIsDraftHydrated(true); })` set the single boolean coordination signal `isDraftHydrated` to `true` on a **rejected** read — without ever having applied anything, `state.present` was still the pristine empty array. The debounced autosave effect, gated only on `isDraftHydrated`, then saw "hydration complete, zero waypoints" and after 900ms called `clearDraft()` — silently deleting whatever real draft row existed in storage, which had never actually been read. Particularly dangerous immediately after item 26/27's "Edit copy in Planning"/"Reverse route" flows seed that same singleton row before navigating in. Separately confirmed safe and unchanged: `RidingScreen.tsx`'s `performCopyOperation` already `await`s `saveDraft(...)` before calling `onNavigateToPlanning?.()`, and `App.tsx` never mounts `PlanningScreen` before that navigation, so the _seeding_ write itself was never part of the race — the bug was entirely inside `PlanningScreen.tsx`'s own hydration/autosave coordination.
    - New invariant: _Planning must not autosave until the initial draft read has completed successfully and the resolved draft, or the deliberate fresh-plan defaults when no draft exists, has been applied as the authoritative in-memory state._ Implemented as an explicit `PlanningDraftHydrationStatus = "loading" | "ready" | "failed"` state machine (extending this file's own existing `locateStatus` tri-state convention, rather than importing a cross-file pattern) plus a numeric `hydrationGenerationRef` (mirroring `useRideNavigation.ts`'s `watchGenerationRef` idiom) as the sole authority on whether a given `getDraft()`/`getPlanningPreferences()` attempt is still relevant — replacing the old per-effect `cancelled` closure boolean entirely, including the _nested_ fresh-session `getPlanningPreferences()` continuation, not just the outer `getDraft()` one. Both the debounced autosave effect and the existing fresh-session location-framing effect now gate on `hydrationStatus === "ready"` instead of the removed `isDraftHydrated` boolean. A rejected `getDraft()` now sets `hydrationStatus` to `"failed"`, never `"ready"` — nothing was read or applied, so autosave stays genuinely blocked, and no `clearDraft()`/`saveDraft()` call can ever fire from that state. The restore batch (waypoints, route name, avoid-ferries, profile, and — set together, never separately — edit-copy/reversal provenance) is still applied as one atomic React state flush inside a single `.then()` callback, exactly as before; only the completion condition changed.
    - Obsolete/superseded results: the generation ref is bumped both at the top of every effect run (initial mount and every retry, via a `hydrationRetryToken` state bumped by the Retry button) and in the effect's own cleanup, so a result from a prior attempt can never apply after unmount, a retry, or React StrictMode's mount→cleanup→mount double-invocation (`getDraft()` is a read, so a harmless duplicate call; only the winning generation's result is ever applied).
    - "Which state wins" while hydration is loading or has failed: rather than blocking Planning's controls before hydration completes (the map and every interactive control already render unconditionally today, and gating them behind a loading screen would have needed a much larger change, breaking a large fraction of the pre-existing, non-async-aware `PlanningScreen.test.tsx` in the process, for no additional data-integrity benefit over the approach below), a new `hasUserModifiedDraftFieldsRef` — set by a new `noteHydrationOverriddenByUserEdit()` helper called at the top of `dispatchWaypointAction` (the one wrapper every waypoint mutation already goes through) and at the cycling-profile buttons' `onClick` and the route-name input's `onChange` — tells the hydration effect's _restore_ branch specifically to skip re-applying its fields once the rider has already directly changed one of the three restorable fields (waypoints, route name, profile) themselves, rather than silently overwriting their edit; `hydrationStatus` still transitions to `"ready"` either way, so autosave is unblocked for whichever state — restored or rider-edited — is now authoritative. Separately, the same helper unblocks a permanently `"failed"` status directly (`setHydrationStatus((current) => current === "failed" ? "ready" : current)`), since a rejected read has no pending promise left to ever resolve it any other way, and the rider has now started fresh work that deserves saving.
    - **A real regression found and fixed during this slice's own verification, not merely theorised**: the first implementation made `noteHydrationOverriddenByUserEdit()` unconditionally force `hydrationStatus` to `"ready"` and bump the generation on _any_ pre-ready edit — which broke three genuine, pre-existing tests (`"a genuinely fresh draft (no prior draft row) seeds avoidFerries from the current Settings default"` and its two siblings), all of which place a waypoint via a bare map tap immediately after mount, with no `await` in between, before the fresh-session `getPlanningPreferences()` read has any chance to resolve. The fix: the rider-edit override now only gates the **restore** branch's field-application (which genuinely conflicts with a waypoint/name/profile edit), never the **fresh-session** branch's `getPlanningPreferences()` seeding (which touches only `avoidFerries` and so can never conflict with any of the three fields the override tracks) — the fresh branch is unconditional, exactly as before this slice. This is a stronger, more precise statement of "which state wins" than the slice's own first draft, verified against real, previously-passing product behaviour rather than assumed.
    - Hydration failure shows a concise `.field-error`/`role="alert"` message ("Your saved plan could not be loaded. Nothing in storage has been changed.") plus a `.btn-secondary` "Retry" button (reusing this screen's own existing error/button vocabulary, not Riding's), positioned directly beneath the existing `NoApiKeyNotice`, above the `editCopyMeta` notice. Nothing is ever auto-cleared from storage on failure. The Retry button itself sets `hydrationStatus` back to `"loading"` directly in its own `onClick` before bumping `hydrationRetryToken` — mirroring `handleLocateMe`'s own existing convention of setting status in the triggering event handler rather than inside the effect it kicks off, which also avoids a `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` lint violation a synchronous `setHydrationStatus("loading")` at the top of the effect body would otherwise trip. A separate, non-blocking `role="status"` "Loading your plan…" message shows only while `hydrationStatus === "loading"` — additive, and in production typically imperceptible, since IndexedDB reads normally resolve in low single-digit milliseconds.
