    - Out-of-order autosave writes (this slice's own required audit): two new characterization tests in `planningDraftRepository.test.ts` prove `saveDraft`/`clearDraft` calls issued back-to-back with zero awaited gap between them still commit strictly in call order against this repository's real Dexie/fake-indexeddb stack — same-object-store readwrite transactions are queued and committed in request order by the IndexedDB spec itself. This closes the audit for the mechanism this slice touches (a single debounced timer per mount, cancelled before a new one is scheduled, so only one `saveDraft`/`clearDraft` call is ever in flight from the autosave effect itself) — **not a fix**, positive regression coverage for already-safe behaviour. It does **not** address item 30 above, which remains a separate, open, unfixed race.
    - Automated coverage: a new `src/ui/planning/PlanningScreen.draftHydration.test.tsx` (deliberately separate from the existing, much larger `PlanningScreen.test.tsx`, which never mocks the draft repository and never uses fake timers — this new file mocks `planningDraftRepository.ts`/`planningPreferencesRepository.ts` and uses `vi.useFakeTimers({ toFake: ["setTimeout", "clearTimeout"] })`, deliberately narrowed to avoid interfering with React's own scheduler, plus manually-controlled promises, to hold a draft read open indefinitely and assert no premature write occurs) covers: no write while an existing-draft read is pending, full restoration of every field (including edit-copy/reversal provenance) once it resolves; fresh-session defaults applied once, then normal autosave; an editable-copy and a reversed-copy draft both surviving a delayed read with zero premature writes and (for the reversed case) zero routing-provider requests; hydration failure blocking autosave and showing the accessible failure state, with a successful retry recovering; a hydration result that resolves after unmount doing nothing; a rider edit landing before a slow restore-branch read resolves taking precedence over that read's own content; the Settings avoid-ferries default still applying even when a waypoint edit lands before the fresh-session preferences read resolves (the regression above, now pinned as an explicit test); and a plain post-hydration edit continuing to debounce-save normally. `e2e/planning.spec.ts` gained one new reload-recovery test for a plain, hand-built (non-edit-copy) Planning draft — the one reload-recovery path not already covered; `e2e/reverseRoute.spec.ts`'s existing `"reloading after creating the reverse draft recovers the draft and notice with no automatic routing request"` test (around line 521) already proves the edit-copy/reverse-seeded reload path and needed no changes, and passes unchanged against this slice's rewritten hydration effect.
    - No routing endpoint/headers/payload/profile, leg caching, GPX behaviour, route-reversal semantics, map camera behaviour, gradient/warning/elevation calculation, saved-route data, service-worker caching, or CI workflow changed. Version bumped to 0.3.19 (`package.json` and `package-lock.json`'s two matching `version` fields, hand-patched, following the established 0.3.17→0.3.18 precedent). This is a pure logic/state-integrity fix with a small, additive accessible-status/error UI; real-device iPhone verification of the new loading/failure/retry UI is still outstanding — do not treat it as manually verified until that check has actually been done.

32. **`ridingFinishAndEnd.spec.ts`'s completion-detection test: an unconfirmed CPU-contention flake**
    - Found 2026-08-12 while hardening the four `page.waitForTimeout(300)` sites (commit `c243100`, atop `9372991`). Under heavy artificial contention (`taskset -c 0-3`, 4-6 Playwright workers pinned to 4 cores), `e2e/ridingFinishAndEnd.spec.ts`'s "conservatively confirms route completion only after consecutive fixes, and Finish ride clears recovery state" test occasionally failed at `await expect(page.getByText("Route complete")).toBeVisible()` (a 5000ms timeout, element not found), after the test's second consecutive finish-line geolocation fix.
    - Reproduced identically on the pristine pre-`c243100` baseline (`git stash`, rebuilt, re-run) under the same contention — 2/30 failures, same assertion, same line — confirming this predates and is unrelated to that commit's own persistence-polling changes. Never reproduced under ordinary, non-contended concurrency across 150+ runs of the same test.
    - Not investigated further: the reproduction depended on artificial contention deliberately introduced to stress-test unrelated code, not a condition either normal local runs or CI would ordinarily hit. Investigate only if this exact assertion is seen failing in an ordinary local run or in a real GitHub Actions run.

### Navigation and library interface

33. **Compact Route Library grouping — done**
    - Follow-up to item 20 (Route pinning). Remove the `Pinned`/`Other routes` `<h2>` headings (`RouteLibrary.tsx`) — the pin toggle itself (`.route-pin-toggle`/`.is-pinned`, item 20) already communicates pin state, and the headings cost vertical space without adding information. Preserve the existing pinned-first ordering, pin-recency-within-pinned, `Most recent`/`Name A–Z` applied only within the unpinned group, and all existing search/sort/rename/export/delete behaviour (`selectRouteLibraryGroups`, `routeLibraryView.ts`) exactly as item 20 built it.
    - Required outcome: no visible `Pinned`/`Other routes` headings; pinned-first ordering, pin-recency-within-pinned, and existing sort within unpinned all unchanged; pin/unpin toggle, its `aria-pressed`/accessible label, and focus behaviour after pin/unpin preserved, with no regression even if the underlying DOM structure changes; search, sort, rename, export, delete, scroll restoration and duplicate-name handling unchanged.
    - **Implementation (supersedes the two-`<ul>` architecture and open `pendingPinFocusIdRef` question this item originally recorded above):** `RouteLibrary.tsx`'s render block became a single `<ul className="route-list">{viewRoutes.map(renderCard)}</ul>` — `viewRoutes` (`[...groups.pinned, ...groups.unpinned]`) was already the correctly-ordered flattened array feeding scroll restoration, rename-orphan focus and `computeFocusRouteIdAfterDelete`, so none of those needed to change. `groups`/`selectRouteLibraryGroups` are unchanged: pinned and unpinned still need their own sort policy (pin recency vs. the rider's chosen order) before the caller flattens them into one render order. `.route-list`'s existing `gap: var(--space-12)` now governs the pinned/unpinned boundary automatically, with no special-cased extra gap — confirmed both by a real-browser `getBoundingClientRect()` gap measurement (12px uniformly, including across the boundary) and a manual screenshot at the 390×844 phone viewport with a pinned/unpinned mix.
    - **`pendingPinFocusIdRef` was kept, not removed — but for a different, previously-undocumented reason than the "two separate `<ul>` parents" rationale this item originally suspected.** The single-list restructure was tried first: removing the ref/effect/`pinButtonRefs` Map/`pinButtonRef` prop and re-running the full pinning suite. Every jsdom/RTL test passed (108/108, including two new tests added specifically to exercise a pin and an unpin crossing a non-empty-pinned/non-empty-unpinned boundary) — jsdom does not reproduce a real browser's native rule that a focused control is blurred automatically the instant it becomes `disabled`, so it couldn't have caught the actual defect. A real-browser Playwright run of the same scenario (`e2e/routeLibraryPinning.spec.ts`) failed immediately (`toBeFocused()` — "Received: inactive"): the pin toggle is `disabled` for the duration of the write, so the browser blurs it on click regardless of DOM structure, and nothing re-focuses it once the mechanism is removed. The mechanism was restored. A second, genuinely pre-existing defect (present before this slice, just never caught by any prior real-browser focus assertion) was found and fixed at the same time, confirmed via direct DOM-state instrumentation rather than guessed: the routes live-query update (which reruns the focus effect, keyed on `groups`) and the `pinPendingIds` state clear (which re-enables the button) are two independent async updates with no guaranteed order, and the live query consistently landed first in this build — so the effect fired and called `.focus()` while the button was _still_ disabled, silently no-opped, and consumed the pending marker with nothing left to retry it. Fixed by adding `pinPendingIds` to the effect's own dependency array and checking `!button.disabled` before consuming the marker, so it retries on the next relevant render instead of giving up — verified via 5 repeated real-Chromium runs of both pinning e2e tests (10/10 passed) after the fix, against a reliable, repeatable failure before it. Both doc comments (`RouteLibrary.tsx`'s effect, `RouteListItem.tsx`'s `pinButtonRef` prop) were rewritten to state this evidence-backed cause, not the original "two separate list parents" premise, which the single-list restructure now makes moot.
    - The delete-confirmation heading (`RouteListItem.tsx`) changed from `<h3>` to `<h2>`, since it previously sat one level beneath the now-removed group `<h2>`s and would otherwise skip a level beneath the page's own single `<h1>` ("Routes"). `index.css`'s `.route-delete-confirm h3` selector and its comment were updated to `.route-delete-confirm h2` accordingly; `role="alertdialog"`/`aria-labelledby`/`aria-describedby`/focus/Escape/Cancel/disabled-while-deleting behaviour is unchanged, since all of it is id- or text-based, not tag-based.
    - Files changed: `src/ui/library/RouteLibrary.tsx`, `src/ui/library/RouteListItem.tsx`, `src/index.css`, `src/ui/library/routeLibraryView.ts` (light doc-comment edit only, no functional change), `src/ui/library/RouteLibrary.test.tsx`, `e2e/routeLibraryPinning.spec.ts`.
    - Verification: focused suites (`RouteLibrary.test.tsx`, `RouteListItem.test.tsx`, `routeLibraryView.test.ts` — 108/108) and the full repository suite (`npm run lint`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run build`, `npm test` — 2194/2194 across 120 files, `npm run e2e` — 98/98 across both the `chromium` and `android-chrome` projects including the two updated pinning tests, `npm run format:check`) all pass. Real-device iPhone verification is now confirmed (17 August 2026): the pinned/unpinned grouping without visible group headings reads correctly on the installed iPhone Home Screen PWA, with pin/search/sort/rename/export/delete behaviour unaffected. Physical Android verification has not happened — do not treat this item as Android-verified until that check has actually been done.

34. **iPhone navigation chrome opacity and waypoint-row layout correction — done**
    - Presentation-only correction, evidenced on the installed iPhone Home Screen PWA in portrait orientation (screenshots reviewed 2026-08-12). Two related but independently testable defects, both now fixed.
    - **Sticky-header safe-area opacity — evidence-backed root cause.** Follow-up to item 24 (Always-sticky compact primary navigation). `index.css`'s `.app-header--sticky` rule pinned itself at `top: env(safe-area-inset-top, 0px)`, so its own opaque `background` began _below_ the status-bar strip, not at it. `.app-shell`'s own `padding-top: env(safe-area-inset-top, 0px)` only reserves that strip once, at the very start of the document flow; once the page is scrolled past that one-time reservation, nothing continued to protect `viewport-y ∈ [0, inset)` — whatever page content had scrolled to that screen position painted there instead, exactly matching the observed "scrolled content visible through the strip" symptom. `.app-shell` itself has no `background` of its own, so there was no ancestor backstop either.
    - **First attempt, tried and empirically disproven — recorded for anyone revisiting this.** The first fix attempted was a negative-margin trick: give `.app-header--sticky` `margin-top: calc(-1 * inset)` to algebraically cancel `.app-shell`'s own padding-top contribution, keeping `top` unchanged. A minimal, framework-free repro (`position: sticky`, first child, ancestor `padding-top: 59px`, `margin-top: -59px`, `top: 59px`) disproved this directly: the rendered box stayed pinned at `y = 59` at every scroll position, never `y = 0` — Chromium does not fold a sticky element's own negative margin into the "flow position" its stickiness offset is computed from, unlike an ordinary static/relative box (confirmed by the identical repro with `position: static`, which correctly rendered at `y = 0`). This is a genuine browser behaviour, not a spec detail this file got wrong — do not reintroduce a negative-margin cancellation for a sticky element without re-testing it directly first.
    - **Shipped fix.** A new project-owned custom property, `--safe-area-inset-top: env(safe-area-inset-top, 0px)` (`:root`), is shared by `.app-shell` and `.app-header--sticky` so the two always agree and the value is independently testable. `.app-shell` gained `.app-shell:has(> .app-header--sticky) { padding-top: 0; }` — removing the ancestor's top padding entirely whenever a sticky header is present (`:has()` is supported since Chrome 105/Safari 15.4, comfortably below this project's target platforms), rather than trying to algebraically cancel it. `.app-header--sticky` folds that same inset directly into its own `padding-top: calc(var(--safe-area-inset-top) + var(--space-8))` and sets `top: 0` — with no ancestor padding left to overcome, the header's natural flow position already sits at the true viewport top, so `top: 0` needs no offsetting trick and is continuous by construction (re-verified with the same minimal-repro method: zero ancestor padding, sticky `top: 0`, the inset folded into the sticky element's own padding-top, stayed pinned at `y = 0` across every scroll position tested, both at rest and once genuinely stuck). `.main-nav`'s own visual position is unchanged (the same total offset as before, `inset + space-8`, just sourced entirely from the header's own box instead of split between the ancestor and header). A new `padding-bottom: var(--space-8)` on `.app-header--sticky` only is the required lower buffer — the base `header` rule (used by active Riding's static header) is untouched, so that header keeps its original zero bottom padding and normal document-flow behaviour exactly as item 24 specifies. On a non-notched device/browser, `--safe-area-inset-top` resolves to `0px`, so every new declaration is a complete no-op there.
    - **Making it testable.** Desktop Chromium and the `android-chrome` Playwright project both always resolve `env(safe-area-inset-top)` to `0px` and cannot emulate iOS standalone status-bar compositing, so `e2e/stickyNavigation.spec.ts` gained a new `"synthetic safe-area inset (iOS status-bar strip coverage)"` group that overrides `--safe-area-inset-top` with a representative non-zero value (59px) via an inline style set through `page.addInitScript`. This surfaced a second genuine, empirically-confirmed finding: `document.documentElement` is not always present yet when an `addInitScript` callback first runs (a bare `document.documentElement.style.setProperty(...)` there threw `Cannot read properties of null`, silently making the whole override a no-op with no visible test failure until the geometry assertions caught it) — fixed with a `MutationObserver` on `document` itself (which always exists) that applies the property the instant `<html>` is inserted, well before this app's own deferred module script can run. The new tests prove: the header's opaque box starts at the true viewport top under the synthetic inset; `.main-nav` still sits below `inset + --space-8`; a `--space-8`-scale gap separates `.main-nav` from the header's own border; the header's background is genuinely opaque; the box stays pinned after a real document scroll; ordinary screens stay sticky under the synthetic inset; and active Riding stays static, with its `padding-bottom` unaffected by the sticky-only buffer. Both new tests were confirmed, by temporarily reverting only the CSS fix and rebuilding, to fail against the pre-fix rules with the exact predicted symptom (`.main-nav`'s bounding box landing at the old, unpatched offset) — genuine regression detectors, not vacuous assertions. This is structural, Chromium-verified evidence of the CSS geometry only, not proof of the real iOS Safari/WKWebView status-bar compositor result, which remains an outstanding real-device check.
    - **Waypoint-row wrapping — confirmed root cause.** Follow-up to Milestone 3's fourth slice (`WaypointList` markup) and item 17. `.waypoint-row-main` was `display: flex; flex-wrap: wrap` over three children — the shrinkable `.waypoint-row-select`, the fixed `.waypoint-row-reorder`, and a standalone Delete button with no `flex-shrink`/`flex-basis` reservation of its own. Once the row's available width fell short of all three at their natural sizes, `flex-wrap` pushed Delete (the last flex item) onto its own line. The previously-recorded bold-selected-label width increase (`.waypoint-row.is-selected .waypoint-row-label { font-weight: 700 }`) is confirmed as a genuine contributor (it narrows the margin before the threshold is crossed) but not the sole cause: the structural defect is that `flex-wrap: wrap` permitted wrapping at all for a row that must always stay single-line.
    - **Fix.** `.waypoint-row-main` changed from `display: flex; flex-wrap: wrap` to `display: grid; grid-template-columns: minmax(0, 1fr) auto auto;` — the entire code change. With exactly three fixed grid children mapped 1:1 onto three explicit columns, there is no `flex-wrap` mechanism left to trigger and no implicit second row, so Delete cannot wrap regardless of waypoint count or selection state. Column 1 absorbs available width via the unchanged `.waypoint-row-select { min-width: 0 }` (which does the same job for a grid item that it previously did for a flex item); columns 2/3 size to the reorder group's and Delete's own natural (≥44px touch-target) content width. No markup changed in `WaypointList.tsx`; `.waypoint-row-relocate` (the selected row's Move/Insert-after sub-row) is a sibling of `.waypoint-row-main`, not a grid child, and is completely unaffected.
    - **Testing.** `WaypointList.test.tsx` gained a separate 12-waypoint fixture (independent of the existing 3-item `WAYPOINTS`, which is untouched) with accessible-name and endpoint-disabled coverage at double-digit ordinals, unselected and selected — Vitest's `css: false` environment cannot observe the grid layout itself, so this only extends existing accessible-name coverage, not a no-wrap proof. The actual no-wrap proof is a new `e2e/planning.spec.ts` test at the 390×844 phone viewport: 12 waypoints placed via direct map taps (no OpenRouteService contact — Calculate route is a separate, explicit action), with genuine `getBoundingClientRect()`-style geometry proving `.waypoint-row-main`'s Select/Move-up/Move-down/Delete controls occupy the same vertical band and the wrapper is no taller than its tallest child, for both an unselected double-digit row and the selected final row, plus that the relocate sub-row sits below without disturbing the main row's own geometry, plus 44×44 touch-target and no-horizontal-overflow checks. Confirmed, by the same temporarily-reverted-CSS method used for Scope A, to fail against the pre-fix rules (the wrapped row rendered at roughly double a single control's height) — a genuine regression detector.
    - Files changed: `src/index.css`, `e2e/stickyNavigation.spec.ts`, `e2e/planning.spec.ts`, `src/ui/planning/WaypointList.test.tsx`, plus this entry and the version bump below. No changes to `App.tsx`, `MainNavigation.tsx`, `navPositionMode.ts`, `WaypointList.tsx`, `PlanningScreen.tsx`, or `e2e/androidMobileLayout.spec.ts` (re-run unmodified as a regression check, since the CSS is shared across both Playwright projects).
    - Verification: focused suites (`App.test.tsx`, `MainNavigation.test.tsx`, `navPositionMode.test.ts`, `WaypointList.test.tsx`, `e2e/stickyNavigation.spec.ts`, the new `e2e/planning.spec.ts` waypoint test, `e2e/androidMobileLayout.spec.ts`) and the full repository suite (`npm run format:check`, `npm run lint`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run build`, `npm test` — 2197/2197 across 120 files, `npm run e2e` — 102/102 across both the `chromium` and `android-chrome` projects) all pass. Version bumped to 0.3.24 (`package.json` and `package-lock.json`'s two matching `version` fields, hand-patched).
    - Real-device iPhone verification of the corrected safe-area strip, including the real iOS Safari/WKWebView status-bar compositor, and of the waypoint-row layout at double-digit counts, is now confirmed — checked on the installed iPhone Home Screen PWA in portrait orientation, most recently at v0.3.25 (see "Manual acceptance status" above). Real Android Chrome verification (distinct from Playwright's `android-chrome` Chromium-emulated project) remains outstanding for both — do not treat either as Android-verified until that check has actually been done.
    - **Corrective follow-up (ordinal 10's endpoint badge still wrapped) — done.** Real iPhone testing after the fix above found one remaining defect the 12-waypoint e2e fixture above never covered: inside column 1 of `.waypoint-row-main` (`.waypoint-row-select`, holding the ordinal badge and the label), "Waypoint 10" wrapped onto two lines specifically when ordinal 10 was an endpoint (white `--finish` or green `--start-finish` badge, `border: 3px solid`) but not when it was an ordinary intermediate waypoint (`border: 2px solid`) — confirmed root cause: the endpoint badge's extra 1px of border per side (+2px total width at a two-digit ordinal) was just enough to push the label past its available shrink space, given `.waypoint-row-select` still inherited the global `button` rule's full `var(--space-8) var(--space-16)` padding and had no internal column structure of its own beyond plain flex. **Fix.** `.waypoint-row-select` became a fixed 2-column grid (`grid-template-columns: 32px minmax(0, 1fr)`, the 32px slot measured directly against the widest real badge — `.waypoint-row-ordinal--finish`/`--start-finish` at a two-digit ordinal, ~27–31px across the font stacks measured — with a small safety margin) — a constant badge-slot width immune to badge-role/border-width variance, so the label always starts at the same x position and gets the same available width regardless of which of the four roles renders; `.waypoint-row-ordinal` gained one added declaration, `justify-self: center`, to keep the badge centred instead of Grid's default stretch-to-fill distorting its circular/rounded-square shape. A real-browser measurement (this button's own font-size, 16px regular weight) found "Waypoint 10" needs ~100px on one line, more than an 8px-per-side padding plus an 8px badge-label gap would leave inside this button's own fixed ~153px grid-track width (dictated externally by `.waypoint-row-main`'s other two columns, untouched) — no combination of gap and badge-slot width closes that shortfall while padding stays at 8px per side, so both the horizontal padding and the internal badge-label gap were reduced to `var(--space-4)` (4px) instead of the originally-proposed `var(--space-8)` (8px), a measured deviation from the initial estimate, chosen because the single-line requirement is the actual outcome that matters; 4px already has a direct precedent in this same file (`.waypoint-row-reorder`'s own inter-button gap). Vertical padding was left entirely unstated (not the shorthand, and not `.waypoint-row-icon-button`'s zero-vertical-padding pattern), so it stays genuinely inherited and the existing row/touch-target vertical geometry is provably unchanged. The dead `flex: 1 1 auto` (a no-op since the fix above made the parent `display: grid`) was removed. **Testing.** A new `e2e/planning.spec.ts` phone-viewport test proves all three ordinal-10 roles (`start-finish` via Return to start, `finish` on a genuinely new open-route 10th waypoint after Undo, `ordinary` once an 11th waypoint is added) stay single-line — via a label-height-vs-known-single-line-label structural comparison, not a hardcoded pixel constant — stay within their own button, stay in the same vertical band as Move/Delete, keep 44×44 touch targets, and that the label's own left edge lands at the same x position across all three roles, proving the fixed badge slot. Confirmed, by the same temporarily-reverted-CSS method used above, to fail against the pre-fix rules with the exact predicted symptom (the label's rendered height roughly doubling, from a genuine text wrap at the space in "Waypoint 10") — a genuine regression detector, not a vacuous assertion, in this same Chromium build. `.waypoint-row-main`'s own 3-column grid and every `.waypoint-row-ordinal`/role-modifier rule's existing visual declaration are unchanged; `WaypointList.tsx` was not touched. Files changed: `src/index.css`, `e2e/planning.spec.ts`, plus this entry and the version bump below. Verification: focused suites (`WaypointList.test.tsx`, `PlanningScreen.test.tsx` — 118/118 unchanged) and the full repository suite (`npm run format:check`, `npm run lint`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run build`, `npm test`, `npm run e2e` across both the `chromium` and `android-chrome` projects) all pass. Version bumped to 0.3.25. **Real-device iPhone verification of this corrective fix is now confirmed.** Checked on the installed iPhone Home Screen PWA in portrait orientation at the deployed v0.3.25 (commit `7fae104`): the centred fixed-width badge slot and aligned labels look correct, waypoint labels stay single-line through waypoint 106 — including endpoint and closed-loop start/finish badge roles, well beyond this slice's own three-state automated proof — and the three-digit layout remains acceptably readable (see "Manual acceptance status" above). Real Android Chrome verification (distinct from Playwright's `android-chrome` Chromium-emulated project, re-run here only as an unrelated regression check) remains separately outstanding — do not treat this corrective slice as Android-verified until that check has actually been done.

### Planning draft workflow

35. **One-time Planning camera fit for restored or seeded waypoints — done**
    - Follow-up to Milestone 3's second slice (the existing fit-once-per-draft-on-first-calculated-route behaviour, `usePlanningRoute.ts`'s `isFirstRouteForDraft`) and to item 26 (Edit an existing route as a new Planning draft). Confirmed root cause matched this item's own original description: no "fit to waypoints" logic existed anywhere in Planning; the only pre-calculation camera framing was the existing one-time ~50×50 km regional box (`localAreaBounds.ts`), explicitly skipped whenever `waypointsRef.current.length > 0` — true for every restored draft and every edit-copy/reverse-copy seed. A restored/seeded draft genuinely fell through to MapLibre's raw whole-world default camera until a route was calculated.
    - **Mechanism.** `PlanningScreen.tsx`'s draft-hydration effect's restore branch now computes, alongside its existing `dispatch`/`setRouteName`/`setAvoidFerries`/`setProfile`/`setEditCopyMeta` calls (still gated by the same `!hasUserModifiedDraftFieldsRef.current` check), the candidate bounds for these restored/seeded waypoints via a new pure helper, `computeWaypointHydrationBounds(waypoints)`: a single waypoint reuses `computeLocalAreaBounds` (the same ~50 km "frame around one coordinate" abstraction the fresh-session effect and Locate-me already use — chosen deliberately over `computeBoundingBox` on one coordinate, which returns a degenerate zero-area box that `fitBounds`'s hardcoded `maxZoom: 16` would resolve into an unhelpfully tight, marginless zoom-in); two or more waypoints use `computeBoundingBox` (`map/routeLayer.ts`), the same coordinate-envelope helper the calculated-route fit already uses. Since calculated route geometry is never persisted in the draft, this candidate is stored in a new `pendingWaypointHydrationBounds` state (`useState<BoundingBox | null>`, never a ref, specifically so setting it triggers React to re-run the effect that consults it) rather than applied directly from the hydration effect.
    - **Where the guard actually lives, and why.** The straightforward design — checking `hasManualCameraActionRef`/`hasAppliedInitialFramingRef` directly inside the hydration effect, right where the candidate bounds are computed — was implemented first and discovered, empirically, to trip `eslint-plugin-react-hooks@7`'s `react-hooks/immutability` rule (part of `recommended-latest`, the React Compiler's own static soundness analysis): reading a ref's `.current` inside **two separate** `useEffect` bodies, when that ref is also mutated from plain callbacks (`handleLocateMe`, `MapView`'s `onUserCameraInteraction` prop), is flagged as unsound, because the analysis can no longer verify the two mutation sites stay consistent across independently-scheduled effects — confirmed by isolated reproduction (a minimal two-effect/one-callback-write probe did **not** reproduce it in isolation, but reverting to a single check-site in the real component made the flagged errors disappear and reappear deterministically as each ref's second read site was added/removed). The existing fresh-session regional-fit effect was already the _sole_ reader of `hasManualCameraActionRef`/`hasAppliedInitialFramingRef` in production code; rather than duplicating either ref (which would create two objects meant to represent the same fact, an anti-pattern this codebase explicitly avoids elsewhere — see `editCopyMeta`'s own "held as one nullable object... so the two fields can never drift out of sync" precedent), the actual guard-and-apply logic was moved into that same effect, extended with a new early branch: when `waypointsRef.current.length > 0` (previously an unconditional early return), it now checks `pendingWaypointHydrationBounds && !hasManualCameraActionRef.current && !hasAppliedInitialFramingRef.current` before calling `setBoundsTarget`, reusing the effect's own pre-existing `hasRequestedInitialLocationRef` "run my meaningful body once per mount" guard for both concerns rather than adding a second one. This keeps `hasManualCameraActionRef`/`hasAppliedInitialFramingRef` read from exactly one effect, satisfying the linter honestly rather than suppressing it, and is a smaller, more centralised diff than the original design — the fresh-session effect is now the single place all three initial-framing paths (regional geolocation fit, Locate-me, restored/seeded-waypoint fit) are decided.
    - **Guard correctness.** `hasManualCameraActionRef` is set synchronously at the very top of `handleLocateMe` (before its own `await`) and inside `onUserCameraInteraction` for any real gesture — so a rider who gestures or presses Locate-me before a slower hydration read resolves is already correctly excluded, with no new ref needed; this covers a real (not hypothetical) race, since `hydrationStatus === "loading"` never disables the map or the Locate-me button. `hasAppliedInitialFramingRef` is both checked (defensive — it cannot already be true when this branch first runs, since the restore branch and the fresh-session geolocation path are mutually exclusive within one mount) and _set_ here, which is what makes a later Locate-me press correctly recentre (`handleLocateMe`'s own `shouldApplyInitialFraming` check) rather than re-doing a fresh regional box-fit — the literal "integrate with `hasAppliedInitialFramingRef`" requirement. `hasUserModifiedDraftFieldsRef` (the pre-existing enclosing guard) already covers "the rider placed their own waypoint(s) before a discarded restore lands": in that case `pendingWaypointHydrationBounds` is never set, so the fresh-session effect's new branch sees `waypointsRef.current.length > 0` (the rider's own waypoint) but a null `pendingWaypointHydrationBounds`, and correctly applies no fit — the rider's own camera at the moment of placement is retained, exactly as a fresh session's manually-placed first waypoint already was. No coordination was needed with the existing calculated-route fit (`usePlanningRoute`'s `isFirstRouteForDraft` → `suppressInitialOverviewFit` → `MapView`'s own `computeBoundingBox(points...)` effect): that mechanism only ever fires once `routing.state.kind === "routed"`, which requires an explicit Calculate press and so is structurally always strictly later than hydration-resolve time — the two fits are temporally and architecturally disjoint, confirmed by a dedicated test proving exactly two `fitBounds` calls (waypoint envelope, then calculated-route geometry, with different bounds) and no third on a later edit-triggered recalculation.
    - **No `MapView.tsx` runtime change needed.** All four camera-command dedup refs (including `lastAppliedBoundsRequestIdRef`) are reset to `null` at the top of the map-creation effect on every `retryToken`-driven recreation, and the caller's own `boundsTarget` React state is untouched by a remount — so the still-current waypoint fit is automatically, correctly reapplied once a replacement map instance's style becomes ready, with zero new code, a generic property of the existing mechanism already relied on by the pre-existing fresh-session/Locate-me fits. Only two doc-comment updates were made there (`BoundsCameraTarget`'s interface comment and its consuming effect's comment), enumerating the new third caller, matching this file's own "enumerate every caller" documentation convention (see `CameraTarget`'s own comment) — no logic changed.
    - **One pre-existing, documented-not-fixed edge case.** Because `boundsTarget` is never cleared after being applied, if the rider manually pans _after_ any of the three fits (fresh-session, Locate-me, or this new restored-waypoint one) and _then_ a map-instance recreation happens (an imagery retry or fallback-style swap), `MapView` re-applies the stale old `boundsTarget`, overriding the manual pan. This is already true today for the two pre-existing fits — not a regression introduced by this slice — and was deliberately left unfixed, matching the item's own explicit "do not casually change MapView's established retry/fallback behaviour... do not expand this slice into a general camera-state rewrite" instruction.
    - **Testing.** `src/ui/planning/PlanningScreen.draftHydration.test.tsx` (extended with a `fitBoundsSpy` on its previously all-stub mock map): fresh-empty-draft and first-waypoint-placed-manually cases show no fit; the rider's-own-edit-wins case shows no fit for the discarded stored waypoints; the pre-existing unmount-discards-a-late-result case now also asserts no fit; a failed-first-read-then-successful-retry case asserts exactly one fit with the correct bounds (a genuinely stale _second_ generation from mid-flight retry-after-retry isn't constructible in this architecture, since Retry is UI-reachable only after a real rejection and a rejected promise can never also later resolve — the unmount case, sharing the identical generation-guard code path, stands in for "stale/superseded never frames"). `src/ui/planning/PlanningScreen.test.tsx` (real Dexie-backed, real-timer `waitFor`): an ordinary two-waypoint restored draft fits once to `computeBoundingBox`; a single restored waypoint fits to `computeLocalAreaBounds`; all four edit-copy/reverse-copy notice tests (exact/derived origin, forward/reverse operation — one reverse variant, since the mechanism doesn't discriminate on `operation`) now also assert the fit; a dedicated test proves no refit across append/select/deselect/undo/redo/delete after the initial restore; a dedicated test proves the sequencing (one waypoint fit, then one calculated-route fit with different bounds, then no third fit on an edit-triggered recalculation); a rider-gesture-before-hydration-resolves test and a rider-presses-Locate-me-before-hydration-resolves test (both landing synchronously, before the real fake-indexeddb-backed `getDraft()` read has any chance to settle) prove the restored-waypoint fit never fires and, for the Locate-me case, that Locate-me's own bounds apply instead — deterministic regardless of which of the two real promises (draft read vs. mocked geolocation) happens to resolve first, since the synchronous ref write always precedes both. **Regression proof**: the new restore-branch `setPendingWaypointHydrationBounds` call was temporarily removed (production code only, tests untouched); the "ordinary restored draft fits once" test then failed with exactly the predicted symptom (no `fitBounds` call — camera stays at MapLibre's default); the implementation was restored and the full suite re-verified green before committing.
    - **Real-browser proof**, using the local deterministic map style (never live map/routing services): `e2e/planning.spec.ts`'s existing reload-recovery test now also asserts, after reload, that both waypoint markers' real DOM bounding boxes lie fully inside the map container, that `data-camera-center`/`data-camera-zoom` are populated (not MapLibre's un-settled default), and — via a genuine manual pan through MapLibre's own deterministic `KeyboardHandler` (an unmodified `ArrowRight`, mirroring the file's existing "Locate me recentres..." test's technique) followed by placing a third waypoint — that the camera is unaffected by that later, unrelated edit; a new single-waypoint variant proves the local-area-box framing (asserting `data-camera-zoom` stays comfortably below `fitBounds`'s `maxZoom: 16`, ruling out a degenerate fit) and marker containment. `e2e/editRouteAsPlanningCopy.spec.ts` and `e2e/reverseRoute.spec.ts` each gained a local `isFullyWithin` helper (duplicated per this project's established no-shared-e2e-helpers-across-specs convention) and the identical marker-containment/camera-settled/manual-pan-preservation proof, inserted into their own main "Edit copy in Planning"/"Reverse route" flow tests — previously, both files navigated straight into a waypoint-populated `PlanningScreen` with zero camera assertions at all. One genuine, harmless behaviour change was confirmed rather than assumed: `editRouteAsPlanningCopy.spec.ts`'s existing raw pixel `mapContainer.click({x:260,y:190})` (appending a third waypoint) now lands against the newly-framed camera instead of MapLibre's previous unframed default — re-run explicitly as part of verification and confirmed still passing, since no assertion in that test pins an absolute coordinate.
    - Files changed: `src/ui/planning/PlanningScreen.tsx`, `src/map/MapView.tsx` (doc comments only), `src/ui/planning/PlanningScreen.draftHydration.test.tsx`, `src/ui/planning/PlanningScreen.test.tsx`, `e2e/planning.spec.ts`, `e2e/editRouteAsPlanningCopy.spec.ts`, `e2e/reverseRoute.spec.ts`. `usePlanningRoute.ts`, `mapAdapter.ts` and `localAreaBounds.ts` were read but not modified — no genuine need was found.
    - Verification: focused suites (`PlanningScreen.draftHydration.test.tsx`, `PlanningScreen.test.tsx`, `usePlanningRoute.test.ts`, `MapView.test.tsx`) and the full repository suite (`npm run format:check`, `npm run lint`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run build`, `npm test` — 2205/2205 across 120 files, `npm run e2e` — 104/104 across both the `chromium` (92) and `android-chrome` (12) projects) all pass. `planning.spec.ts`/`editRouteAsPlanningCopy.spec.ts`/`reverseRoute.spec.ts` are structurally excluded from the `android-chrome` project by this repo's own `android*.spec.ts` filename-matching convention (`playwright.config.ts`), so this item's new browser coverage — like the rest of these three files — only ever runs under `chromium`; this is a pre-existing project-selection limitation, not a gap introduced here. Version bumped to 0.3.26 (`package.json` and `package-lock.json`'s two matching `version` fields, hand-patched).
    - Real-device iPhone verification is now confirmed (13 August 2026): checked on the installed iPhone Home Screen PWA once commit `b8cec2c`'s deploy reached production — the first deploy that actually carried this item's changes live, since `0665d00`'s own deploy attempt was blocked by the unrelated E2E precondition failure item 44 records — the restored/seeded-waypoint Planning view no longer opens at MapLibre's whole-world default zoom and is now framed appropriately. Physical Android verification remains outstanding — per item 43's ledger, do not treat this as Android-verified until that check has actually been done. See also "Manual acceptance status" above.

36. **Compact per-draft routing preferences and reordered Calculate action — done**
    - Follow-up to item 14 (Selectable Planning cycling profiles), item 15 (Avoid ferries by default) and Milestone 4's fourth visual-migration slice (the previous `Route options` panel). Supersedes that panel's _prominence_ and location, not its underlying data model — `RoutingProfile`, `avoidFerries`, `computeRouteCalculationFingerprint`/`isStale`/`canSaveOrExportPlan` (`usePlanningRoute.ts`) are byte-identical to before; this was a presentation and Settings-defaults change only, plus one hydration-coordination redesign it made necessary (below).
    - **Storage/defaulting.** `StoredPlanningPreferences` (`storage/db.ts`) gained one plain, optional, non-indexed field, `profileByDefault?: string`, alongside the existing `avoidFerriesByDefault` — no Dexie version bump, following the exact precedent every other additive field on this table and on `StoredPlanningDraft`/`StoredRideState` already established. `PlanningPreferences`/`fromStoredPlanningPreferences`/`toStoredPlanningPreferences` (`storage/mapping.ts`) resolve it via a real `isRoutingProfile` validity check (mirroring `fromStoredPlanningDraft`'s own `profile` handling), never a bare `??` — a missing, corrupt or future-unknown stored value defaults to `DEFAULT_ROUTING_PROFILE` (`cycling-road`). `planningPreferencesRepository.ts` needed no code change at all, since `getPlanningPreferences`/`savePlanningPreferences` were already generic over the whole `PlanningPreferences` shape.
    - **Settings** (`Route planning` panel, `SettingsScreen.tsx`) gained a `Default cycling profile` control — a `role="group"` pair of buttons (`ROUTING_PROFILES`, `aria-pressed`, `.cycling-profile-group`/`.cycling-profile-button`/`.is-selected`, all reused verbatim from Planning's own existing pattern, no new CSS) with a visible `<p className="setting-row-title">` label (`aria-labelledby`), placed above the existing `Avoid ferries by default` checkbox. Both controls share one writer, `savePreferences(next: PlanningPreferences)`, so every save carries the _complete_ resolved object (never a partial one) — the concrete mechanism behind "a profile change must never reset the ferry default, and vice versa": each handler reads the other field's current resolved value into the write. A synchronous `isSavingPreferencesRef` guard (mirroring `isSavingRef`/`isLocatingRef` elsewhere in this codebase) plus `disabled={isSavingPreferences}` on both controls together prevent two overlapping writes from clobbering one another — proven by a dedicated test that toggles ferries then immediately changes profile and asserts neither write reverted the other. Non-optimistic/failure/Saving-status behaviour is unchanged from the pre-existing ferries checkbox, just generalised to cover both fields with one shared error/status pair. `routing/routingProfiles.ts`'s `cycling-road` description lost its now-false "The default profile for every new plan." claim (the road-bike routing description itself, and `DEFAULT_ROUTING_PROFILE` as the fallback when nothing is stored, are unchanged) — `isDefault`/`ROUTING_PROFILES` still key off `DEFAULT_ROUTING_PROFILE`, unrelated to the new Settings-configurable default.
    - **Fresh-vs-restored draft boundary** (unchanged from items 30/31): the same "no draft row, or a draft row with zero waypoints" boundary. A genuinely fresh draft copies both Settings defaults once, from a single `getPlanningPreferences()` read inside the existing hydration effect; a restored draft (one or more waypoints) always keeps its own stored `profile`/`avoidFerries` exactly as before, never the Settings defaults; an already-open or already-persisted draft is never rewritten, recalculated or invalidated by a later Settings change.
    - **Field-specific hydration coordination.** Before this slice, `avoidFerries` was never directly editable inside Planning, so the single boolean `hasUserModifiedDraftFieldsRef` only ever needed to gate the restore branch atomically. Now that `avoidFerries` is directly editable (like `profile` already was) and both can be touched before the fresh-session `getPlanningPreferences()` read resolves, a coarse boolean could no longer serve both consumers without either overwriting a rider's in-flight choice or suppressing an unrelated Settings default. `hasUserModifiedDraftFieldsRef` is now `Record<"waypoints" | "routeName" | "profile" | "avoidFerries", boolean>`: the restore branch still gates atomically (`Object.values(...).some(Boolean)` — any one of the four fields touched skips the _entire_ restore, preserving "never partially apply stale stored fields"); the fresh-session branch gates `profile`/`avoidFerries` independently against their own flag, so editing one never suppresses the untouched other's Settings default, and never reads the `waypoints`/`routeName` flags at all, so an early waypoint or route-name edit still can't suppress either default (the pre-existing invariant, now proven for `profile` too, not just `avoidFerries`). A `getPlanningPreferences()` rejection on a fresh session leaves `profile`/`avoidFerries` at their safe `useState` fallback values (`cycling-road`/avoided) and still reaches `hydrationStatus: "ready"`, identically to the pre-existing ferries-only behaviour.
    - **Current-draft controls reuse existing staleness gating unchanged.** The profile buttons (now inside the new disclosure) and the new avoid-ferries checkbox both call `setProfile`/`setAvoidFerries` directly — the same live state `usePlanningRoute`'s `computeRouteCalculationFingerprint`/`isStale` already reads every render, so a change marks an existing routed result stale immediately (before the debounce even starts), disables Save/Export, retains the previous result visibly, and clears once the matching recalculation resolves — no new gating logic was written, only new UI entry points into state that already existed. Both controls make zero routing-provider requests before Calculate is pressed explicitly, and switching back to a previously-used profile/ferries combination remains compatible with the existing per-leg cache.
    - **Interaction design.** A native `<details className="settings-disclosure">` (collapsed summary text exactly `Change`, reusing the exact same disclosure class as `How recalculation works` — no new focus-management state, no modal) sits beside the always-visible compact summary (`describeCurrentDraftRoutingSummary`, e.g. "Routing: Road bike · Ferries avoided"). Inside it: a `role="group" aria-label="Cycling profile for this draft"` button pair (mirroring Planning's pre-existing, purely-`aria-label`-driven convention, no separate visible heading needed since the button labels speak for themselves) and a real `<input type="checkbox">` wrapped in `.setting-row`/`.setting-row-checkbox`/`.setting-row-text`/`.setting-row-title` (accessible name "Avoid ferries for this draft", reusing Settings' own checkbox pattern). Nothing about the `<details>` element's mount identity changes when `profile`/`avoidFerries` change, so it never unexpectedly closes on selection; whether it's open is never persisted. `NoApiKeyNotice`'s `onOpenSettings={onNavigateToSettings}` remains the only Settings-navigation affordance left in Planning — no second one was added for the current-draft controls.
    - **Action-card and heading-order result.** `PlanningScreen.tsx`'s previous standalone `role="group" aria-label="Waypoint actions"` row and the entire `Route options` panel (heading included) were replaced by one `.panel.stack.planning-section` action card, positioned directly after the map and before `Waypoints`, containing in order: the unchanged waypoint-actions row (Undo/Redo/Return to start/conditional Add to end); the unchanged `.planning-calculate-actions` block (Calculate button, key status, error, stale-result message, the `How recalculation works` disclosure — none of these ever sit inside a closed disclosure, per this file's own established rule); then the compact routing summary plus the `Change` disclosure. DOM/heading order is now `Plan a route` (h1) → action card (no heading) → `Waypoints` (h2) → conditional `Route overview` (h2) → `Save or export` (h2) — no `Route options` heading exists anywhere.
    - **Testing.** Storage: no-row/legacy-row/corrupt-value defaults, full round-trip of every profile/ferry combination, and a dedicated `db.migration.test.ts` no-schema-bump block for `profileByDefault`, mirroring the existing `editCopySourceRouteId`/`editCopyWaypointsOrigin` template. Settings: default-pressed state, persistence, cross-field preservation, non-optimistic failure, and the disabled-while-saving guard on both controls together. Planning hydration (`PlanningScreen.draftHydration.test.tsx`): all four pending-hydration field-specific scenarios (profile-only edit, ferries-only edit, an early waypoint/route-name edit still letting both defaults seed, and a slow restore still blocked atomically by touching only the new ferries checkbox), plus the preferences-rejection fallback case. `PlanningScreen.test.tsx`: heading-order assertion rewritten (no `Route options`, plus a `compareDocumentPosition` proof that Calculate precedes `Waypoints`), the two tests documenting the old read-only ferries UI removed, the `cycling profile selector` block and every restore/persistence test updated to open `Change` first, and new tests for the avoid-ferries checkbox mirroring the profile selector's own coverage (staleness on change, zero requests before Calculate, leg-cache compatibility, persistence across reload). `e2e/planning.spec.ts`: the existing General-cycling routing test extended to also toggle ferries through the `Change` disclosure and assert the ORS request body omits `avoid_features: ["ferries"]`; two new tests (a fresh draft picking up a General-cycling Settings default with zero requests before Calculate; Calculate's real visual/DOM position above `Waypoints`); the phone-viewport layout test extended to prove the compact summary row and open `Change` disclosure introduce no horizontal overflow and every revealed control keeps a real ≥44×44px touch target.
    - **Resolved tooling note (TypeScript 6.0.3 closure narrowing).** While writing the `e2e/planning.spec.ts` ferries/profile request-body test above, a bare `let` variable (`capturedUrl`/`capturedBody`) reassigned only inside an asynchronous Playwright `page.route` callback and later read, via optional chaining, outside that callback was incorrectly narrowed to `never` at the read site — `expect(capturedBody?.options?.avoid_features ?? [])` failed to typecheck with `Property 'options' does not exist on type 'never'`, even though the value was genuinely assignable. Reproduced in isolation with a minimal file outside this project (`tsc --strict`), confirming it is a real TypeScript 6.0.3 (`~6.0.2` per `package.json`) narrowing defect in this exact shape, not a mistake specific to this file. Resolved, with no loss of coverage, by storing the callback's captured state as properties of one plain object (`const captured: { url: string | null; body: CapturedOrsRequestBody | null } = { url: null, body: null }`, mutated as `captured.url =`/`captured.body =` inside the route callback) instead of two separate `let` bindings — this pattern does not trigger the narrowing bug. See `e2e/planning.spec.ts`'s `captured` object (commit `91bedd4`) for the exact code; this is a test-tooling workaround only; no production code was affected.
    - **Verification.** Focused: storage (`mapping.test.ts`, `planningPreferencesRepository.test.ts`, `db.migration.test.ts`, `routingProfiles.test.ts` — 69 tests), `SettingsScreen.test.tsx` (23), `PlanningScreen.draftHydration.test.tsx` (13), `PlanningScreen.saveAutosaveRace.test.tsx` (10), `PlanningScreen.test.tsx` (108) — all pass. Full: `npm run format`, `npm run lint`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run build`, `npm test` — 2220/2220 across 120 files, `npm run e2e` — 106/106 across both the `chromium` (94) and `android-chrome` (12) projects, `npm run format:check` run last, immediately before committing — all pass. Version bumped to 0.3.27 (`package.json` and `package-lock.json`'s two matching `version` fields, hand-patched, following the established precedent).
    - Real-device iPhone verification is now confirmed (17 August 2026): the compact routing-preferences summary and its `Change` disclosure work as intended, changing profile or the ferries setting for the current draft triggers automatic recalculation, and Save stays correctly disabled while the result is stale or recalculating. Physical Android verification of this slice's visual result has not happened — do not treat this item as Android-verified until that check has actually been done.

37. **Clear the current draft and standardise draft terminology — done**
    - **Terminology.** A fresh, direct-source audit (not a reuse of this item's own earlier example list, which had gone stale — see below) found 8 user-visible strings meaning the mutable, locally autosaved Planning workspace, all renamed "plan"→"draft": `RidingScreen.tsx`'s `EDIT_COPY_CONFIG`/`REVERSE_ROUTE_CONFIG` `dialogTitle`/`dialogMessage` (`"Replace your current plan?"`→`"...draft?"`, `"Replace your current plan to reverse this route?"`→`"...draft..."`, both `dialogMessage`s' `"...unsaved plan in Planning..."`→`"...unsaved draft in Planning..."`), `handleCopyOperationClick`'s catch-path message (`"Your existing plan could not be checked. Try again."`→`"...draft..."`), `PlanningScreen.tsx`'s hydration loading/failure text (`"Loading your plan…"`→`"...draft…"`, `"Your saved plan could not be loaded..."`→`"...draft..."`), and `SettingsScreen.tsx`'s avoid-ferries-by-default hint (`"Used when a new plan is created."`→`"...draft..."`, found fresh — not on this item's own prior example list at all). This item's own previous text cited `PlanningScreen.tsx`'s avoid-ferries hint reading `"Set when this plan was created"` as a confirmed target; that exact string no longer exists anywhere in source (confirmed via `git log -S` — deleted by item 36's own "Compact per-draft routing preferences" refactor, which replaced the whole panel it lived in) — this entry replaces that stale reference with the real, current 8-item list above rather than perpetuating it. `grep -rniE` across every `*.test.ts(x)`/`*.spec.ts` in `src/`/`e2e/` for all 8 exact strings found genuinely zero existing assertions on any of them going in — except one the same sweep initially missed by being too specific (`"unsaved plan in Planning"`, case-sensitive on the wrong casing): `e2e/reverseRoute.spec.ts`'s own `/reversing this route will replace your unsaved plan/i` regex, a real pre-existing test that would have broken, caught only once the full e2e suite was actually run — fixed alongside two harmless, non-assertion comment updates in `reverseRoute.spec.ts`/`editRouteAsPlanningCopy.spec.ts` ("Start an unrelated, unsaved plan" → "...unsaved draft"). Deliberately **not** renamed: `<h1>Plan a route</h1>`/`aria-label="Planning"` (screen/domain name), `RidingScreen.tsx`'s `"Edit copy in Planning"` button label (item 38's own scope), `MainNavigation.tsx`'s `"Plan"` nav label, `SettingsScreen.tsx`'s `"Route planning"` heading, and the `"Planned route"` default _saved-route_ name (a different, larger-blast-radius concept — the default display name of a saved route, not the draft workspace label — with ~30+ existing test assertions across 6 files; out of this item's own scope). `RidingScreen.test.tsx`'s `"Unsaved plan"` string, used only as an arbitrary fixture value for a seeded draft's `routeName` in several tests, is left as-is (test data, not app source).
    - **Clear draft action.** A `.btn-danger` `Clear draft` trigger sits in its own new row at the end of the existing `.panel.stack.planning-section` action card (`PlanningScreen.tsx`, after the routing-summary/`Change`-disclosure row, before the card's own closing tag) — not inside `role="group" aria-label="Waypoint actions"` (a narrower, waypoint-list-specific group) and not folded into "Save or export" (the semantic opposite of a wipe). The shared `ConfirmDialog` is rendered as a top-level sibling immediately after the action card (mirroring `RidingScreen.tsx`'s own End-ride dialog placement), with the exact required copy: title `Clear this draft?`; message "This removes all waypoints, the calculated route and other unsaved draft details. Saved routes are not affected."; confirm label `Clear draft` (`"Clearing…"` while in flight, matching the established `"Saving…"`/`"Ending ride…"`/`"Deleting…"` in-flight-label vocabulary); cancel label `Cancel`.
    - **Race coordination — reuse, not a third mechanism.** `handleClearDraftConfirm` reuses `saveGenerationRef`/`saveTimeoutRef` literally (the same ref objects `handleSave` already declares) to cancel the pending autosave timer and invalidate stale continuations, exactly as `handleSave` itself does — satisfying this item's own "not a third, independently-invented mechanism" requirement to the letter. Clear gets its own re-entrancy/visibility state (`isClearingRef`/`isClearing`/`clearDraftError`/`isClearDraftConfirmOpen`/`clearDraftTriggerRef`), since it must work in states Save cannot (`routing.state.kind !== "routed"`) and the two are made mutually exclusive both directions: `handleSave`'s existing guard gained `|| isClearingRef.current`; the Save button's `disabled` gained `|| isClearing`; the Clear trigger's `disabled` is `isSaving || isClearing`. `clearDraft()` (writes `planningDrafts`) and a fresh `getPlanningPreferences()` read (reads `planningPreferences`) run via `Promise.all` — independent tables, no ordering dependency; the preferences read swallows its own rejection and falls back to the same safe hardcoded defaults `getPlanningPreferences()` itself resolves to when nothing has ever been saved (mirroring the existing fresh-session hydration branch's identical precedent), so a Settings-read hiccup is never reported as "the draft could not be cleared" when the destructive clear itself succeeded. `dispatchWaypointAction({type:"reset", waypoints:[]})` (not raw `dispatch`) both clears the full `waypointHistoryReducer` `past`/`present`/`future`/`selectedWaypointId` history in one action and calls `noteHydrationOverriddenByUserEdit("waypoints")` as a side effect — which is what protects against a still-in-flight _original_ mount-time `getDraft()` read resolving with stale pre-clear content afterward, reusing the existing per-field gate on `hasUserModifiedDraftFieldsRef` with no new protection code (proved directly, not assumed — see Testing).
    - **A real bug found and fixed during this slice's own e2e testing, not merely theorised.** The first implementation gated the profile/ferries reseed the same way the hydration effect's own fresh-session branch does — `if (!hasUserModifiedDraftFieldsRef.current.profile) setProfile(preferences.profileByDefault)` — reasoning by analogy from that existing branch. This was wrong: `hasUserModifiedDraftFieldsRef`'s flags mean "modified since this component _mounted_" and are never reset by an ordinary edit, so a rider who customises routing via the "Change" disclosure at any point before pressing Clear draft — an entirely normal flow, e.g. immediately after arriving via "Edit copy in Planning" — already has one or both flags permanently `true`, silently skipping Clear's own reseed exactly when it matters most: `e2e/clearPlanningDraft.spec.ts`'s main test (which drives the real "Change" disclosure buttons, not a mocked draft) failed with `aria-pressed="false"` on "Road bike" after Clear, revealing the old draft's just-cleared "General cycling" choice had survived. The equivalent `PlanningScreen.clearDraft.test.tsx` case did **not** catch this on its own first pass, because it seeded the draft's non-default profile directly via the mocked `getDraft()` resolution rather than clicking through the real UI controls — `hasUserModifiedDraftFieldsRef` was never actually touched by that path, a concrete demonstration of why this item's own "browser-level proof, not just mocked-call assertions" testing requirement mattered here specifically, not just in the abstract. Fixed by making the reseed **unconditional** — `setProfile(preferences.profileByDefault); setAvoidFerries(preferences.avoidFerriesByDefault);`, no gate at all — since Clear draft is itself the explicit "start over" request and always wins over whatever was set before it; `hasUserModifiedDraftFieldsRef` itself is otherwise left completely untouched by Clear (every other consumer — the original hydration effect's own restore/fresh-session branches — was traced and found to have no scenario where a stale flag value causes incorrect behaviour, since that effect never re-runs after Clear, which doesn't touch `hydrationRetryToken`). The regression was then reproduced deterministically at the unit/component level too (reverting only the fix, re-running, confirming the exact same `aria-pressed="false"` failure, then restoring the fix) and the strengthened `PlanningScreen.clearDraft.test.tsx` case now genuinely clicks the real "Change" disclosure controls before clearing, closing the gap the first pass left.
    - **`usePlanningRoute` extension — closes an in-flight-calculation gap.** The existing render-time `isInsufficientWaypoints` branch only ever cleared `routedResult` when waypoints dropped below 2 — it never aborted `abortControllerRef.current`, bumped `requestSeqRef.current`, or reset `isCalculating`/`updatingLegCount`/`lastErrorMessage`, so a calculation genuinely in flight when Clear draft ran (Calculate pressed, then Clear pressed before the response landed) was not aborted, and its `.then()`/`.catch()` — which only check `requestSeq !== requestSeqRef.current`, unchanged by that branch — could still have applied a stale routed result afterward. A new `reset(): void` (`usePlanningRoute.ts`, added to `UsePlanningRouteResult`, inserted immediately after `calculateNow`) aborts the controller, bumps `requestSeqRef.current`, clears the debounce timer, and resets `routedResult`/`isCalculating`/`updatingLegCount`/`lastErrorMessage` — called explicitly from Clear draft's own success handler, never automatically by the hook itself. Proved directly at the narrowest layer (`usePlanningRoute.test.ts`'s new `describe("reset", ...)` block: a queued request is genuinely aborted, and resolving it _after_ `reset()` neither revives `state.kind === "routed"` nor surfaces the abort as a user-facing error) and again through the real component (`PlanningScreen.clearDraft.test.tsx`: Calculate pressed, Clear draft confirmed while the controllable provider's promise is still pending, the promise is _then_ resolved with a route, and no Route summary region ever appears).
    - **Camera/geolocation fresh-session re-eligibility.** A new `freshSessionFramingToken` state (declared beside `hydrationRetryToken`, the same "just a counter, whose only job is to appear in a dependency array" idiom) was added to the existing fresh-session location-framing effect's dependency array — the only change to that effect; its body is untouched. Clear draft's success handler resets, in one synchronous batch, `hasRequestedInitialLocationRef.current = false` (re-arms the "run my meaningful body once" guard), `hasAppliedInitialFramingRef.current = false`, `hasManualCameraActionRef.current = false`, `hasManualGestureRef.current = false` (needed, not optional — a stale-`true` value would permanently downgrade a post-clear Locate-me press to recentre-only instead of the one-time regional fit a genuinely fresh mount would allow, reachable whenever the automatic post-clear geolocation attempt itself fails or returns null), and `pendingWaypointHydrationBounds = null`, then bumps `freshSessionFramingToken`. Writing (never reading) `hasManualCameraActionRef`/`hasAppliedInitialFramingRef` from this plain callback does not reproduce item 35's own documented `react-hooks/immutability` lint constraint, which is specifically about a _read_ from two separate effect bodies — the location-framing effect remains the sole reader, exactly like `handleLocateMe` and `onUserCameraInteraction` already are for the same two refs (confirmed clean, not merely argued: `npm run lint` passes with zero new suppressions). Ordering safety (the location effect's `waypointsRef.current.length > 0` check must see the post-clear empty array, not a stale one) is a direct consequence of React's documented effect-declaration-order execution per commit, since the `waypointsRef`-mirroring effect is declared textually earlier in the file and both effects' dependencies change in the same batched update. Proved end-to-end in both `PlanningScreen.clearDraft.test.tsx` (a `fitBoundsSpy` on the mock map records exactly one additional call directly attributable to the clear, and none from a later ordinary edit) and `e2e/clearPlanningDraft.spec.ts` (a genuine manual pan via MapLibre's `KeyboardHandler`, then Clear draft, asserts the real `data-camera-center` attribute changes away from the panned position and stays put across a later edit) — the e2e test deliberately does not assert the re-fit lands on a _specific new_ geolocation fix, having discovered along the way that `getApproximateLocationOnce`'s own deliberate `maximumAge: 5 * 60 * 1000` (a correct, pre-existing production choice, left untouched) means the real Geolocation API is permitted to return a recently-cached position rather than a brand-new reading even after `context.setGeolocation()` changes the mocked fix mid-test — a genuinely different `requestApproximateLocation` result being what gets applied is proved deterministically instead at the component level, where the mock has no such caching.
    - Cancel and a storage failure both preserve the draft exactly, mirroring the established `RouteListItem`/`RidingScreen` "disabled while in flight, so Cancel can never appear to undo work storage is still carrying out" precedent: Escape independently re-checks `isClearingRef.current` (since it bypasses `cancelDisabled`); a `clearDraft()` rejection shows a `role="alert"` "The draft could not be cleared on this device. Try again." message and closes the dialog, with focus restored to the trigger via a `useEffect` keyed on the error's own object identity (not an imperative call inside the `.catch()` block) — the trigger is still `disabled` in the DOM at the exact synchronous point `.catch()` runs, and `.focus()` on a still-disabled element silently no-ops, the same real, previously-found class of bug item 29's own End-ride implementation already documents.
    - **Testing.** `usePlanningRoute.test.ts` gained a 4-case `reset` block (no-op while idle, aborts + prevents late revival, clears a prior error/leg-progress state, cancels a scheduled recalculation debounce). A new `src/ui/planning/PlanningScreen.clearDraft.test.tsx` (13 cases, mirroring `PlanningScreen.saveAutosaveRace.test.tsx`'s exact mocked-repository/fake-timer/controlled-promise harness) covers: the dialog's exact copy and Cancel-focused state; Cancel and Escape both preserving state with no storage call; a full success case (genuinely clicking "Change" controls, not just seeding via mock — see the bug paragraph above) proving every field's reset including full undo/redo-history and routed-result removal; a `getPlanningPreferences()` rejection falling back safely with no error shown; a `clearDraft()` rejection preserving state, showing the accessible error, and a working focus-correct retry; a rapid double-confirm issuing exactly one `clearDraft()` call; Save-vs-Clear mutual exclusion; a pending autosave timer failing to resurrect the row; a delayed _original_ hydration read failing to resurrect old content after Clear (the scenario that actually exercises Clear's own `hasUserModifiedDraftFieldsRef` protection, distinct from item 31's existing rider-edits-before-hydration coverage); a delayed routing-provider result failing to restore the old route; the camera re-fit-exactly-once proof; and the renamed hydration-status terminology. `RidingScreen.test.tsx` gained a new terminology-focused test for the "could not be checked" catch path (previously entirely untested) and a positive assertion of the renamed reverse-confirmation dialog text; `SettingsScreen.test.tsx` gained one assertion for the renamed ferries hint. A new `e2e/clearPlanningDraft.spec.ts` (5 tests) proves, through the real UI and actual IndexedDB (`readPlanningDraftRow`): a meaningful draft — waypoints, a calculated route, a custom name, custom per-draft routing settings genuinely selected via the "Change" disclosure, and edit-copy provenance from a real "Edit copy in Planning" trip — opens the confirmation, is preserved exactly by Cancel, and is wiped to a genuinely fresh session using the real (untouched) current Settings defaults by Confirm, with the row proved absent across the complete autosave-debounce danger window via the established `assertPlanningDraftStaysCleared` polling pattern (duplicated locally, per this project's convention, from `editRouteAsPlanningCopy.spec.ts`/`reverseRoute.spec.ts`); a `page.reload()` after clearing does not resurrect the old draft or its calculated result; the fresh-session regional camera fit becomes available again after a genuine manual pan, exactly once; and a phone-viewport (390×844) test proves no horizontal overflow with the dialog open and a real ≥44×44px touch target on both the trigger and the dialog's own confirm button. Per this item's own testing paragraph, the delayed-hydration-read and delayed-routing-provider races are proved only at the component level (matching item 31's own precedent for its analogous races) — the brief's own "Unit/component" vs. "Real browser" section split scopes those two specifically to the former.
