38. **Reverse the current draft inside Planning — done**
    - Supersedes the pre-ride `Reverse route` entry point completed in item 27. Item 27's underlying mechanism — `reverseEditableWaypoints`, `suggestReversedRouteName`, the `<acn:planning>` GPX round-trip, the closed-loop/20-waypoint-cap/exact-provenance behaviour it proved — remains valid, completed history and is reused, not reimplemented; only _where the rider triggers reversal_ changed. See item 27 for the full historical implementation record; it is not restated here.
    - New workflow: open a saved/imported route → `Edit copy` (renamed from `Edit copy in Planning`; there is no competing edit destination in this PWA, so the shorter name is unambiguous — the confirmation dialog copy still explains that it opens/replaces the Planning draft) → in Planning, edit waypoints normally, or reverse the current draft via a new `Reverse route` action inside Planning itself, in the existing `role="group" aria-label="Waypoint actions"` row directly after `Return to start` and before the conditional `Add to end`. Riding's pre-ride panel's own former `Reverse route` button, `REVERSE_ROUTE_CONFIG`, and the whole `CopyOperationKind`/`CopyOperationConfig` parameterised-guard machinery that let the two pre-ride actions share one implementation are all deleted from `RidingScreen.tsx` — reversal no longer has a pre-ride entry point at all, and with only one copy action left (`performEditCopy`/`handleEditCopyClick`/`handleEditCopyCancel`), the kind-parameterised guard collapsed to a single `isEditCopyActionPendingRef`/`isEditCopyConfirmOpen`/`isEditCopyInFlight`/`editCopyError` set — a dead two-operation architecture was not kept merely to shrink the diff.
    - **Resolved undo/redo architecture (was the item's own "flag, not yet resolved" note; now settled).** `src/ui/planning/waypointHistory.ts`'s `WaypointHistoryState.past`/`present`/`future` were widened from bare `readonly Waypoint[]` entries to a small `WaypointDraftSnapshot { waypoints: readonly Waypoint[]; routeName: string }` — option (a) from the item's own two candidates, chosen over a second, reversal-scoped coupled mechanism because anything that must be undoable has to live in the one reducer that owns Undo/Redo; a second mechanism would itself be a second, parallel source of truth for "what does Undo do," the exact anti-pattern the item's own note warned against. Two new `WaypointAction` variants: `{type: "rename"; routeName: string}` mutates `present.routeName` only and **never** pushes a history entry (mirrors `select`'s existing "not every action needs to be undoable" precedent) — this is the literal mechanism keeping ordinary route-name _typing_ out of undo/redo, since the route-name `<input>`'s `onChange` now dispatches `rename` via plain `dispatch`, deliberately not the `dispatchWaypointAction` wrapper (typing a name must not clear an active warning/route-feature selection, unlike a genuine waypoint edit); and `{type: "reverse"}`, a normal history-creating action (like `append`) that pushes the current snapshot onto `past` and produces one new snapshot with both fields changed together — waypoints via a private `reverseDraftWaypoints` (reuses `reverseEditableWaypoints` for the coordinate-level reversal, reversing ids in lockstep via a plain array `.reverse()` rather than minting fresh ones with `createWaypointId`, since these are pre-existing, possibly-referenced live waypoints, not new placements) and the name via `suggestReversedRouteName`, guarded by a blank/whitespace-only check (`state.present.routeName.trim().length === 0` leaves the name exactly as-is — this guard lives in the reducer's `reverse` case, not inside `suggestReversedRouteName` itself, whose pure "no trimming, appends verbatim" contract is unchanged and still the caller's responsibility to special-case). `reset` gained a required `routeName` field (used by the hydration restore branch, the post-Save reset and Clear draft's own reset, all merged from two separate calls — `dispatchWaypointAction({type:"reset", waypoints:[]})` + `setRouteName(...)` — into one atomic dispatch). Every pre-existing action (`append`/`insertAfter`/`move`/`reorder`/`delete`/`returnToStart`) now carries `state.present.routeName` forward unchanged into its new snapshot — the mechanical but exhaustive part of the diff — so ordinary Undo/Redo never touches the name unexpectedly. `PlanningScreen.tsx`'s previously-separate `const [routeName, setRouteName] = useState("Planned route")` is deleted entirely; every read site became `state.present.routeName`/`state.present.waypoints` (autosave persist body and its dependency array, `waypointsRef`, `selectedIndex`, `waypointRoles`, `canReturnToStart`, `handleSave`/`handleExport`'s `buildPlanningProvenance` call and trimmed-name logic, `planningOverlay`, `describeCrosshairAction`, the Calculate button's `disabled` check, `WaypointList`'s props). Confirmed by construction, not just asserted: a `rename` reuses the same `present.waypoints` array reference (only `routeName` changes), so typing a name never retriggers `usePlanningRoute`'s separate recalculation-debounce effect, whose dependency is `waypoints` specifically — while the autosave-persist effect still correctly fires on a rename, since it depends on the whole `state.present` object, which does get a new reference on every `rename` (the object spread copies the array reference, not the array's own identity-triggering role).
    - **Correctness trace, verified against the actual reducer** (not merely asserted): (1) reversal → Undo restores the pre-reversal `{waypoints, name}` snapshot verbatim, since both were captured together in one object when `reverse` pushed onto `past`; (2) reversal → an ordinary edit (e.g. append) → Undo → Undo: the edit's own push carries the reversed name forward unchanged, so the first Undo reverts only the edit and keeps the reversed name, and the second Undo crosses the reversal boundary, restoring the original order and name together; (3) reversal → Undo → a new action clears `future` exactly as every other history-creating action already does. All three traces are proven directly by dedicated reducer-level tests in `waypointHistory.test.ts`, not deferred to component-level coverage alone.
    - **Explicit Calculate required after reversal — zero code changes to `usePlanningRoute.ts`.** `handleReverseRoute` calls `routing.reset()` (item 37's existing function) strictly after `dispatchWaypointAction({type:"reverse"})`, mirroring `handleClearDraftConfirm`'s own established ordering. Verified precisely why this suffices: `reset()` synchronously aborts any in-flight request, bumps the request-sequence ref, clears any scheduled debounce timer, and sets `routedResult` to `null`; a _separate_ effect (declared earlier in the file, keyed on `[routedResult]`) then sets `hasRoutedResultRef.current = false`; because both the dispatch and `reset()` run synchronously in one event handler (one React batch, one commit) and effects fire in declaration order per commit, the debounced recalculation-scheduling effect's own `if (!hasRoutedResultRef.current) return;` guard is already correct by the time it next runs — no new provider request is ever scheduled, satisfying "zero requests, including past the normal ~900ms debounce" with no changes to the hook itself. `isStale`'s own order-sensitive fingerprint check (unchanged) already made Save/Export unavailable the instant waypoint order changed, but only `reset()` stops the _automatic recalculation_ from firing — the two mechanisms serve genuinely different purposes here. After an explicit Calculate, reversed leg requests fall out of the existing per-leg resolution machinery for free (`buildRouteLegKey` is already directional over each leg's own endpoints) — no leg-cache or routing-adapter change was needed.
    - **`editCopyMeta`/`editCopyOperation` — deliberately left completely untouched by reversal**, confirmed against the full audit the item required. `editCopyMeta` describes _seed provenance_ ("this draft was created via Edit copy, from source route X"), not live edit history; `handleReverseRoute` never calls `setEditCopyMeta`. Consequences, all verified: `describeEditCopyNotice`, `storage/mapping.ts`'s `EditCopyOperation`/`isEditCopyOperation`, and `storage/db.ts`'s `editCopyOperation?: string` field all stay byte-identical — no IndexedDB schema bump, since this is a pure in-memory workflow change over already-additive optional fields. Since the pre-ride `REVERSE_ROUTE_CONFIG` (the sole writer of `operation: "reverse"`) is deleted, `"reverse"` becomes a **frozen, read-only legacy value** for pre-existing v0.3.17–v0.3.28 draft rows only; `fromStoredPlanningDraft`'s existing `?? "forward"` default already handles this correctly with no change. A freshly Edit-copied-then-Reversed-in-Planning draft therefore shows the **unchanged forward notice** ("Editable copy created from the route's original planning waypoints..." / "...estimated...") rather than a reverse-specific one — a deliberate behavioural difference from item 27 (whose reversal only ever happened at seed time, so its own dedicated reverse notice made sense then); this is not a contradiction, since the notice narrates how the draft was _seeded_, a historical fact unaffected by later edits, exactly as it already tolerates an ordinary append/delete without updating. `buildPlanningProvenance` already stamped from _live_ `state.present.waypoints` at Save/Export time, so a Save after Calculate on a reversed draft naturally produces `planningProvenance` with the reversed order, recovered exactly via the existing, unmodified `<acn:planning>` GPX extension — no new route/GPX field. Pre-existing tests that seed `editCopyOperation: "reverse"` directly via a mocked/raw stored draft row (`PlanningScreen.test.tsx`, `PlanningScreen.draftHydration.test.tsx`, `mapping.test.ts`) remain valid, now explicitly documented as legacy-read-compatibility coverage rather than a reachable current UI path.
    - Must-preserve behaviour, all confirmed unchanged: closed-loop reversal semantics (`[A,B,C,A]` → `[A,C,B,A]`, value-equal not reference-equal start/finish — proven directly by a dedicated reducer test and an e2e test); the existing 20-waypoint derivation cap (reversal only reorders an already-resolved, already-capped waypoint list from `resolveEditableWaypoints`/`deriveWaypointsFromRoute` at Edit-copy seed time — it never re-derives, so a reversed derived draft cannot exceed the cap merely because Reverse was pressed); exact `planningProvenance`-based recovery vs. geometry-derived fallback (both origins tested); the source saved/imported route is never mutated (proven by reopening it independently after saving a reversed copy). No confirmation dialog for the in-Planning Reverse route action (local + fully undoable) and no re-entrancy guard beyond its own `disabled` prop — deliberately matching Undo/Redo/Return to start's own lack of one; a rapid double-click simply double-reverses, harmlessly and fully undoably.
    - **Testing.** Pure/reducer: `waypointHistory.test.ts` gained `describe("rename", ...)` (no history entry, same `waypoints` array reference preserved, doesn't disturb selection) and `describe("reverse", ...)` (2/3-waypoint and closed-loop order reversal, ids reversed in lockstep not re-minted, named/blank/whitespace-only/already-suffixed name handling, exactly one history entry pushed with `future` cleared, selection cleared, no-op below 2 waypoints, and the three correctness-trace scenarios above at the reducer level) — every pre-existing append/insertAfter/move/reorder/delete/returnToStart/select/undo/redo/reset test was mechanically adapted to the new snapshot shape with no semantic change. `routeNaming.test.ts` gained one test proving `suggestReversedRouteName("")` still returns `" (reversed)"` verbatim — the pure contract is unchanged; the blank-draft guard lives one level up. Component: a new `src/ui/planning/PlanningScreen.reverseRoute.test.tsx` (mirroring `PlanningScreen.clearDraft.test.tsx`'s mocked-repository/fake-timer/controlled-promise harness, per this file's own established per-concern-split convention) covers disabled-below-2/enabled-at-2+, positioning inside the Waypoint-actions group, order+name reversal for named/blank/already-suffixed drafts persisted together via autosave, closed-loop reversal, clearing an active selection and a pending Move/Insert-after relocation, zero provider requests before Calculate (including past the debounce and across Undo/Redo of the reversal itself), `canSaveOrExportPlan` becoming `false` immediately after reversal with an explicit Calculate re-enabling Save/Export, a stale in-flight calculation result resolved after reversal failing to reappear, the notice staying unchanged for an Edit-copy-seeded draft, identical behaviour for a hand-planned draft with no `editCopyMeta`, and a fresh mount restoring a persisted reversed draft from storage. `RidingScreen.test.tsx`'s `describe("Edit copy in Planning", ...)` was renamed to `describe("Edit copy", ...)`, its shared-hint test rewritten to single-button framing, the entire `describe("Reverse route", ...)` block (6 tests) and two of the three "share a single in-flight guard" cross-kind tests deleted (no surviving equivalent with one action), the third (`"rapid double-click on Reverse route alone..."`) ported into the renamed `Edit copy` block retargeted at the sole remaining button, and one new test added positively asserting no `Reverse route` button exists in Riding at all. Browser: `e2e/reverseRoute.spec.ts` was reworked in place (not deleted and recreated) around a new `editCopyThenReverseInPlanning` helper (Edit copy, then press Planning's own Reverse route button), covering all ten required scenarios across 7 tests — zero requests until Calculate including past the debounce, atomic Undo/Redo with zero provider contact; Calculate sends the reversed leg coordinates and Save creates an independent route leaving the original unchanged and reopenable; the exact-provenance GPX export/offline-reimport/Edit-copy round trip; a derived arbitrary GPX capped at 20 waypoints; closed-loop reversal with the exact leg-by-leg reversed-and-reordered proof item 27 already established; reload recovering a reversed draft with zero automatic routing requests; and a phone-viewport (390×844) test proving the button is a real ≥44×44px touch target with no horizontal overflow. The old pre-ride confirmation-dialog test was deleted outright (no confirmation for the in-Planning action); `EXACT_REVERSE_NOTICE`/`DERIVED_REVERSE_NOTICE` were replaced with the unchanged forward-notice constants; the file's own camera-fit/marker-containment/manual-pan-survival proof was deliberately not re-added, since it exercises the Edit-copy seeding step alone (unchanged by this item) and is already fully proven by `editRouteAsPlanningCopy.spec.ts`. `e2e/editRouteAsPlanningCopy.spec.ts` and `e2e/clearPlanningDraft.spec.ts` had every `"Edit copy in Planning"` button-name query and rationale comment updated to `"Edit copy"`.
    - Files changed: `src/ui/planning/waypointHistory.ts` (+`.test.ts`), `src/ui/planning/PlanningScreen.tsx`, `src/domain/routeNaming.ts` (+`.test.ts`, doc-comment only), `src/ui/riding/RidingScreen.tsx` (+`.test.tsx`), `src/domain/editableWaypoints.ts`/`src/storage/mapping.ts`/`src/storage/db.ts` (doc-comment-only "Edit copy" renames), `e2e/reverseRoute.spec.ts`, `e2e/editRouteAsPlanningCopy.spec.ts`, `e2e/clearPlanningDraft.spec.ts`; new: `src/ui/planning/PlanningScreen.reverseRoute.test.tsx`.

### Map zoom-dependent route and waypoint presentation

39. **Low-zoom surface-warning width correction — done**
    - Direct follow-up to item 23 (Low-zoom route-colour and waypoint-marker legibility), which fixed ascent/descent overlay and neutral-base widths but left warning-layer widths unaddressed. No change to warning detection, categories, colours, dash patterns, selection semantics, details panels, or warning geometry (`src/map/warningLayer.ts` is untouched).
    - **Confirmed root cause**, and one correction to this item's own prior text: `routeWidthPolicy.ts`'s two zoom-recession families resolve identically at close zoom (15) but diverge below it — `recedingWidthStops` (multipliers 0.4 at overview zoom 6, 0.65 at regional zoom 11) recedes markedly faster than `legibleWidthStops` (0.6 at overview, 0.8 at regional). `MapView.tsx` applied `legibleWidthStops` — not `recedingWidthStops` as this item's own pre-implementation note incorrectly claimed — to the neutral base/casing (`acn-route-remaining-line`/`acn-route-completed-line`), the selected route-feature halo, _and_ every warning-related layer (`WARNING_SELECTED_PAINT`, width 13; each `WARNING_CATEGORY_PAINT` entry, widths 8–10). Warnings sharing the neutral base's gentle recession curve, rather than the faster-receding curve item 23 already gave the climb/descent colour overlays, is exactly why warnings remained visually dominant at low zoom in a way ascent/descent overlays no longer were since item 23.
    - **Chosen policy**: a third recession family, `warningWidthStops(closeWidthPx)` (`routeWidthPolicy.ts`), built via the existing `buildStops` helper with `WARNING_MULTIPLIERS = { overview: 0.45, regional: 0.6 }` — a distinct, warning-specific family, not a reuse of either existing one, since a direct swap onto `recedingWidthStops` would have narrowed the selected-warning halo below the selected route-feature halo at overview zoom (`13 × 0.4 = 5.2` vs `9 × 0.6 = 5.4`), inverting item 23's own established nested-ring priority. The chosen multipliers preserve every required inequality at every zoom stop:
      | width (px)                           | overview (×0.45) | regional (×0.6) | close |
      | ------------------------------------ | ---------------- | --------------- | ----- |
      | 8 (unknown-surface)                  | 3.6              | 4.8             | 8     |
      | 9 (other/ferry/questionable-surface) | 4.05             | 5.4             | 9     |
      | 10 (unsuitable-surface/obstacle)     | 4.5              | 6.0             | 10    |
      | 13 (selected warning)                | 5.85             | 7.8             | 13    |
      - Strictly narrower than `legibleWidthStops` at overview/regional, exactly equal at close, for every warning width — a warning now recedes rather than dominating a full-route overview.
      - Every category width stays wider than `recedingWidthStops(5)` (the climb/descent macro/micro overlay centre: 2/3.25/5) at every zoom, even the narrowest category (3.6 > 2, 4.8 > 3.25 at overview/regional) — a warning still visually outranks a climb/descent it overlaps.
      - Every category width stays wider than `legibleWidthStops(5)` (the neutral route base: 3/4/5) at every zoom, even the narrowest category (3.6 > 3, 4.8 > 4, 8 > 5).
      - `warningWidthStops(13)` (selected warning) stays wider than `legibleWidthStops(9)` (selected route-feature halo: 5.4/7.2/9) at every stop (5.85 > 5.4, 7.8 > 7.2, 13 > 9) — a selected warning still outranks a selected climb/descent.
      - `warningWidthStops(13)` stays wider than every warning category width at every stop, and proportional ordering within the warning family is preserved throughout — both hold by construction, since every warning width shares one multiplier applied to a strictly-ordered set of close-widths.
      - Treat these as first-pass, reasoned starting values pending real-device acceptance, mirroring this file's own established humility for `routeWidthPolicy.ts`'s existing constants.
    - **Applied consistently, with no Planning/Riding mode branch**: `WARNING_CATEGORY_PAINT`'s six entries and `WARNING_SELECTED_PAINT` now use `warningWidthStops` instead of `legibleWidthStops`; the neutral base, selected route-feature halo, climb/descent macro/micro overlays and Planning-preview layer are all unchanged. `RidingScreen.tsx` never passes a `warningOverlay` prop to `MapView` — warnings are a Planning-only UI/data feature (see "## Planning behaviour" above); Riding's pre-ride overview and active riding render the warning _layers_ unconditionally (as before) but their GeoJSON sources stay empty there, by existing, correct design, unchanged by this item. "Applied consistently" therefore means the shared paint constants are installed identically for every `MapView` caller by construction, proven directly by a new Planning/Riding parity test covering all six warning category layers.
    - **Files changed**: `src/map/routeWidthPolicy.ts` (new `WARNING_MULTIPLIERS`/`warningWidthStops`, updated doc comments), `src/map/MapView.tsx` (seven `legibleWidthStops` → `warningWidthStops` call sites, updated doc comments including the nested-ring rationale comment inside `addRouteAndPositionLayers`). No change to `src/map/warningLayer.ts`, `src/map/mapAdapter.ts`, or warning detection/normalisation code.
    - **Tests**: `routeWidthPolicy.test.ts` gained a `describe("warningWidthStops", ...)` block (9 tests: close-zoom-unchanged, ascending zoom stops, monotonicity, strictly-narrower-than-legible, wider-than-gradient-centre, wider-than-neutral-base, selected-warning-wider-than-selected-feature, selected-warning-wider-than-every-category, proportional ordering). `MapView.test.tsx`: the existing exhaustive per-layer paint test now expects `warningWidthStops` for the seven warning-related layers; a new test proves the real installed paint hierarchy (warning casings wider than the neutral base and macro overlay at overview/regional; the selected-warning halo wider than the selected route-feature halo at every stop); the Planning/Riding parity test's layer list was extended to all six warning category layers; a new "zoom-invariant warning source data" test (mirroring item 23's own pattern) proves a camera-only zoom change never touches warning GeoJSON source data; the fallback-style test gained assertions that the local fallback style installs the same `warningWidthStops` paints; a new test proves the full `WARNING_CATEGORIES_IN_PAINT_ORDER` add-order sequence, which no prior test covered exhaustively. `e2e/lowZoomLegibility.spec.ts` gained a `MOCK_WARNING_ORS_RESPONSE` fixture (surface codes 8 "Compacted gravel"/`questionable-surface` and 12 "Ground or mud"/`unsuitable-surface`, verified against `src/routing/surfaceCodes.ts`'s real code table) and three new tests: warning count/category/selection/details surviving a Planning zoom round trip; the same route surviving Save → pre-ride overview → Start riding across further zoom round trips (with an inline comment recording the Riding-has-no-warning-UI scope note above); and the warning staying selected and the route staying rendered under the local fallback style across a zoom round trip.
    - **Verification**: focused `npx vitest run src/map/routeWidthPolicy.test.ts src/map/MapView.test.tsx src/map/warningLayer.test.ts` (165/165 passed) and `npx playwright test e2e/lowZoomLegibility.spec.ts` (6/6 passed), with the three new e2e tests additionally repeated 5× under `--workers=1` (15/15 passed, no flakiness). Full gate (`npm run format`, `npm run lint`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run build`, `npm test`, `npm run e2e`, `npm run format:check` run last) — see this file's own version-history discipline; results recorded at the time of the commit that shipped this item.

Physical Android verification has not happened — do not treat this item as Android-verified until that check has actually been done.

### Ride lifecycle

40. **Riding information and action proximity — done**
    - Pre-ride: `Recognised climbs` (item 13's `RidingClimbSelector`) now sits inside the Route profile card rather than as a separate top-level block. `RidingScreen.tsx`'s render tree previously alternated a single ternary slot (directly after the map, before the profile panel) between `RidingClimbSelector` (idle) and the active End-ride row (non-idle); that ternary is deleted entirely, and both branches relocate. The selector is now inserted, gated identically (`nav.geolocationStatus === "idle"`), inside the existing IIFE-returned Fragment within `.ride-elevation-section`/`.ride-profile-panel`, directly between the already-adjacent `<GradientColoursDisclosure .../>` and `<RouteFeatureDetailsPanel .../>` — a true zero-gap insertion, since nothing sat between those two before. Resulting order: `<h1>` route name → `<h2>Route profile</h2>` → elevation-window buttons → main elevation chart → `GradientColoursDisclosure` → `RidingClimbSelector`'s own `<h2>Recognised climbs</h2>` section → `RouteFeatureDetailsPanel` (selected climb's `<h3>` heading, detail chart and facts, when a feature is selected — DOM-adjacent to the selector by construction) → `GradientSegmentDetailsPanel`. `RidingClimbSelector.tsx`'s own wrapper `className` changed from `"panel stack"` to `"stack"` (both the empty-climbs and populated branches) — dropping its bordered/shadowed `.panel` box so it renders as a plain embedded subsection, consistent with its new siblings inside `.ride-elevation-section` (`RouteFeatureDetailsPanel`/`GradientColoursDisclosure`/`GradientSegmentDetailsPanel` all have zero dedicated CSS and were already unboxed — confirmed by grep before making the change); no new CSS rule was added anywhere. Heading hierarchy was checked, not just assumed: h1 → h2 "Route profile" → h2 "Recognised climbs" (two sibling h2s, not a skipped level) → h3 (`RouteFeatureDetailsPanel`'s heading) → h3 (`GradientSegmentDetailsPanel`'s, if rendered). `<section aria-label="Recognised climbs">` becomes a region landmark nested inside two plain, unlabelled `<div>`s inside the "Riding" region — exactly the same nesting depth `RouteFeatureDetailsPanel`/`GradientSegmentDetailsPanel` already had before this slice, not a new pattern. React reconciliation was confirmed safe rather than assumed: the new slot is a static JSX child of a Fragment (not a `.map()`-produced array) occupying one fixed index on every render, so toggling it between an element and `null` only ever mounts/unmounts that one position — `RouteFeatureDetailsPanel`/`GradientSegmentDetailsPanel` keep their own component identity across the idle→watching transition regardless, which is why the pre-existing `.ride-elevation-section`-same-DOM-node identity test needed no change. No climb-detection, progress-calculation, elevation-window, or feature-selection logic changed anywhere — `climbs`, `selectedFeature`, `selectRouteFeature`, `handleClearRouteFeatureSelection` are the same values passed to the same props, only their JSX position moved.
    - Active riding: the `End ride` trigger moved from below the map (the same now-deleted ternary slot above) to the top of the screen. The pre-existing `.ride-start-panel` ternary (`nav.geolocationStatus === "idle" ? <div className="panel stack ride-start-panel">...</div> : null`) had its `else` branch changed from `null` to the byte-identical active End-ride row (`<div className="ride-end-ride-row">`, same `ref={endRideTriggerRef}`, same `onClick={handleEndRideClick}`, same `disabled={activeFinalizeSource !== null}`, same conditional error paragraph) that previously lived in the deleted ternary. The shared `<ConfirmDialog open={isEndRideConfirmOpen} title="End this ride?" .../>` was not moved at all — it was already the very next unconditional sibling after that ternary, so it now automatically sits immediately after whichever branch renders (pre-ride: after `.ride-start-panel`; active: after the new `.ride-end-ride-row`), satisfying "the confirmation immediately follows the trigger area" with zero dialog changes. Resulting active-riding order: `RidingWakeLockControl` (unchanged, still first) → route header → offline notice → `.ride-end-ride-row` → `ConfirmDialog` → geolocation-error banner → waiting-for-fix → `RidingStatusStrip` → `RidingNextManoeuvrePanel` → `RidingRouteCompletionPanel` → paused-toast → map → elevation wrapper (no End-ride row there any more). `performFinalizeRide`, `handleEndRideClick`/`handleEndRideCancel`, the focus-restoration `useEffect`, `isFinalizeActionPendingRef`, `activeFinalizeSource`/`finalizeError`, the pre-ride Resume-riding End-ride trigger inside `.ride-start-panel`, and `RidingRouteCompletionPanel`/Finish ride's own position are all byte-identical — only the active trigger's JSX position moved. `endRideTriggerRef`'s doc comment was updated to describe the new active-tracking slot location ("near the top of the screen, directly after the offline notice and before the map") rather than "below the map"; its "exactly one is ever mounted at a time" guarantee is unchanged and still true.
    - Testing: new tests in `RidingScreen.test.tsx` (mirroring the file's own established `compareDocumentPosition`/`getByRole("region", ...)` conventions, e.g. its existing "Gradient segment details"/"Climb progress" region queries and its wake-lock-before-h1 `compareDocumentPosition` test) prove: `.ride-profile-panel` contains the `Recognised climbs` region and the `Route profile` heading precedes it in DOM order; once a climb is selected, the `Recognised climbs` region's `nextElementSibling` is the `Route feature details` region (zero-gap adjacency); the active End-ride row precedes both the next-manoeuvre panel and the map container in DOM order; the opened confirmation dialog is the End-ride row's `nextElementSibling`; a clean pre-ride state shows no End-ride button; and a resumable pre-ride state (seeded via `setActiveRideState`) still shows `Resume riding` and an End-ride button contained within `.ride-start-panel`, with no separate active-tracking row present. `e2e/gradientColouring.spec.ts`'s existing 390 px-viewport test ("presents the pre-ride route briefing in reading order...") was extended in place, not duplicated: its `h1, h2` heading-text-order assertion changed from `["gradient-route", "Recognised climbs", "Route profile"]` to `["gradient-route", "Route profile", "Recognised climbs"]`; new assertions prove the climb-selector region is contained in `.ride-profile-panel`, is immediately followed by the `Route feature details` region once a climb is selected, and (after starting the ride) that `.ride-end-ride-row` precedes `[data-testid="map-container"]` in DOM order, that its opened confirmation is its `nextElementSibling`, and that no horizontal overflow appears at any point including with the dialog open. `RidingScreen.finishEndRide.test.tsx`, `RidingScreen.completionArming.test.tsx`, `RidingClimbSelector.test.tsx`, and `e2e/ridingFinishAndEnd.spec.ts` were re-run unmodified and confirmed passing — all were already role/name/text/state-based with zero DOM-order or class-position assertions this slice would disturb.
    - Files changed: `src/ui/riding/RidingClimbSelector.tsx`, `src/ui/riding/RidingScreen.tsx`; tests: `src/ui/riding/RidingScreen.test.tsx`, `e2e/gradientColouring.spec.ts`. No routing, camera, wake-lock, geolocation, storage, GPX, CSS, or completion-arming behaviour changed anywhere — confirmed by full-suite verification, not merely by inspection. Version bumped to 0.3.31 (`package.json` and `package-lock.json`'s two matching `version` fields, hand-patched, following the established precedent). Verification: `npm run format`, `npm run lint`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run build`, `npm test` (2287/2287 across 122 files), `npm run e2e` (115/115 across both the `chromium` and `android-chrome` projects), `npm run format:check` run last, immediately before committing — all pass.
    - Real-device iPhone verification is now confirmed (17 August 2026): the climb-selector/route-profile proximity and the top-of-screen End-ride confirmation flow read correctly on the installed iPhone Home Screen PWA. Closed-loop completion and `Finish ride` specifically are also now confirmed, via a fresh field test of the build carrying item 46's fix (see item 46's own entry and "Manual acceptance status" above). Physical Android verification has not happened at all — do not treat any part of this item as Android-verified until that check has actually been done.

41. **Ride launcher, explicit session recovery and post-finalisation route clearing — done**
    - Follow-up to item 6 (the existing "Ride, no selected route" empty screen offering `Choose a route`) and item 29 (End/Finish ride). Foundation for item 42 (free roam), which remains unimplemented and unscoped beyond this slice's own storage discriminant.
    - **Confirmed root cause** matched this item's own original description exactly: `App.tsx`'s `screen`/`selectedRoute` were plain, unpersisted `useState`, with no mount-time read of any persisted session state — `getActiveRideState()` was called only inside `useRideNavigation.ts`'s own mount-restore effect (reached only once `RidingScreen` was already mounted with a `selectedRoute`) and read-only in `DiagnosticsScreen.tsx`. A reload always returned to the Routes screen; "Resume riding" only became reachable once the rider manually reopened the _same_ route by name. Nothing cleared `App.tsx`'s own `selectedRoute` after a successful End/Finish ride, so the rider stayed looking at that same route's freshly-reset pre-ride panel rather than a genuinely empty state.
    - **Storage discriminant.** `src/storage/db.ts` gained `export type RideSessionKind = "route"` (a storage-only discriminant, placed and doc-commented alongside the file's existing `StoredElevationViewMode` precedent, not a domain-level type — it has no meaning outside a stored row) and a new optional `StoredRideState.kind?: string` field — deliberately a **plain string, not the narrower `RideSessionKind` union**, at this storage boundary, mirroring `editCopyOperation`/`profile`'s own established "Dexie never validates stored data" convention on `StoredPlanningDraft`, rather than trusting an arbitrary IndexedDB value at the type level. Optional/non-indexed, following every prior field added to this table since v1 — no Dexie `version()` bump. `src/storage/mapping.ts` gained `isRideSessionKind` (mirrors `isEditCopyOperation`'s exact `value === "..."` shape) and exported `resolveStoredRideSessionKind(stored): RideSessionKind | "unsupported"`. `toStoredRideState(...)` now hardcodes `kind: "route"` in its returned object literal — no new parameter, since it remains the sole writer and only ever writes route sessions today; its signature and its one call site (the persistence effect in `useRideNavigation.ts`) are byte-identical.
    - **A deliberate deviation from this file's own usual corrupt-value convention.** Every other closed-string-union field on this table (`editCopyOperation`, `elevationViewMode`'s tag, `sortOrder`) defaults a corrupt or unrecognised value to a concrete fallback via `isX(value) ? value : default`. `resolveStoredRideSessionKind` does not: `undefined` (a legacy row) resolves to `"route"` — the only kind this table has ever held, so this is the correct legacy default — but a **present-but-unrecognised** value (a future kind from a newer app version, or a corrupted row) resolves to a distinct `"unsupported"` outcome, never silently to `"route"`. Reasoning: unlike `editCopyOperation` (a closed, cosmetic, two-value field whose absence has exactly one proven past meaning and never gates a differently-shaped read), `kind` is a discriminant this project already knows it will widen (item 42) to a variant carrying different, route-incompatible fields (no meaningful `routeId`). Defaulting an unrecognised value to `"route"` here would let the launcher confidently call `getRoute(stored.routeId)` on a row whose actual shape it cannot interpret — wrong in a way that actively misleads the rider, not merely cosmetically wrong. The launcher treats `"unsupported"` exactly like a route that no longer resolves: explain that the session can't be recovered by this version of the app, offer only a confirmed discard, never a Resume action.
    - **The launcher.** A new, self-contained `src/ui/riding/RidingLauncher.tsx` replaces the previous inline empty-state `<section>` in `App.tsx` — it imports `getActiveRideState`/`getRoute`/`clearActiveRideState`/`resolveStoredRideSessionKind` directly (no DI props for storage functions, matching every sibling screen's convention; its own tests use real Dexie/fake-indexeddb plus targeted `vi.spyOn` for failure injection, never `vi.mock`). It owns its own hydration state machine, mirroring `PlanningScreen.tsx`'s established `"loading" | "ready" | "failed"` + generation-ref + retry-token pattern exactly, but as a two-step async function inside the effect (read the ride-state row, then conditionally read the route it references) rather than a `.then()` chain, since the second read depends on the first's result. Never renders the "no session" state while the read is merely unresolved (a restrained `role="status"` "Checking for an unfinished ride…" message covers that gap); a genuine read failure shows an accessible `role="alert"` message plus Retry, never falsely "no session" and never any deletion.
    - **Three resolved states**, `type RidingLauncherSessionState = { status: "none" } | { status: "resumable"; route: PlannedRoute } | { status: "unresumable"; reason: "route-missing" | "unsupported-kind" }`: no active row shows the pre-existing, byte-identical "No route selected yet…" copy plus **Choose a route**; a resumable route session shows the route's name and a `.route-card-meta` distance/ascent line, plus **Resume route** (deliberately not "Resume riding" — that label is reserved for `RidingScreen`'s own pre-ride recovery panel, and a future free-roam session could also be "resumed") and a danger **End ride**; an unresumable session (route deleted, or an unrecognised/unsupported kind) explains the problem and offers only a danger **Discard unfinished ride**, never a Resume action. `Resume route` calls the new `onResumeRoute(route)` prop and never starts geolocation itself — `RidingScreen`'s own pre-ride panel still gates the GPS watch behind its own explicit "Resume riding" tap.
    - **End ride and Discard unfinished ride share one internal mechanism**, `performClearSession(action: "end-ride" | "discard-unfinished")`, parameterised exactly like `RidingScreen.tsx`'s own `performFinalizeRide(source)` via a small per-action copy lookup table (dialog title/message/confirm label/pending label/error message/log context) — since the two actions are mutually exclusive per resolved state, one shared `ConfirmDialog` instance suffices. It calls `clearActiveRideState()` **directly**, deliberately not via `useRideNavigation.finish()`: `finish()`'s only logic beyond the storage clear is resetting hook-local React state (GPS watch, camera, wake lock, completion tracker), none of which has any launcher analogue since `RidingScreen` was never mounted for an unresumed session — constructing `useRideNavigation(route, ...)` merely to call `.finish()` on it would be architecturally backwards and is outright impossible for the route-missing case (no `PlannedRoute` exists there at all). `clearActiveRideState()` itself remains the single function that ever touches `db.rideState.delete(...)` — both call sites reuse it, satisfying "one authoritative clear path" without a second raw Dexie access point. A synchronous `isClearActionPendingRef` guards rapid double submission, mirroring `isFinalizeActionPendingRef`. Cancel and Escape both preserve the row and restore focus to the trigger; a storage-clear failure preserves the row, shows an accessible retryable error, and restores focus via a `useEffect` keyed on the error's own object identity — not a direct `.focus()` call inside the `catch` block, since the trigger is still `disabled` in the DOM at that exact synchronous point (the same real, previously-documented disabled-focus hazard `RidingScreen.tsx`'s own `performFinalizeRide` already guards against).
    - **Finalisation integration.** `RidingScreenProps` gained `onRideFinalized?: () => void`, called as the last statement of `performFinalizeRide`'s try block — after `nav.finish()` has already cleared persisted state and this screen's own existing runtime cleanup (`camera.resetCamera()`, `completion.reset()`, `setReachedManoeuvreIndex(0)`, closing the confirmation dialog) has already applied, so it fires only once finalisation has fully, successfully completed. Wrapped in its own **nested** `try { onRideFinalized?.(); } catch (callbackError) { logError("riding-ride-finalized-callback", callbackError); }`, deliberately separate from the outer catch: by the time this line runs, storage is already cleared, so a throw here left in the outer try would show the existing "The ride could not be ended on this device. Try again." message — a lie, since finalisation already succeeded — while leaving it fully unguarded would surface as an unhandled promise rejection at the `void performFinalizeRide(...)` call site. The nested catch avoids both failure modes and still logs the bug via the existing diagnostics mechanism. `App.tsx` gained `handleResumeRoute(route)` (`setSelectedRoute(route); setScreen("riding"); notifyNewRideContent();` — deliberately **not** capturing `window.scrollY` into `routesScrollYRef` the way `handleOpenRoute` does, since the rider is already on the Ride screen, not Routes, when this fires, and capturing the launcher's own scroll offset here would wrongly overwrite the Routes-restore ref) and `handleRideFinalized()` (`setSelectedRoute(null); notifyNewRideContent();` — the sole point that unmounts `RidingScreen` and mounts `RidingLauncher` in its place; `screen` stays `"riding"` throughout, and the existing `useResetScrollForNewRideContent` token-bump mechanism is reused unchanged to land the launcher at the top of the document). `isRidingActive` reverts to `false` for free via `RidingScreen`'s own existing unmount-driven `onRidingActiveChange` cleanup the instant `selectedRoute` clears and React swaps the conditional's rendered element — no new wiring needed, confirmed by a dedicated App-level test asserting the header returns to sticky.
    - **Failure semantics, held throughout.** The success callback never fires on cancellation, Escape, a genuine storage failure, or a duplicate/re-entrant submission — each of those paths leaves the same route selected and the ride active/resumable, with all of `performFinalizeRide`'s existing focus-restoration/retry semantics untouched. An ordinary nav-tab change, PWA suspension, reload, transient GPS error, or "Try again" never clears `selectedRoute` or persisted state — only a genuinely successful End/Finish transition reaches `onRideFinalized`.
    - **Tests.** `src/storage/mapping.test.ts` (`toStoredRideState` always writes `kind: "route"`; `resolveStoredRideSessionKind` for absent/recognised/unrecognised values) and `src/storage/db.migration.test.ts` (a legacy `rideState` row without `kind` loads cleanly with no schema version bump; a row with `kind: "route"` round-trips) prove the storage layer. A new `src/ui/riding/RidingLauncher.tsx` + `RidingLauncher.test.tsx` (13 tests) cover every resolved state, the loading/failed hydration states, zero geolocation calls merely from rendering, Resume route's exact route-object callback, End-ride/Discard cancel/Escape/failure/retry, and the unsupported-kind path (seeded by writing a corrupt `kind` value directly, bypassing `toStoredRideState`, since no real writer ever produces one today). `src/ui/riding/RidingScreen.finishEndRide.test.tsx` gained a new `describe("RidingScreen onRideFinalized", ...)` block (6 tests): a controlled-promise proof that `onRideFinalized` never fires before `clearActiveRideState()` has actually resolved; successful End ride and Finish ride each calling it exactly once; cancellation/Escape never calling it; a storage failure never calling it followed by a successful retry calling it exactly once; a rapid double confirm calling it at most once; and a throwing `onRideFinalized` still reaching the clean "Start riding" state with no `finalizeError` shown, logged via `"riding-ride-finalized-callback"`. `src/App.test.tsx` gained a new `describe("App — Ride launcher session recovery", ...)` block (3 tests): a resumable session discovered purely from persisted storage (seeded directly, never via `selectedRoute`) driving the launcher and `Resume route` opening the existing recovery state; a successful End ride from the resumed state clearing `selectedRoute`, bumping the scroll-reset spy, and restoring the sticky header; and a storage-clear failure leaving `RidingScreen` still shown with the same route selected. Its pre-existing "shows the empty Ride state…" test was updated to `findByRole` for the now-genuinely-async "Choose a route" button.
    - **Browser coverage.** `e2e/ridingFinishAndEnd.spec.ts`'s two existing tests had their post-End/post-Finish assertions changed from "same route, clean pre-ride overview" to the empty launcher (`<h1>Ride</h1>`, `Choose a route` visible, `Resume route`/the route's own heading hidden) — the subsequent `waitForClearedRideState`/`page.reload()`/reopen-from-Routes assertions are unchanged and still prove the route survived; the first test's own title was updated to match. A new `e2e/ridingLauncher.spec.ts` (3 tests, `chromium` project only) proves: a real `page.reload()` exposes the persisted session in the launcher (navigated to directly via the "Ride" tab, never through Routes) with zero OpenRouteService requests throughout, and `Resume route` restores existing progress (`Resume riding`, not `Start riding`); the launcher can end an unfinished ride directly, without ever clicking `Resume route` or engaging GPS; and — reloading first, so `App.tsx`'s stale in-memory `selectedRoute` can't short-circuit the scenario — deleting the route via Route Library's own real Delete → confirm flow while a session references it makes the launcher explain the problem and offer only a confirmed `Discard unfinished ride`, with cancel preserving the row. `e2e/androidPersistenceAndOffline.spec.ts`'s own doc comment (which documented the pre-launcher "reload always returns to Routes" contract as apparently the only recovery path) gained one added sentence noting the launcher now offers a second, more direct recovery path via the "Ride" tab itself — a comment-only change; that file's own manual-reopen-via-Routes test needed no assertion changes and passes unmodified.
    - Files changed: `src/storage/db.ts`, `src/storage/mapping.ts`, `src/ui/riding/RidingLauncher.tsx` (new), `src/ui/riding/RidingScreen.tsx`, `src/App.tsx`, `src/ui/shared/useResetScrollForNewRideContent.ts` (doc comment only); tests: `src/storage/mapping.test.ts`, `src/storage/db.migration.test.ts`, `src/ui/riding/RidingLauncher.test.tsx` (new), `src/ui/riding/RidingScreen.finishEndRide.test.tsx`, `src/App.test.tsx`, `e2e/ridingFinishAndEnd.spec.ts`, `e2e/ridingLauncher.spec.ts` (new), `e2e/androidPersistenceAndOffline.spec.ts` (doc comment only).
    - Verification: `npm run format`, `npm run lint`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run build`, `npm test` — 2315/2315 across 123 files, `npm run e2e` — 118/118 across both the `chromium` (106) and `android-chrome` (12) projects, `npm run format:check` run last, immediately before committing — all pass. Version bumped to 0.3.32 (`package.json` and `package-lock.json`'s two matching `version` fields, hand-patched, following the established precedent).
    - Item 42 (route-less free roam) remains entirely unimplemented — no `Start free roam`/`Resume free roam`/free-roam camera/wake-lock/persistence exists anywhere; this slice only widened `StoredRideState` with the `kind` discriminant item 42 will need. Real-device iPhone verification is now confirmed (17 August 2026): the empty/resumable/missing-route launcher states and their End-ride paths all work correctly on the installed iPhone Home Screen PWA. The Finish-ride finalisation path specifically is also now confirmed, via a fresh field test of the build carrying item 46's fix (see item 46's own entry and "Manual acceptance status" above). Physical Android verification has not happened at all — do not treat any part of this item as Android-verified until that check has actually been done.

42. **Route-less free roam — done**
    - Implements the idle Ride launcher's second entry point: `Start free roam`/`Resume free roam`, alongside `Choose a route`/`Resume route`. Free roam shows the live GPS position on the ordinary map with camera follow (course-based bearing once reliable, north-up before that, retaining the last reliable bearing while stationary/course-unreliable) — no route line, no warnings, no elevation/manoeuvre/climb/completion UI, none of it mounted, not merely hidden. The same shared interaction language as route Riding: a top-of-screen `End ride` + confirmation, `Keep screen awake` when supported, North-up/Follow controls, waiting-for-fix/error/stale states with `Try again`, and manual-gesture-pauses-follow/Follow-resumes-it behaviour. No `Finish ride` (no destination).
    - **Storage: `StoredRideState` widened into a discriminated union**, exactly as item 41's own foundation anticipated — `StoredRouteRideState | StoredFreeRoamRideState` (`src/storage/db.ts`), both `kind`-tagged. `StoredRouteRideState.kind` is deliberately left as a plain `string | undefined` (not narrowed to the literal `"route"`), even now that the type is a union — `resolveStoredRideSessionKind` must still be able to resolve a corrupted/future value on a route-shaped row to `"unsupported"`, which a narrowed literal would make impossible to construct without an unsafe cast. `StoredFreeRoamRideState.kind` is a required literal `"free-roam"`, since no legacy free-roam row can exist. `StoredFreeRoamRideState` has no `routeId`/match/off-route/elevation/climb/completion fields at all — the type system, not just a runtime convention, is what stops free-roam code from ever touching route-progress state or calling `getRoute`. No Dexie `version()` bump: `stores()` only lists indexes, unaffected by a purely TypeScript-level union. Two new type guards in `src/storage/mapping.ts`, `isStoredRouteRideState`/`isStoredFreeRoamRideState` (both backed by `resolveStoredRideSessionKind`), are how every route-only or free-roam-only field access narrows before touching a `StoredRideState`-typed value — `useRideNavigation.ts`'s restore effect now narrows via `isStoredRouteRideState` before reading `stored.routeId`, and `DiagnosticsScreen.tsx`'s "Active route" field (a genuine existing call site the union broke, found only by re-running `tsc`, not anticipated in the original plan) resolves to `"Free roam"` for a free-roam row rather than failing to compile or silently showing "None". New mapping pair `toStoredFreeRoamState`/`fromStoredFreeRoamState`, mirroring `toStoredRideState`/`fromStoredRideState`'s exact defaulting conventions (`cameraMode ?? "overview"`, bearing/pitch `?? 0`, `wakeLockDesired ?? false`; `lastReliableBearingDegrees ?? null`, never a fabricated 0 — the camera hook itself decides the north-up fallback). No separate "initial state" helper: `RidingLauncher`'s persist-first write just calls `toStoredFreeRoamState` directly with an inline overview/null literal, matching this codebase's own established convention of each consumer owning its trivial default (see `useRideNavigation.ts`'s/`RidingScreen.tsx`'s own local `DEFAULT_CAMERA_STATE` constants).
    - A genuine TypeScript gotcha, found and fixed rather than merely avoided: `Partial<A | B>` does not distribute over a union — it only allows overriding keys common to both members. `rideStateRepository.test.ts`'s and (pre-existing, from item 41) `RidingLauncher.test.tsx`'s own `buildRideState(overrides: Partial<StoredRideState> = {})` test helpers were both retyped to `Partial<StoredRouteRideState>`. Three existing tests (`mapping.test.ts`, `RidingLauncher.test.tsx` ×2) had used `kind: "free-roam"` as their example of a _present-but-unrecognised_ kind value, written pre-emptively by item 41 before free-roam was real — these were changed to a definitely-fictional `"training-session"` placeholder, and genuine free-roam-recognised cases were added alongside them, so the "unsupported kind" path stays actually tested for the one value now most likely to occur in practice.
    - **Two new, deliberately separate hooks — not a `route: PlannedRoute | null` parameterisation of the existing ones.** `src/ui/riding/useFreeRoamNavigation.ts` copies `useRideNavigation.ts`'s watch-lifecycle machinery (generation token, `statusRef`, `isFinalizingRef`, `start`/`stop`, the visibilitychange/pageshow effect, the persistence-effect's guard structure) near-verbatim — confirmed, by direct re-reading, to have zero coupling to `route` there either; only route-matching (`processFix`), elevation and climb/completion state are genuinely route-shaped, none of which free roam has. State surface: `geolocationStatus`, `geolocationError`, `currentFix`, `isStale`, `wakeLockDesired`+setter, `start`, `finish`, `restoredCameraState`, `restoredLastReliableBearingDegrees` — no `coreState`/`matchedDistanceFromStartMetres`/`offRouteLevel`/elevation/climb fields carried as always-null placeholders. `useFreeRoamNavigation.finish()` reuses `clearActiveRideState()`, the same single storage-clear path `useRideNavigation.finish()` uses. `src/ui/riding/useFreeRoamCamera.ts` reuses the shared, pure `rideCamera.ts` reducer and `selectTravelBearingDegrees` bearing policy completely unchanged — confirmed line-by-line that feeding it `routeTangentBearingDegrees: null` and a constant `offRouteLevel: "on-route"` already produces exactly "GPS course when reliable, else retain the last stable bearing, else none" — with its own hook-local adapted copy of `useRideCamera.ts`'s private `hookReducer` (orchestration glue, not policy). The one genuine behavioural difference from route Riding: `persistableLastReliableBearingDegrees` is exposed unconditionally, regardless of camera mode (route Riding's `persistableCameraState` only ever carries a real bearing while `mode === "free"`, since a route tangent is always available to re-derive one on resume; free roam has no such fallback, so the last-commanded bearing must be persisted separately to frame a resumed-into-following session before the first fresh fix). The "avoid a world-view flash on a resumed-into-following session" requirement is met entirely inside `useFreeRoamCamera.ts` as a hook-local synthetic `cameraTarget` fallback (`initialFramingCommand`, active only while `currentFix && isStale && mode === "following"`, framing the restored fix at the restored last-reliable bearing, pitch 0, non-animated) layered on top of the reducer's own unmodified output — deliberately not a change to the shared reducer, since route Riding fills the identical gap via `MapView`'s separate overview-fit-to-route-bounds effect, which free roam has nothing to fit (`points=[]` always yields a null bounding box). Self-cancels on three independent paths (a real fresh-fix command landing, `isStale` flipping false, or the rider leaving "following" mode), proven directly in `useFreeRoamCamera.test.ts`.
    - **A real race condition found and fixed while writing the browser-level tests, not merely theorised.** `RidingLauncher`'s "Start free roam" always persists an initial row with `cameraMode: "overview"` before `FreeRoamScreen` ever mounts (there is nothing more meaningful to write before any fix has been accepted). `FreeRoamScreen`'s own mount effect calls `requestFollow()` synchronously in the same tick — but the storage read `restoredCameraState` depends on resolves asynchronously, so the resulting "restore" dispatch always arrives _after_ the follow request and, unconditionally, reset mode straight back to "overview" every single time a brand-new session started — silently undoing `requestFollow()`. First caught by `e2e/freeRoam.spec.ts`'s own "a position change moves the followed camera" test never reaching `FOLLOW_PITCH_DEGREES` (35°). Fixed in `useFreeRoamCamera.ts`'s restore-dispatch effect: an "overview" restored mode is now skipped entirely, never dispatched — sound because "overview" is `rideCameraReducer`'s own `INITIAL_RIDE_CAMERA_STATE` already (dispatching it is redundant at best), and because a genuine mid-session resume's persisted mode is always `"following"` or `"free"` by construction (a real `Resume free roam` only ever becomes reachable after the first fix has already been accepted and persisted, per `useFreeRoamNavigation`'s own persistence-effect guard). Pinned as a permanent regression test in `useFreeRoamCamera.test.ts` (`describe("an 'overview' restore never undoes an explicit requestFollow()")`), not just fixed and left to the e2e layer alone.
    - **`RidingLauncher.tsx`**: a third `RidingLauncherSessionState` status, `{status: "resumable-free-roam"}` (no payload — `FreeRoamScreen` re-reads storage itself, mirroring how `RidingScreen` already independently owns its own restore rather than the launcher passing state through); the hydration effect branches via `isStoredFreeRoamRideState`/`isStoredRouteRideState` (never raw `.kind` comparison) into free-roam-resumable / route-flow-as-before / unsupported, with **no `getRoute` call for a free-roam row**. A third `LauncherClearAction`, `"end-free-roam"`, with its own copy that never mentions a saved route ("Your free roam position and camera state will be cleared."), reusing `performClearSession`/`clearActiveRideState()` completely unchanged. `Start free roam` persists first (via `toStoredFreeRoamState`) inside its own async handler with the identical re-entrancy-guard/pending-state/accessible-error pattern `performClearSession` already established in this file; on success it calls the new `onOpenFreeRoam` prop, on failure it stays on the launcher with no watch ever started — the literal decision-#2 requirement. `Resume free roam` calls `onOpenFreeRoam` directly, no write needed. A new optional `clock?: Clock` prop (default `systemClock`) supplies the `startedAt` timestamp — no `clock` prop existed on this component before. A new optional `blockedRouteOpenReason?: "free-roam-unfinished" | "check-failed" | null` prop renders a `role="alert"` explanation near the top, independent of which session-state branch is active below it.
    - **`App.tsx`: an explicit `RidingContent` union — `{kind:"none"} | {kind:"route"; route: PlannedRoute} | {kind:"free-roam"}`** — replaces the previous nullable `selectedRoute`, so a route session and a free-roam session can never be conflated; free roam is never represented as a fake `PlannedRoute`. `handleOpenRoute`/`handleRouteSaved` (the two entry points that bypass the launcher entirely — a Route Library card click, a Planning save) became internally async, calling a new `checkFreeRoamConflict()` (reads `getActiveRideState()`, narrows via `isStoredFreeRoamRideState`; a storage-read failure resolves to `"check-failed"`, **failing closed** — never silently treated as "no conflict") before proceeding; on a conflict they redirect to the Ride screen with `blockedRouteOpenReason` set, never opening the route, and the row is left completely untouched. `handleResumeRoute` needs no such guard at all: since `rideState` is a singleton row, it can only ever fire from the launcher's own "Resume route" button, which can only render once the launcher's hydration already found a route row — meaning free roam can never be the unfinished session at that point, by construction. `RouteLibrary`/`PlanningScreen` needed zero changes — both handlers keep their existing external `(route: PlannedRoute) => void` signatures.
    - **The single most important design decision in this slice**, resolving a real tension between two of the product's own stated decisions rather than picking one side by accident: decision #1 (`Start free roam`/`Resume free roam` is _one_ deliberate action that both opens the screen and starts the watch) versus decision #4 (returning to Ride must require an explicit `Resume free roam` action, never a silent restart). Route Riding resolves an analogous tension with a **two-tap** pattern (`RidingScreen` always mounts idle, with its own in-screen "Resume riding" second tap, since `geolocationStatus` never survives an unmount). Copying that pattern for free roam would mean the very first "Start free roam" click lands on a _second_ internal "Start free roam" button before GPS actually starts — directly contradicting decision #1's one-tap wording, for no benefit (there is no "preview before riding" use case to justify it). Resolution: `FreeRoamScreen` has **no internal idle panel** — it calls `nav.start()`/`camera.requestFollow()` unconditionally in a mount effect, satisfying decision #1 literally. This is made safe against decision #4 by a new `App.tsx` `handleNavigate` wrapper around `MainNavigation`'s `onNavigate`: leaving the Ride screen while it was showing an active free-roam session resets the in-memory `ridingContent` pointer back to `{kind:"none"}` (never the persisted row — `FreeRoamScreen`'s own unmount already stops its GPS watch and releases its wake lock via ordinary cleanup effects). Returning to "Ride" therefore always re-renders `RidingLauncher` fresh, which re-hydrates from storage, finds the still-unfinished row, and requires a fresh, explicit `Resume free roam` tap — literally satisfying decision #4. Deliberately **not** applied when `ridingContent.kind === "route"` — route Riding's existing, tested, two-tap behaviour is completely unchanged, proven by a dedicated `App.test.tsx` case asserting the identical navigate-away-and-back sequence leaves an open route session's `RidingScreen` shown directly. This also incidentally simplifies the conflict-guard message placement: since `handleOpenRoute`/`handleRouteSaved` can only ever fire while `screen` is `"library"`/`"planning"` — which, given the reset above, is only reachable after `ridingContent` has already returned to `{kind:"none"}` if free roam had been showing — `blockedRouteOpenReason` only ever needs to be threaded to `RidingLauncher`, never `FreeRoamScreen`.
    - **`FreeRoamScreen.tsx`** (new, `src/ui/riding/`): a genuinely small, separate component, not a stripped-down `RidingScreen` — reuses that screen's already-proven pieces (the `cameraStateRef`/getter hook-bridge pattern, extended to bundle `cameraState` and `lastReliableBearingDegrees` into one combined getter since both flow from the same camera hook and are needed together on every persistence write; the `onRidingActiveChange` effect; the offline notice; top-of-screen `End ride` + `ConfirmDialog`, copy never mentioning a saved route; the geolocation-error banner + `Try again`; the waiting-for-fix status row; `.ride-map-container`'s `--active`/`--overview` toggle; North-up/Follow controls; `RidingWakeLockControl`, identically gated) but owns none of `RidingScreen`'s route-specific machinery. `MapView` needed **zero changes**: `points={[]}` plus every overlay prop omitted already yields exactly "position dot, no route/warnings/features/arrows/badges", confirmed directly against `MapView.tsx`'s actual effects rather than assumed, and already a well-tested existing configuration (Planning's own before-any-waypoint state). A new, small `FreeRoamStatusStrip.tsx` — not `RidingStatusStrip` (whose `offRouteLevel`/`distanceRemainingMetres` props are fundamentally route-shaped) — shows only GPS accuracy and fresh/stale, mirroring `RidingStatusStrip`'s own exact wording/role conventions for that one line. Exactly one `<h1 className="screen-title">Free roam</h1>`.
    - No routing-provider request is possible from any free-roam code path — confirmed structurally, not just by testing: neither `useFreeRoamNavigation.ts` nor `useFreeRoamCamera.ts` nor `FreeRoamScreen.tsx` imports anything from `routing/`. High-accuracy geolocation is already guaranteed "only while visible/active" for free: `browserGeolocationSource.watchPosition` already unconditionally passes `{enableHighAccuracy: true, maximumAge: 0}`, and the mount/unmount-driven watch lifecycle (only started by `FreeRoamScreen`'s own mount effect, stopped by its unmount) is the same mechanism route Riding already relies on. No track/speed/heading history is ever accumulated — only the latest fix and the derived last-reliable bearing persist, per this project's explicit non-goals; no test constructs or asserts an accumulated location array.
    - **Files changed**: `src/storage/db.ts`, `src/storage/mapping.ts`, `src/ui/riding/useRideNavigation.ts` (the one narrowing fix), `src/ui/diagnostics/DiagnosticsScreen.tsx` (the "Active route" field fix), `src/ui/riding/RidingLauncher.tsx`, `src/App.tsx`; new: `src/ui/riding/useFreeRoamNavigation.ts`, `src/ui/riding/useFreeRoamCamera.ts`, `src/ui/riding/FreeRoamScreen.tsx`, `src/ui/riding/FreeRoamStatusStrip.tsx`. No changes needed to `src/map/MapView.tsx`, `src/map/mapAdapter.ts`, `src/ui/riding/rideCamera.ts` (the pure reducer), `src/ui/riding/RidingWakeLockControl.tsx`, `src/ui/riding/useScreenWakeLock.ts`, `src/platform/geolocation.ts`, `src/platform/wakeLock.ts`, `src/ui/library/RouteLibrary.tsx`, `src/ui/planning/PlanningScreen.tsx` — confirmed, not assumed.
    - **Tests**: new `src/storage/mapping.test.ts` cases (`isStoredRouteRideState`/`isStoredFreeRoamRideState`, `toStoredFreeRoamState`/`fromStoredFreeRoamState` round-trip and conservative-default cases, a genuine `resolveStoredRideSessionKind` `"free-roam"` case); a new `db.migration.test.ts` free-roam round-trip case (proving no schema bump — a typed variable, not a fresh object literal, is passed to `.put()` there, since Dexie's own `InsertType<StoredRideState, "id">` utility, built from a plain `Omit`, doesn't distribute `keyof` over a union type parameter and would otherwise reject a genuinely valid field via TypeScript's fresh-literal excess-property check — a library-typing quirk, not a real problem with the row's shape, confirmed by `rideStateRepository.ts`'s own `.put(state)` call needing no such workaround since it already receives an already-typed variable). New `src/ui/riding/useFreeRoamNavigation.test.ts` (mirrors `useRideNavigation.test.ts`'s watch-lifecycle/`finish()` structure, plus a restore suite and a persistence suite). New `src/ui/riding/useFreeRoamCamera.test.ts` (bearing wiring, the initial-framing synthetic command and its three cancellation paths, `persistableLastReliableBearingDegrees` always populated, manual-gesture pause/Follow, North-up, `resetCamera`, and the "overview restore never undoes requestFollow()" regression suite). Direction-following map rotation was not exercised by this walk: the implementation deliberately trusts GPS course only at or above 2.5 m/s (approximately 9 km/h), a threshold this walk never reached, so the map retaining its initial orientation while continuing to follow position is expected, not a defect.
