### End-to-end test reliability

44. **`e2e/ridingCamera.spec.ts`'s manual-rotation/manual-gesture preconditions — done**
    - Found via a failed `Deploy to GitHub Pages` run, [31691965402](https://github.com/adf-MD/amazing-cycling-navigation/actions/runs/31691965402) (build job [94421062907](https://github.com/adf-MD/amazing-cycling-navigation/actions/runs/31691965402/job/94421062907)), for commit `0665d00` ("Fit Planning camera to restored waypoints", v0.3.26). Formatting, linting, type checking, unit/component tests and the production build all passed; the workflow's Pages setup/artifact-upload/deploy steps never ran because the E2E step failed first. The single failure was `e2e/ridingCamera.spec.ts:47:1`, "Riding: pressing Northwards twice, with a manual rotation in between, rotates back to north both times", timing out on `await expect.poll(() => mapContainer.getAttribute("data-camera-bearing")).not.toBe("0")` — the synthetic right-button `page.mouse` drag used only to establish that test's own intervening-manual-rotation precondition never caused MapLibre to publish a changed bearing, so the second Northwards press was never actually exercised.
    - `0665d00` touched only `PlanningScreen.tsx`, `MapView.tsx` (doc comments) and Planning-specific tests — it did not touch Riding production code or `e2e/ridingCamera.spec.ts` at all — so this is not evidence of a regression from that commit. It is a fresh instance of the exact narrow, CI-environment-sensitive MapLibre `DragRotateHandler`/missing-`moveend` failure mode already documented and fixed once for Planning in item 21 above. `e2e/ridingCamera.spec.ts` still carried the older, non-deterministic right-button-drag precondition in **both** of its tests (the Northwards one above, and "re-pressing Follow location with an unchanged GPS fix, after a manual gesture, resumes following") and needed the same hardening item 21 already gave Planning.
    - Confirmed safe by direct source inspection before changing anything: `mapAdapter.ts`'s `onUserCameraInteraction` (the sole "was this a genuine user gesture" mechanism) fires on MapLibre's `movestart` event whenever `event.originalEvent` is set — true for both a real pointer drag and a real keyboard-driven `easeTo`, and not true for this app's own programmatic `fitBounds`/`setCamera` calls. `RidingScreen.tsx:937` wires `onUserCameraInteraction={camera.reportUserInteraction}` — the identical generic mechanism Planning already uses to unpress its own North-up control (proven working from the keyboard path by item 21's own test). A keyboard-triggered ease is therefore indistinguishable, to production code, from a real drag for both the Northwards-unpress contract and Riding's own Follow-mode-pause contract. No production code changed.
    - **Fix.** Both tests now establish their manual-gesture precondition via a new local helper, `establishManualRotationAndPitch` (`e2e/ridingCamera.spec.ts`), mirroring `planning.spec.ts`'s own identically-named helper per this file's established no-shared-e2e-helpers-across-specs convention: focus the real `<canvas>` inside `[data-testid="map-container"]`, then drive MapLibre's own deterministic `KeyboardHandler` (`Shift+ArrowRight` then, once settled, `Shift+ArrowUp`) through its ordinary `easeTo`/`movestart`/`rotatestart`/`moveend` pipeline — eliminating the drag/inertia state machine by construction rather than retrying past it. Unlike Planning's helper, which assumes a bearing/pitch baseline of `"0"`, this version captures the map's own currently-settled bearing and pitch before pressing any key and polls for a genuine change from _that_ baseline — needed because the "re-pressing Follow location" test deliberately begins from its travel-up followed camera (pitch 35°, `FOLLOW_PITCH_DEGREES`), never from north-up/level. The Northwards test also gained an explicit `data-camera-pitch === "0"` baseline assertion and `aria-pressed` checks on `northButton` (unpressed after the manual gesture, pressed again after the second press) — real end-to-end proof of the same "manual rotation unpresses Northwards" contract `PlanningScreen.test.tsx` already covers at the mock level, through the real `onCameraSettled` path. The Follow-location test's existing `aria-pressed === "false"` and `not.toBe(followedBearing)` assertions immediately after the gesture were kept exactly as they were, even though they now prove a fact the helper's own internal poll already established, since removing the assertion that the manual change genuinely occurred was explicitly out of scope. `e2e/directionArrows.spec.ts`'s own independent real right-button-drag coverage of `DragRotateHandler` (its "arrows remain attached and correctly oriented after the map is rotated" test) is completely untouched — this fix removes the drag only where it was a precondition for unrelated behaviour, not the only remaining browser coverage of pointer rotation.
    - **Verification.** Focused: `npx playwright test e2e/ridingCamera.spec.ts` — 2/2 passed. Repeated stability, mirroring item 21's own method: `npx playwright test e2e/ridingCamera.spec.ts --repeat-each=20 --workers=1` — 40/40 passed, both with a normal reused dev server and again with `CI=1` set (forcing a fresh `vite preview` boot per `playwright.config.ts`'s `reuseExistingServer: !process.env.CI`) — 40/40 passed. Regression: `npx playwright test e2e/directionArrows.spec.ts` — 3/3 passed, including the real-drag rotation test. Full required verification (`npm run format`, `npm run lint`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run build`, `npm test` — 2205/2205 across 120 files, `npm run e2e` — 104/104 across both the `chromium` and `android-chrome` projects, `npm run format:check` run last, immediately before committing) all pass.
    - No production code changed anywhere (`src/` is untouched); only `e2e/ridingCamera.spec.ts` and this entry changed. No version bump, matching item 21's and item 30's own established precedent for test-only hardening fixes.
    - Pushed to `main` as commit `b8cec2c` ("Harden Riding camera E2E manual-gesture precondition"). The resulting `Deploy to GitHub Pages` run completed successfully and the site redeployed — confirmed directly by the user via the GitHub Actions UI (this sandbox has no network access to GitHub Actions, so the exact run ID/URL was not independently fetched and is not recorded here). This confirms the diagnosis above: the only defect was this file's own flaky E2E precondition, not the production build or the Pages workflow configuration, which is why no production-code change was needed. Because `0665d00`'s own deploy attempt was blocked by that precondition failure, `0665d00` itself was never actually served from GitHub Pages on its own — `b8cec2c` (which carries `0665d00`'s changes forward unchanged, plus this fix) is the first commit whose deploy actually reached production.

45. **`e2e/ridingLauncher.spec.ts`'s shared setup helper: an accepted-fix/committed-persistence race — done**
    - Found via a failed `Deploy to GitHub Pages` run, [run 31797235417](https://github.com/adf-MD/amazing-cycling-navigation/actions/runs/31797235417) (build job [94756890778](https://github.com/adf-MD/amazing-cycling-navigation/actions/runs/31797235417/job/94756890778)), for commit `2dc431f` ("Add Ride launcher session recovery", v0.3.32). Formatting, linting, type checking, all unit/component tests and the production build all passed; Pages setup/artifact-upload/deploy never ran because the E2E step failed first, blocking deployment. Playwright reported 117 passing and one failure: `e2e/ridingLauncher.spec.ts:136:1`, "the launcher can end an unfinished ride directly, without ever resuming GPS", timing out on the first post-reload assertion, `await expect(page.getByRole("button", { name: "Resume route" })).toBeVisible();`. The other two tests in that same file, all Android-emulated tests, and the production build all passed.
    - **Confirmed root cause**, by direct source inspection, not assumption: the file's shared `establishUnfinishedRide(...)` helper returned as soon as `await expect(page.getByText("On route")).toBeVisible()` resolved — that text is driven by `coreState.offRouteMachineState`, set synchronously inside `useRideNavigation.ts`'s `handleFix` callback. The actual persisted `rideState` row is written by a **separate** `useEffect` (`useRideNavigation.ts`'s persistence effect) that calls the async, un-throttled `setActiveRideState(...)` Dexie write after React commits that state update — fire-and-forget, with no signal back to the UI (`.catch()` only, no `await` anywhere upstream of it). All three tests in the file call `establishUnfinishedRide(...)` and then `page.reload()` soon after; a reload that wins this race finds no persisted session, so the launcher never shows `Resume route`/`End ride`/`Discard`. This is a missing E2E setup postcondition, not a production or GitHub Pages workflow defect — confirmed by every other CI stage passing cleanly, and this is the identical class of race `e2e/androidPersistenceAndOffline.spec.ts` already documented and fixed once before (see item 25's own follow-up).
    - **Fix**: `establishUnfinishedRide` now imports and uses `readSavedRouteId` alongside the file's existing `readActiveRideStateRow` (both from `e2e/support/rideStateDb.ts`, unchanged), mirroring `androidPersistenceAndOffline.spec.ts`'s own established idiom exactly rather than inventing a second one. After the existing "On route" UI assertion, it now resolves the just-saved route's real id (`readSavedRouteId(page, routeName)`, asserted non-null) and polls the real committed row (`expect.poll(() => readActiveRideStateRow(page), { timeout: 10_000 })`) to `toMatchObject({ kind: "route", routeId, lastFix: expect.anything(), lastMatchedPointIndex: expect.any(Number), matchedDistanceFromStartMetres: expect.any(Number) })` — `kind: "route"` confirmed present on every written row via `src/storage/mapping.ts`'s `toStoredRideState` — before returning. Because this proof lives inside the one shared helper, all three reload-dependent scenarios in the file receive the same reliable precondition, not just the one that happened to fail in CI. `completionArmed` is deliberately not asserted: unlike `androidPersistenceAndOffline.spec.ts`'s own fixture (pinned at the route's start for its whole test, so `completionArmed` is provably always `false` there), this file's fix moves 400 m along a 1000 m route — inside `rideCompletion.ts`'s own interior-progress arming range — so no specific value is deterministically justified here, and the task's own required-field list didn't call for it. The helper's doc comment was extended to state this UI-state-vs-committed-persistence distinction explicitly, crediting the precedent it reuses.
    - **Verification.** Focused: `npx playwright test e2e/ridingLauncher.spec.ts` — 3/3 passed. Repeated stability of the previously-failing scenario alone, mirroring items 21/44's own established method: `npx playwright test e2e/ridingLauncher.spec.ts --grep "the launcher can end an unfinished ride directly" --repeat-each=20 --workers=1` — 20/20 passed; the identical run repeated with `CI=1` set (forcing `playwright.config.ts`'s `reuseExistingServer: false` path, i.e. a fresh `vite preview` boot per run, matching how GitHub Actions itself launches the server) — 20/20 passed. Full required gate: `npm run format` (no changes beyond this slice's own edits), `npm run lint` (clean), `npm run typecheck` (clean), `npm run build` (succeeded), `npm test` — 2315/2315 across 123 files, `npm run e2e` — 118/118 across both the `chromium` (106, including all three `ridingLauncher.spec.ts` tests) and `android-chrome` (12) projects, `npm run format:check` run last, immediately before committing (clean) — all passed.
    - No production code changed anywhere (`src/` is untouched); only `e2e/ridingLauncher.spec.ts` and this entry changed. No version bump, matching items 21/30/44's own established precedent for test-only hardening fixes.
    - Pushed to `main` as commit `7796ab5` ("Harden Ride launcher E2E persistence precondition"). The user directly confirmed, via the GitHub Actions UI, that the resulting `Deploy to GitHub Pages` run completed successfully and the site redeployed — this sandbox has no network access to GitHub Actions, so the exact run ID/URL was not independently fetched and is not recorded here; the confirmation is the user's own direct observation, not an assistant-verified fact. This is the baseline commit item 42 (route-less free roam) built on top of.

### Ride navigation reliability

46. **Preserve closed-loop progress at the finish — done**
    - **Real iPhone field symptom.** During the same field ride that exercised items 40–42 (recorded under item 43's ledger and each of those items' own entries above), the rider completed a closed-loop route whose start and finish share the same coordinate. Progress tracked correctly for the whole ride, including through a suspension/reload/Resume-riding cycle. On returning to the finish, the rider continued a few metres further, looking for space between parked cars. Displayed route progress then snapped from near-total distance back to near the beginning of the loop, while the app still reported `On route` and showed distance remaining as if the ride had barely started. Because the conservative route-completion detector (item 29) reads exactly this frozen/reliable progress value, `Finish ride` was never offered. This is a report of the pre-fix defect, not a verification of the fix below — see item 43's own ledger for the required post-fix real-device retest.
    - **Evidence-backed root cause**, confirmed by direct reading of `src/navigation/projection.ts` and independently re-verified with real numeric arithmetic run against the installed `@turf/nearest-point-on-line`: `projectFixOntoRoute` first searches a `WINDOW_RADIUS_METRES` (400 m) route-distance window around `lastMatch` — the mechanism that correctly disambiguates loops, self-intersections and out-and-back sections — and only falls back to a whole-route search when the windowed match is rejected by `isClippedAtEdge` as sitting at a clipped edge of that window. Near a closed loop's finish, `lastMatch.distanceFromStartMetres ≈ total`, so the window `[total − 400, total]` (there is nothing beyond the route's own total distance) is clipped only on its lower side; its upper side naturally reaches the route's own genuine, un-clipped final point. The pre-fix `isClippedAtEdge` conflated "was _either_ side of the window clipped" with "was _this match's_ edge the one that was clipped" — a correct windowed match sitting exactly at the route's genuine final boundary was wrongly rejected purely because the _other_, unrelated side happened to be truncated. This fell through to a whole-route nearest-point search, which — since the loop's start and finish are geographically coincident — resolved to the route's start (`@turf/nearest-point-on-line`'s internal tie-break is a strict `<` replace, so the first-encountered coincident vertex, index 0, wins). Nothing downstream distrusted this: `rideNavigationCore.ts`'s `lastReliableMatch = raw === "off-route" ? previous.lastReliableMatch : projection` only freezes on a raw `"off-route"` classification, but a reacquired fix classifies as `"untrusted"` (a different value), so the false reacquire flowed straight through into the presentation/reliable distance shown to the rider and into completion detection — explaining why no off-route warning appeared either.
    - **The exact correction.** `isClippedAtEdge` (file-private, not exported — no signature or call-site change anywhere) now judges each edge independently, preserving the original boundary tightness (`<=`/`>=`/`-1`) per side:
      ```ts
      function isClippedAtEdge(
        match: NearestMatch,
        range: IndexRange,
        pointCount: number,
      ): boolean {
        const clippedAtStart =
          range.startIndex > 0 && match.pointIndex <= range.startIndex;
        const clippedAtEnd =
          range.endIndex < pointCount - 1 && match.pointIndex >= range.endIndex - 1;
        return clippedAtStart || clippedAtEnd;
      }
      ```
      No other change anywhere in `projection.ts`, `rideNavigationCore.ts`, `offRoute.ts` or `rideCompletion.ts` — the fix is fully contained in this one function, confirmed by tracing the whole call chain before and after.
    - **Existing safeguards against immediate completion at the start are unchanged and re-proven.** The arming gate from item 29's own follow-up slice (`isRouteArmingFixEligible`: a departure-radius check plus a 10%–80% interior-progress fraction, requiring 2 consecutive eligible fixes before any fix can count towards completion at all) was not touched. A closed-loop route's fresh, unarmed first fix at the shared start/finish coordinate still resolves to the start (proven directly, both at the projection layer with no `lastMatch` and at the completion layer via the pre-existing "hostile closed-loop start projection" test in `rideCompletion.test.ts`, both re-run and still passing unmodified). The new coverage below additionally proves a full lap's worth of continuous progress reaches the genuine finish without a false reacquire, and that the arming/completion pipeline confirms only after the existing consecutive-fix requirement — never merely from proximity to the shared coordinate.
    - **Deterministic regression coverage added:**
      - `src/test/fixtures/closedLoopRoute.ts` (new): a shared ~3.6 km square closed-loop `RoutePoint[]` fixture, matching `outAndBackRoute.ts`/`selfIntersectingRoute.ts`'s own convention; `src/navigation/rideCompletion.test.ts`'s previously inline, duplicate `CLOSED_LOOP_WAYPOINTS`/`CLOSED_LOOP_POINTS` now import from it instead (a mechanical refactor, its own 35 tests re-run and confirmed unaffected).
      - `src/navigation/projection.test.ts`: 6 new tests — a windowed match at the exact shared finish coordinate, and a few metres past it, both proven to no longer fall back to a whole-route reacquire; a fresh ride's first fix (no `lastMatch`) still resolves to the start, not the finish (documented as depending on `@turf/nearest-point-on-line`'s own internal, version-sensitive tie-break, so a future turf bump failing this specific test is a legible flag point, not a mystery regression); two new tests on a separate long straight-route fixture proving a _genuinely_ clipped start edge and a _genuinely_ clipped end edge (unrelated to the loop's coincident geometry) still correctly reacquire; and a closed-loop addition to the existing `walkRoute()`-based continuity suite, proving `distanceFromStartMetres` stays monotonically non-decreasing all the way around the loop, including at the shared coordinate.
      - `src/navigation/rideNavigationCore.test.ts`: one new test walking the closed-loop fixture through sequential `processFix` calls, proving both `lastMatch` and `lastReliableMatch` distance climb monotonically to the route total with `reacquired: false` at the finish. Deliberately does **not** assert on `offRouteMachineState` staying `"on-route"` as a proxy for "no false reacquire" — a reacquired fix classifies `"untrusted"`, not `"off-route"`, so the off-route machine is left completely unchanged either way; that assertion would not have caught the original bug, which is exactly why the field defect produced no off-route warning at all.
      - `src/ui/riding/RidingScreen.closedLoopCompletion.test.tsx` (new file, mirroring `RidingScreen.completionArming.test.tsx`'s own harness and this codebase's established convention of splitting test files by sub-concern): two tests — a full lap, armed via two consecutive interior fixes then walked forward in small route-distance strides (never one large jump, which could itself trigger a legitimate reacquire that resolves to the coincident start regardless of whether this fix works, silently failing to exercise the bug at all) to the finish and a few metres past it, confirming completion without a false reacquire; and a session restored via `setActiveRideState` with `completionArmed: true` and near-total matched progress near the finish, resumed, confirming completion on the next eligible fixes without snapping to the start. Both tests were confirmed, by temporarily reverting only the production fix, to fail against the pre-fix code with the predicted symptom (`Route complete` never appearing), then to pass again once restored.
      - `e2e/ridingFinishAndEnd.spec.ts`: one new real-browser test using a new, locally-defined `buildClosedLoopRouteGpx()` helper (own copy, per this repo's established no-shared-GPX-helpers-across-specs convention) generating a ~1.2 km square-loop GPX track. Imports and opens the route, arms via two consecutive interior `context.setGeolocation` fixes, walks forward towards the finish in several intermediate steps, then confirms completion via a fix exactly at the shared finish coordinate followed by one a few metres past it (the "looking for parking" field scenario) — each step deterministically awaits its own exact `Remaining: X km` text before the next is issued, rather than a fixed sleep, closing a real timing race discovered while writing this test (Playwright's mocked geolocation can coalesce rapid successive `setGeolocation` calls faster than the app's watch/render cycle can keep up, so each step's own postcondition must be awaited explicitly). Confirms `Finish ride` clears the persisted session (polled via the existing `e2e/support/rideStateDb.ts` helper) without re-proving the generic reload-and-reopen mechanics already covered by this file's existing straight-route test. Confirmed, by the same temporarily-reverted-fix method, to fail against the pre-fix code at exactly the finish step, and to pass reliably (5/5, then a further repeated run) once restored.
    - **Verification.** Focused: `projection.test.ts` (15/15), `rideNavigationCore.test.ts` (8/8), `rideCompletion.test.ts` (35/35, post-refactor), `RidingScreen.closedLoopCompletion.test.tsx` (2/2), `e2e/ridingFinishAndEnd.spec.ts` (3/3, `chromium` project). Full required gate: `npm run format`, `npm run lint`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run build`, `npm test` — 2406/2406 across 128 files (one `RouteLibrary.test.tsx` focus-timing test failed once under full-suite parallel load, confirmed pre-existing and unrelated — 46/46 pass in isolation, and a full re-run of the entire suite passed 2406/2406 clean, matching the identical known flake item 33 already documented), `npm run e2e` — 129/129 across both the `chromium` (115) and `android-chrome` (14) projects, `npm run format:check` run last, immediately before committing — all pass. Version bumped to 0.3.34 (`package.json` and `package-lock.json`'s two matching `version` fields, hand-patched, following the established precedent).
    - **Automated verification is not real-device acceptance.** Every test above is deterministic/simulated (Vitest fixtures, mocked geolocation, or Playwright's mocked `context.setGeolocation`) — none of it, by itself, rode an actual closed loop on an actual iPhone. The user's field evidence recorded earlier in this entry identified the **pre-fix defect** through real riding; it was not, and must not be read as, a verification of this slice's **fix**.
    - **Real-device retest — now confirmed (17 August 2026).** That retest has since been done: a post-fix real bicycle ride to the finish of a closed loop, on the deployed build carrying this fix, confirmed the corrected behaviour directly — starting at the shared start/finish did not offer `Finish ride`; completion became armed only after departure and credible interior progress; returning to the shared endpoint retained progress near the route total instead of snapping back to the start; continuing a few metres past the endpoint still allowed completion within the existing proximity allowance; `Route complete` appeared only after the required consecutive eligible fixes; `Finish ride` cleared the unfinished session and returned to the empty Ride launcher; the saved route remained available; and suspension, reload and `Resume riding` during the loop did not break the distinction between starting and finishing (see "Manual acceptance status" above). This item is now manually verified on the deployed iPhone PWA — see items 40 and 41's own entries, which depended on this retest and are now updated accordingly.

47. **Skip synthetic waypoint-seam manoeuvres in Riding — done**
    - Real-device symptom: the next-manoeuvre panel sometimes showed bare `Waypoint` plus a distance where a real instruction (e.g. "Turn left onto …") should appear, obscuring or delaying the next actionable turn.
    - Confirmed root cause, verified directly against source before fixing: `src/navigation/nextManoeuvre.ts`'s `selectNextManoeuvre` (item 8's ninth-slice mechanism) advanced and selected purely by distance/index against `MANOEUVRE_REACHED_TOLERANCE_METRES`, with no filtering on `Manoeuvre.type`. `src/routing/stitchPlannedRouteLegs.ts` synthesises a `type: "waypoint"` entry (instruction text dropped) at an internal multi-leg seam whenever a leg's trailing `finish` and the next leg's leading `start` fall within its dedup tolerance — confirmed, via `src/routing/manoeuvreTypes.ts`'s own doc comment and test, that `decodeOrsManoeuvreType` never produces `"waypoint"` from a raw provider step; it is purely synthetic. `src/ui/riding/RidingNextManoeuvrePanel.tsx`'s `genericManoeuvreLabel` has a dedicated `case "waypoint": return "Waypoint"` (distinct from the `"unknown"` fallback, "Continue on the route"), which is why the panel rendered literally "Waypoint" instead of a generic continuation message. No existing test (`nextManoeuvre.test.ts`, `RidingNextManoeuvrePanel.test.tsx`, `RidingScreen.test.tsx`, any e2e spec) exercised a `"waypoint"`-type selection before this slice.
    - **Fix and why `selectNextManoeuvre` is the correct boundary**: it is the sole production selection point (`RidingScreen.tsx`'s only call site), so a fix there covers every current and future presentation consumer with no filtering needed anywhere else — never at the seam-synthesis site, never in the panel. The existing physical, distance-based `reachedIndex` advancement loop (and its monotonic `Math.max(reachedIndex, previousReachedIndex)` clamp) is completely unchanged. A new, purely type-based forward scan runs immediately afterwards, starting from the already-computed `reachedIndex`: a local `displayIndex` advances while `manoeuvres[displayIndex]?.type === "waypoint"` — deliberately distance-independent (no `MANOEUVRE_REACHED_TOLERANCE_METRES` check at all), so a seam is skipped pre-emptively, well before it is physically reached, and a real manoeuvre near a seam is never swallowed by proximity alone. `displayIndex`, not `reachedIndex`, now drives the presented `selection.index`/`.manoeuvre`/`.remainingDistanceMetres`; the top-level `NextManoeuvreResult.reachedIndex` returned to the caller is untouched, still purely physical, so `RidingScreen.tsx`'s `reachedManoeuvreIndex` feedback loop needed no change at all. Monotonicity is inherited for free rather than re-implemented: since `reachedIndex` is already non-decreasing across calls, and `displayIndex` is a pure, stateless function of `(manoeuvres, reachedIndex)` — "the smallest index ≥ reachedIndex that is non-waypoint" — the constraint set can only shrink as `reachedIndex` grows, so `displayIndex` can only stay equal or increase across calls too; no new persisted state was needed to avoid regressing to an already-skipped seam. `NextManoeuvreSelection.index` and the top-level `reachedIndex` now genuinely diverge once a seam is skipped (previously always identical) — confirmed by a repo-wide grep that no consumer read `selection.index` before this slice, so the divergence is safe; both interfaces' doc comments now state it explicitly. `RidingNextManoeuvrePanel.tsx` needed **no change at all**: its existing `selection: null` + `isTrusted: true` → render-nothing branch is already exactly correct once the selector never returns a waypoint-typed selection.
    - **Deliberate test-coverage deviation from this item's own original wording**: rather than adding a `RidingNextManoeuvrePanel.test.tsx` case that constructs a `"waypoint"`-typed `selection` prop by hand, a `RidingScreen.test.tsx` integration test proves the true selector-to-panel contract instead. A hand-built panel-level case would feed the panel a prop the real selector can no longer produce — an impossible state once this fix lands — and so would prove nothing beyond the panel's own pre-existing generic-fallback robustness, already covered by its existing "renders a legacy non-canonical manoeuvre type without throwing" test.
    - Files changed: `src/navigation/nextManoeuvre.ts` (the fix, plus updated `NextManoeuvreSelection.index`/`NextManoeuvreResult.reachedIndex` doc comments explaining the divergence). Tests: `src/navigation/nextManoeuvre.test.ts` (six new cases — pre-emptive skip of a seam well before it is physically reached; a seam and a real turn sharing an identical route distance, real turn wins; a real turn immediately behind a reached seam not swallowed by tolerance proximity, mirroring the file's existing "two manoeuvres closer together than the tolerance" test; no regression to a skipped seam after backward GPS jitter, fed through two sequential calls; multiple adjacent seams — a middle leg with no turns of its own, a realistic production shape — skipped together; a trailing run of nothing-but-seams correctly returning `selection: null` with `reachedIndex` still consistent and forward); `src/ui/riding/RidingScreen.test.tsx` (one new case inside the existing `describe("next manoeuvre", ...)` block, a `plannerRouteWithWaypointSeam` fixture carrying a real left turn, a synthetic seam, a real right turn and the finish, proving the panel shows the real right turn — never the literal text "Waypoint" — both well before and immediately after the seam is physically reached); `e2e/ridingNextManoeuvre.spec.ts` (one new test driving a genuinely stitched 2-leg, 3-waypoint Planning flow through mocked ORS, so `stitchPlannedRouteLegs.ts` itself produces the seam in production, not a hand-constructed fixture — each leg gets its own fixed, disconnected-from-clicks mocked response supplying a real turn plus the `finish`/`start` pair that collapses, assigned by request arrival order exactly as `e2e/planning.spec.ts`'s own already-passing "plans a route across three waypoints using exactly one routing request per section" test already relies on and proves for this identical "N clicks then one Calculate press" flow, with the same coordinate-continuity assertion reused here as a diagnostic canary; drives geolocation across the seam and proves the panel always shows the correct real turn and never the text "Waypoint", both pre-emptively and after physically passing it). No change to `src/routing/stitchPlannedRouteLegs.ts` (+ its tests), `src/routing/manoeuvreTypes.ts` (+ its tests), `src/domain/manoeuvreTrust.ts`, or `src/ui/riding/RidingNextManoeuvrePanel.tsx` (+ its tests) — confirmed unnecessary by direct reading, not merely left alone by omission.
    - Every new/changed test was confirmed, by temporarily reverting only the production fix (`src/navigation/nextManoeuvre.ts`) and re-running, to fail against the pre-fix code with the exact predicted symptom (the seam's own text, or the absence of the real turn) — genuine regression detectors, not vacuous assertions — then to pass again once the fix was restored; the new e2e test was additionally repeated 5× with no flakiness.
    - Verification: focused (`nextManoeuvre.test.ts` 22/22; `RidingNextManoeuvrePanel.test.tsx`/`RidingScreen.test.tsx`/`stitchPlannedRouteLegs.test.ts` combined 190/190; `e2e/ridingNextManoeuvre.spec.ts` 4/4) and the full repository gate — `npm run format`, `npm run lint`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run build`, `npm test` (2413/2413 across 128 files — one unrelated `src/gpx/parseAcnExtension.test.ts` timeout was observed once under full-suite parallel load and confirmed pre-existing and unrelated: it passed in isolation in ~20s, right at the default 20s test timeout, and a full re-run of the entire suite passed clean), `npm run e2e` (130/130 across both the `chromium` and `android-chrome` projects), `npm run format:check` run last — all pass. Version bumped to 0.3.35 (`package.json` and `package-lock.json`'s two matching `version` fields, hand-patched, following the established precedent).
    - **Automated evidence only.** No real-device testing was performed for this slice. Post-deployment iPhone verification (confirming the next-manoeuvre panel correctly skips a genuine multi-waypoint route's internal seam and shows the next real turn) and physical Android verification both remain outstanding — do not treat this item as manually verified until each has actually been done.

### Planning draft workflow

48. **Planning action-card polish — done**
    - Follow-up to item 36 (Compact per-draft routing preferences and reordered Calculate action).
    - Confirmed current behaviour: `.settings-disclosure` (shared by the "Change" routing-preferences disclosure and "How recalculation works") plus `.settings-disclosure summary`'s own `min-height: var(--touch-target-min)` (44px) yields a closed height of `44px summary + 8px×2 padding + 1px×2 border = 62px` — about 18px taller than an ordinary 44px button such as "Clear draft" (exact CSS confirmed in `src/index.css`). The "Change" disclosure sits beside the compact routing-summary text as siblings in the same `display: flex; flex-wrap: wrap` `.row` (`PlanningScreen.tsx`); because the `<details>` element's own intrinsic content width grows once open (profile buttons plus a checkbox row, versus just the closed "Change" label), this is a structural shift risk inferred from the CSS rather than one confirmed by an actual rendered observation — verify visually before treating it as settled. "How recalculation works" currently exists only inside Planning's `.planning-calculate-actions`; Settings' "Route planning" panel has no equivalent disclosure today (Settings' only existing `.settings-disclosure` lives in the separate OpenRouteService/API-key panel).
    - Required outcome: restructure so "Change" sits beneath the compact routing summary (stacked), not sharing a flex row with it. Give the closed "Change" trigger the same effective height as an ordinary action button (~44px) while keeping its own touch target ≥44×44px. Ensure opening/closing "Change" cannot shift or rewrap the routing-summary text or the trigger's own horizontal position. Move "How recalculation works" out of Planning entirely and into Settings > Route planning, reusing the existing `.settings-disclosure` pattern. Keep the provider key status, provider error, and stale-result message always visible in Planning, never folded into any disclosure — per this file's own existing "never hide the current provider error or stale-result message" rule. Preserve the existing automatic-recalculation behaviour (explicit first Calculate; a later route-affecting edit schedules the existing automatic recalculation; Save/Export stay unavailable until it completes) completely unchanged — this is confirmed working as intended and must not be described as a defect anywhere in this item.
    - Required coverage: a real-browser (Playwright) geometry assertion that the closed "Change" control's height matches an ordinary button's, and that no horizontal shift occurs across open/close at a representative phone viewport (Vitest's `css:false` environment cannot verify computed height); a Settings component test confirming "How recalculation works" renders there and no longer in Planning.
    - **Implementation.** The compact routing-summary paragraph and the "Change" disclosure moved from a shared `.row` into a new `.planning-routing-summary` (`display:flex; flex-direction:column; gap: var(--space-8)`, mirroring `.planning-calculate-actions`'s own tight-vertical-grouping precedent) — stacking, not sharing a flex row, is what structurally stops "Change"'s much-wider open content from reflowing the summary text beside it. A new modifier, `.settings-disclosure--compact`, applies only to Planning's "Change" `<details>` (`className="settings-disclosure settings-disclosure--compact"`) — never to Settings' disclosures, which keep the base class alone. It relocates the border/padding that makes the control read as a button: closed, the `<summary>` alone carries a border+padding box model identical in shape to the global `button` rule, so it renders at exactly the same 44px `min-height` floor a button does (verified directly against a real rendered page: `<details>`'s own closed height is 44px, matching `Undo`'s height exactly, versus 62px before); open, that border/padding relocates onto the outer `<details>` (to frame the revealed profile-buttons/ferries-checkbox content) while the `<summary>`'s own border/padding drops to 0 — 16px of left padding comes from the summary itself when closed and from the outer `<details>` when open, so the outer control's own box (not `<summary>`'s, which legitimately relocates its border/padding inward when opened even though the rendered text does not move — a real distinction the Playwright coverage below had to account for directly) never shifts horizontally. A `list-style:none`/`summary::-webkit-details-marker{display:none}` pair on the compact summary rule suppresses a possible stray Safari-only native disclosure marker (Chromium/Firefox already suppress it via the base rule's existing `display:flex`, confirmed by direct inspection finding no `list-style`/`::marker` override existed anywhere before this slice) — a defensive, narrowly-scoped addition since Safari/WebKit isn't covered by this repository's Playwright projects (`chromium`/`android-chrome` only) and the app's primary target is an iPhone Safari PWA. "How recalculation works" (verbatim wording) moved from Planning's `.planning-calculate-actions` to the end of Settings' existing "Route planning" `<section>`, using the unmodified base `.settings-disclosure` class, mirroring the OpenRouteService panel's own existing "How the key and route data are used" disclosure's unconditional last-child placement exactly. Provider-key status/error/stale-result messages in Planning are untouched — they were never inside the moved disclosure.
    - One accepted, documented visual characteristic found during implementation, not fixed: `ConfirmDialog`'s rendered box (`.route-delete-confirm`, item 49 below) has its own `margin-top: 0.5rem` which doesn't collapse through a flex-item boundary, so the gap above an open Clear-draft confirmation is ~24px rather than the uniform 16px elsewhere in the action card — a pre-existing property of the shared component, deliberately left alone rather than special-cased with a scoped override, since it's visually minor and the component is reused by Settings/Riding confirmations too.
    - Real-browser verification (`e2e/planning.spec.ts`'s existing phone-viewport test, extended): the closed "Change" control's overall height (measured on the outer `<details>`, not `<summary>` — `<summary>`'s own height is always 44px regardless of state, so the real 62px-vs-44px discrepancy only shows up one level up, a distinction found and corrected during this slice's own test-writing) matches `Undo`'s height within 1px; the routing-summary paragraph's and "Change" control's own x-position stay within 1px across closed → open → re-closed; no horizontal overflow at either state. Per this file's own testing discipline, both new geometry assertions were confirmed, by temporarily reverting only the production CSS/JSX changes and re-running, to fail against the pre-fix implementation with the exact predicted symptom (an 18px height mismatch; a 17px x-shift on `<summary>`'s own box before the test was corrected to measure `<details>` instead) before being relied on as regression evidence.
    - **Post-deployment follow-up (hierarchy correction).** Real iPhone inspection of the deployed build found a genuine, if more subtle, problem the automated geometry proof above didn't (and couldn't) catch: the routing summary ("Routing: Road bike · Ferries avoided") rendered as a small, muted `.field-hint` paragraph sitting beside a full-width, bold, bordered "Change" control — the two didn't read as one unit, and "Change" visually dominated the actual routing state it exists to change. This follow-up **deliberately supersedes** this item's own "closed disclosure matches an ordinary 44px button's height" requirement — two lines of text can no longer fit in 44px — while keeping its ≥44×44px touch-target and no-horizontal-shift requirements fully intact, now widened to also cover width, not merely x-position.
    - **Revised hierarchy.** The routing value now renders as the primary line _inside_ the "Change" disclosure's own `<summary>` (not beside it in a separate `<p>`), with "Change" plus an explicit open/closed chevron as a smaller, secondary line beneath it — both inside the one bordered card `.settings-disclosure--compact` already produced. `PlanningScreen.tsx`'s previous `<div className="planning-routing-summary">` wrapper (holding a sibling `<p className="field-hint">` and the `<details>`) is removed outright — `<details>` is now a direct child of the action card's `.stack`, immediately followed by the Clear-draft slot exactly as before, just one level shallower. `<summary>` now contains only phrasing content (nested `<span>`s, per its own HTML content model): `.planning-routing-disclosure-header` (a flex-column wrapper; `width: 100%` is required since `<summary>`'s own `display:flex` doesn't stretch a shrink-to-fit flex item to the row's own width by default, and without it a longer value — "General cycling · Ferries allowed" — could overflow rather than wrap; no `align-items` override is needed beyond that, since flex's own default `stretch` already gives `.planning-routing-disclosure-value`/`-action` the header's full width for free, the same reasoning this file already relies on for `.stack`'s own default stretch elsewhere), `.planning-routing-disclosure-value` (`1rem`/`600`/full `--colour-text` contrast — no longer the low-emphasis `.field-hint` treatment), `.planning-routing-disclosure-action` (`0.85rem`/`500`/`--colour-accent` — strictly smaller _and_ lighter than the value, never the reverse), and `.planning-routing-disclosure-chevron` (a plain `▾` glyph, `aria-hidden`, rotating 180° via `.settings-disclosure--compact[open] .planning-routing-disclosure-chevron` — a real, non-colour shape change communicates open/closed, needing no dedicated `prefers-reduced-motion` handling since the existing global `*, *::before, *::after` transition-duration override already covers it). "Change" is deliberately isolated in its own leaf `.planning-routing-disclosure-action-label` span, sibling to the chevron rather than its parent: nesting the chevron glyph _inside_ the same element as "Change" would make that element's own text content `"Change▾"`, breaking every pre-existing `getByText("Change", {exact:true})` call site (~15, across `PlanningScreen.tsx`'s own tests and `e2e/planning.spec.ts`) that depends on some element's full text being exactly `"Change"`. `describeCurrentDraftRoutingSummary` itself is unchanged — only its call site moved.
    - **Accessibility.** Native `<details>`/`<summary>` semantics are unchanged; no ARIA role is added since `<summary>` has no implicit role recognised by this project's `aria-query`/`@testing-library/dom` versions (confirmed by inspecting `node_modules` — role-based queries would never match it here, consistent with this codebase's pre-existing `.closest("summary")` convention, e.g. `SettingsScreen.test.tsx`). No `aria-label` is added either: the routing value and "Change" are both plain, visible (non-`aria-hidden`) text within the one `<summary>`, so the browser's own name-from-content algorithm concatenates them into one accessible name (e.g. "Routing: Road bike · Ferries avoided Change") conveying both the current state and that it can be changed, before it is opened — a redundant/divergent `aria-label` would only risk a WCAG 2.5.3 (Label in Name) mismatch. Only the chevron is `aria-hidden`.
    - **`PlanningScreen.clearDraft.test.tsx` consequence.** Its one test anchoring on `changeDetails.parentElement` (to find the now-removed wrapper, then assert `wrapper.nextElementSibling` was the Clear-draft slot from item 49) was updated to anchor directly on `changeDetails.nextElementSibling` — a strictly simpler, equally precise expression of the identical fact, made possible by the DOM flattening above, not a weakening.
    - **New/replaced coverage.** `PlanningScreen.test.tsx` gained a `describe("routing disclosure hierarchy (backlog item 48 follow-up)", ...)` block (4 tests): the routing value and "Change" share the same `<summary>` ancestor, which belongs to the disclosure revealing the profile/ferries controls; there is exactly one visible copy of each, with the value preceding "Change" in DOM order (`compareDocumentPosition`, mirroring this file's own existing convention at its "Calculate precedes Waypoints" test); both are visible, non-hidden text within the closed `<summary>` and the chevron carries `aria-hidden="true"`; changing profile or ferries updates the header's text in place with no duplicate. `e2e/planning.spec.ts`'s existing phone-viewport test had its "Change" control's height-parity-with-`Undo` assertion retired (the requirement this follow-up supersedes) and replaced with: the whole `<summary>` (not just "Change") is a real ≥44×44px touch target; the value sits above, and never overlaps, the "Change"+chevron row, both fully contained within the bordered `<details>`; computed `font-size`/`font-weight` prove "Change" is strictly smaller and no heavier than the value; clicking the _value_ text (not "Change" or the chevron) opens the disclosure, proving the whole header is tappable; the chevron's computed `transform` differs between closed and open; exactly one copy of the value and of "Change" exist anywhere (`toHaveCount(1)`) with the value still visible while open; the outer `<details>` card's `x` **and** `width` (not x alone, as before) stay stable within 1px across closed → open → re-closed; both the short (`Road bike · Ferries avoided`) and long (`General cycling · Ferries allowed`) states remain non-overlapping, fully contained, and overflow-free. Both the new component assertions and the rewritten Playwright assertions were confirmed, by temporarily reverting only the production CSS/JSX changes and re-running, to fail against the pre-fix implementation for the predicted reason (the pre-fix `<summary>Change</summary>` has no `<summary>` _ancestor_ reachable from the "Change" text node, since "Change" _is_ the summary — the real, if slightly different from first-guessed, structural cause) before being relied on as regression evidence.
    - **Verification.** `npm run format`, `npm run lint`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run build`, `npm test` — 2419/2419 across 128 files, `npm run e2e` — 131/131 across both the `chromium` and `android-chrome` projects, `npm run format:check` run last, immediately before committing (a lint-driven narrowing-style edit reintroduced formatting drift in `PlanningScreen.test.tsx`, caught here and fixed with one more `npm run format` pass before re-verifying lint/typecheck/tests) — all pass. Version bumped to 0.3.37 (`package.json` and `package-lock.json`'s two matching `version` fields, hand-patched, following the established precedent). This commit's own SHA is not self-cited here — a commit's diff cannot correctly reference its own resulting hash, which is why item 46's own implementation commit (which this file's history shows immediately above) likewise records only its version bump, never a self-referential SHA; only a _later_ item may correctly cite an _earlier_, already-existing commit.
    - Real-device iPhone verification is now confirmed: the revised routing-disclosure hierarchy (the value-and-"Change" `<summary>` restructuring) reads correctly on the installed iPhone Home Screen PWA, with no touch-target or overflow regression. This confirms only the hierarchy correction itself, not physical Android Chrome verification (still outstanding), and does not itself verify any other, separately-untested aspect of items 48 or 49.

49. **Inline confirmation for Clear draft — done**
    - Follow-up to item 37 (Clear the current draft and standardise draft terminology).
    - Confirmed current behaviour: Clear draft's trigger button and its `ConfirmDialog` are not an in-place morph — the dialog is a separate sibling element rendered after the action-card `<div>` closes (`PlanningScreen.tsx`), positioned between the action card and the Waypoints section. `ConfirmDialog.tsx` itself has no concept of "the trigger"; it always renders its own standalone `role="alertdialog"` box wherever the caller places it, returning `null` when closed.
    - Required outcome: pressing `Clear draft` replaces only the button/trigger area in place with the expanded "Clear this draft?" explanation and Cancel/confirm actions — not a separate card appearing elsewhere in the action card or screen. The rest of the Planning action card remains visible and stable around it. Preserve every existing semantic from item 37 exactly: Cancel keeps the complete draft; confirming clears waypoints/route/name/routing settings while saved routes are untouched; a rejected write shows the existing accessible error and retry, never a false success; Escape and focus restoration keep working; a rapid double-click issues at most one clear; the button reads "Clearing…" while in flight; the existing `saveGenerationRef`/`saveTimeoutRef` race-cancellation reuse from Save is untouched.
    - May combine with item 48 into one reviewable Planning-action-card slice if, at implementation time, the two changes touch overlapping markup with no meaningful way to test them independently — do not force a merge if it would couple the unrelated disclosure-layout and destructive-confirmation concerns.
    - Required coverage: a test proving the confirmation renders in the trigger's own DOM position, not appended elsewhere; existing Cancel/Escape/failure/retry/double-click coverage from `PlanningScreen.clearDraft.test.tsx` continues to pass in substance (updated only for the new DOM structure).
    - **Implementation.** Combined with item 48 into one slice, as permitted above, since both touch the same action-card JSX region and the geometry/placement coverage for each is easiest to write and verify together. `ConfirmDialog.tsx` itself is unmodified — no new prop, no in-place mode added to the shared component — keeping Settings' key-deletion dialog and Riding's End-ride/Edit-copy dialogs (item 50, not started) completely unaffected. Instead, a plain ternary renders directly at the exact JSX position the `Clear draft` row already occupied (a direct child of the action card's `.stack`, the same nesting depth as every other action-card section, not one level deeper): either `<ConfirmDialog>` or the button row, never both, with the old separate sibling `<ConfirmDialog>` deleted outright. Because `.stack` defaults `align-items` to `stretch` (unoverridden), `ConfirmDialog`'s rendered box fills the same width the button row already did with no new CSS needed for stretching.
    - **Focus restoration** needed real rework, not a cosmetic change: `handleClearDraftCancel` previously called `clearDraftTriggerRef.current?.focus()` immediately, which only ever worked because the trigger was unconditionally always-mounted before this slice (only the dialog toggled). Under the ternary, the trigger genuinely unmounts while the dialog is open, so an immediate call at that exact synchronous point would target a stale/null ref. The previous single-purpose `clearDraftError`-keyed effect (whose sole job was focus restoration after a failed clear) was replaced with one unified, self-re-checking mechanism covering both Cancel/Escape and Confirm-failure: a `pendingClearDraftFocusRef` flag, set by `handleClearDraftCancel` and by `handleClearDraftConfirm`'s `.catch()` block, consumed by a single `useEffect` with no dependency array that re-checks the ref's live readiness (mounted **and** not disabled) on every render rather than consuming the request unconditionally on the first post-set commit — correct regardless of whether the triggering state change lands in one commit (Cancel/Escape's single synchronous close) or two (the async `.catch()`/`.finally()` pair, which need not land in the same commit, even though it empirically does today via this app's own promise-microtask-batching behaviour — the previous effect's own correctness quietly depended on that coincidence holding, which the new mechanism no longer needs).
    - **Test-anchor strategy**: no new `data-testid` was added. The "same slot" proof anchors on the "Change" `<details>` element itself, found via its visible text (`screen.getByText("Change", {exact:true}).closest("details").parentElement`, i.e. the `.planning-routing-summary` wrapper reached without ever naming that class in the test) and asserts its `nextElementSibling` holds the trigger when closed and _is_ the `alertdialog` element when open (`toBe`, proving nothing else was inserted) — mirroring this codebase's existing `nextElementSibling`-based precedent for an analogous inline confirmation (see item 50's own text) rather than inventing a new selector convention.
    - **A real, if narrow, existing-test staleness bug was found and fixed while running the pre-existing suite**, not merely anticipated: two of the 13 pre-existing `PlanningScreen.clearDraft.test.tsx` tests (`"Cancel preserves the draft exactly…"`, `"a clearDraft() rejection…permits retry with focus restored"`) captured `const trigger = clearDraftTriggerButton();` once, before the confirmation opened, and reused that reference afterward — valid before this slice (the trigger never unmounted), now stale once it genuinely does. Both were fixed to re-query the trigger after the state transition that remounts it; every other call site in the file already re-queried fresh and needed no change (confirmed by grep across every `clearDraftTriggerButton()`/`const trigger` use in the file).
    - Real-browser verification (`e2e/clearPlanningDraft.spec.ts`, a new dedicated phone-viewport test alongside the existing touch-target one): a DOM-structural check (`page.evaluate`, mirroring the Vitest `nextElementSibling` proof) confirms the confirmation occupies the trigger's exact slot when open and nothing else was inserted (`getByRole("button", {name:"Clear draft"})` has count 1, and it's the dialog's own confirm button); the rest of the action card (`Waypoint actions` group, `Calculate route` button) stays visible with the dialog open, and the dialog itself sits above the `Waypoints` heading; Cancel restores the trigger in the same slot, focused. Confirmed, by the same temporarily-reverted-production-code method used for item 48, to fail against the pre-fix sibling-dialog structure (`nextSiblingRole` resolves to `null` instead of `"alertdialog"`) before being relied on as regression evidence.
    - Files changed: `src/ui/planning/PlanningScreen.tsx`, `src/ui/settings/SettingsScreen.tsx`, `src/index.css`; tests: `src/ui/planning/PlanningScreen.clearDraft.test.tsx` (14 tests, +1), `src/ui/planning/PlanningScreen.test.tsx` (108 tests, one replaced), `src/ui/settings/SettingsScreen.test.tsx` (25 tests, +1), `e2e/planning.spec.ts`, `e2e/clearPlanningDraft.spec.ts` (+1 test). No routing, GPX, map, elevation, Riding, or storage-schema behaviour changed anywhere.

### Ride lifecycle

50. **Inline End-ride confirmation everywhere — done**
    - Applies the same in-place-morph treatment as item 49 to every End-ride/Discard trigger shipped by items 29 (Finish/End ride), 40 (Riding information and action proximity, which moved the active-tracking trigger to the top of the screen), 41 (Ride launcher session recovery), and 42 (Route-less free roam).
    - **Surfaces covered**, all six: `RidingScreen.tsx`'s active-tracking `.ride-end-ride-row`; `RidingScreen.tsx`'s resumable pre-ride panel (only rendered while `nav.currentFix` is truthy); `FreeRoamScreen.tsx`'s `.ride-end-ride-row`; and `RidingLauncher.tsx`'s three mutually-exclusive recovery panels (`resumable-route`, `resumable-free-roam`, `unresumable` → Discard). `RidingScreen.tsx`'s unrelated Edit-copy `ConfirmDialog`, `RidingRouteCompletionPanel`/Finish ride (confirmation-free, untouched), `SettingsScreen.tsx`'s API-key deletion, `PlanningScreen.tsx`'s Clear-draft (item 49, the reference pattern), and `RouteListItem.tsx`'s hand-rolled route-deletion confirmation (a live, deliberately-untouched "separate sibling, not a morph" precedent, confirmed out of scope by direct reading) are all unchanged.
    - **Action-slot architecture.** At each surface, a persistent wrapper `<div>` stays mounted for as long as its enclosing conditional branch is active; only its _children_ toggle between the trigger button (+ optional error paragraph) and `<ConfirmDialog>`, via one small render-helper function per file (`RidingScreen.tsx`'s and `FreeRoamScreen.tsx`'s `renderEndRideAction()`; `RidingLauncher.tsx`'s `renderClearAction()`, closing over the already-derived `clearAction` const with an explicit `if (!clearAction) return null;` guard, and now also driving each trigger's own label from `LAUNCHER_CLEAR_ACTION_COPY[...].confirmLabel` instead of a separately hardcoded string — confirmed char-for-char identical to the three pre-existing labels, eliminating a duplicated literal with no behaviour change). This deliberately differs from Planning's exact item-49 pattern (which swaps the _whole_ wrapper out): several of these sites have sibling content that must stay visible around the action (Resume-riding/Edit-copy buttons, panel heading/meta, Resume-route/Resume-free-roam buttons), and `.ride-end-ride-row` already carried a DOM-order contract (`RidingScreen.test.tsx`'s item-40 tests, `e2e/gradientColouring.spec.ts`) satisfied more simply by a persistent node than by hunting for a stable external landmark to anchor a full swap on. `.ride-end-ride-row` (RidingScreen's active branch, FreeRoamScreen) is reused unchanged — it already carried zero CSS. RidingScreen's idle/resumable panel (previously a bare fragment, no wrapper) gained a new `.ride-end-ride-panel-row stack` — kept a _distinct_ class from `.ride-end-ride-row`, since an existing test explicitly asserts the latter is absent in that state, and given `.stack` to preserve the 16px flex-gap the button/error previously got as direct children of `.ride-start-panel`'s own `.stack`. `RidingLauncher.tsx`'s three panels each gained a new `.ride-launcher-clear-row stack`, same gap-preservation reasoning (previously direct children of `<div className="panel stack">`). No other CSS was added or needed — confirmed by direct inspection that `.ride-end-ride-row` carried no rule at all before this slice.
    - **Why the shared lifecycle handlers stayed unchanged.** `performFinalizeRide` (RidingScreen, RidingScreen/FreeRoamScreen share the identical shape) and `performClearSession` (RidingLauncher) are untouched in their storage-clear-then-reset ordering, `onRideFinalized`-fires-only-after-success timing (with its own nested try/catch so a caller-side bug is logged, never misreported as a finalisation failure), and `isFinalizeActionPendingRef`/`isClearActionPendingRef` duplicate-submission guards — this slice is presentation/focus-management only. The only production changes inside these functions are: (a) each catch block now also sets a new pending-focus latch (RidingScreen: only for `source === "end"`, since Finish's own trigger never unmounts and needs no latch); (b) `setIsXConfirmOpen(false)` is otherwise byte-identical.
    - **Focus restoration.** Every Cancel/Escape handler's old direct, synchronous `ref.current?.focus()` call is unconditionally broken once its trigger genuinely unmounts (a stale/null ref at that exact point) — this is not an optional robustness upgrade, it reproduces item 49's own pre-fix bug verbatim. Fixed identically at all three call sites (`RidingScreen.tsx`'s `pendingEndRideFocusRef`, `FreeRoamScreen.tsx`'s `pendingEndRideFocusRef`, `RidingLauncher.tsx`'s `pendingClearFocusRef`): a `useRef(false)` latch set to `true` by the Cancel handler (before `setIsXConfirmOpen(false)`) and by the confirm-failure catch block, consumed by one `useEffect` with **no dependency array** that re-checks the ref's live readiness (mounted **and** not disabled) on every render — mirrors `PlanningScreen.tsx`'s `pendingClearDraftFocusRef` exactly. `RidingScreen.tsx`'s pre-existing combined `[finalizeError]`-deps effect was split in two: an unchanged-in-substance one scoped to `source === "finish"` (Finish's trigger never unmounts, so its existing mechanism needed no change), and the new no-deps latch effect for `source === "end"` only.
    - **Deterministic component evidence** (all extending, not duplicating, the three named files): `RidingScreen.finishEndRide.test.tsx` (20 tests) — both trigger locations replaced in their own action-slot position (`.ride-end-ride-row`/`.ride-end-ride-panel-row` `.contains()` the dialog, never both trigger and dialog simultaneously, proven via `getAllByRole("button", {name:"End ride"})` returning exactly the dialog's own confirm button) with surrounding content (route heading, next-manoeuvre status, map container; Resume-riding/Edit-copy buttons) staying mounted; Cancel/Escape restore focus to a freshly re-queried, re-enabled trigger; a storage failure remounts+focuses the trigger with the existing error and permits retry; a rapid double confirm click cannot double-finalise; Finish ride shows no alertdialog before or after its click, confirmed both explicitly and by its own unchanged completion-panel test. `FreeRoamScreen.endRide.test.tsx` (8 tests) — same slot/focus/failure/duplicate-submission proofs, plus heading/status/map staying mounted. `RidingLauncher.test.tsx` (24 tests) — all three panel variants (route End ride, free-roam End ride, Discard) each proven to replace the trigger inside `.ride-launcher-clear-row` with the rest of that panel (route heading, Resume route/Resume free roam button, the unresumable explanation text) staying visible, plus the pre-existing copy/failure/retry/pending/no-GPS assertions preserved. Two genuinely pre-existing stale-DOM-reference bugs were found and fixed while writing this coverage (mirroring item 49's own "two of the 13 pre-existing tests" precedent) — `RidingScreen.finishEndRide.test.tsx`'s `"cancelling or pressing Escape on the End-ride dialog never calls onRideFinalized"` and `RidingLauncher.test.tsx`'s `"Discard unfinished ride: cancel/failure preserve the row..."` both reused a button reference captured before a Cancel round-trip, which is now a detached node once the trigger genuinely unmounts; both re-query fresh. `RidingScreen.test.tsx`'s item-40 test `"renders the opened End-ride confirmation immediately after the top action row"` (renamed, rewritten) now asserts `.ride-end-ride-row.contains(dialog)` instead of a `nextElementSibling` equality that the new architecture structurally cannot satisfy.
    - **Real-browser evidence**, extending `e2e/ridingFinishAndEnd.spec.ts`, `e2e/freeRoam.spec.ts`, `e2e/ridingLauncher.spec.ts` (`page.evaluate`-based `.contains()` DOM proofs, mirroring `e2e/clearPlanningDraft.spec.ts`'s own `readClearDraftSlotRole` template) plus one out-of-the-named-list fix: `e2e/gradientColouring.spec.ts`'s own pre-existing `dialogFollowsEndRideRow` structural check (a real, existing assertion this architecture change breaks, confirmed via full-repo grep to be, together with the item-40 Vitest test, the only two `nextElementSibling`-based checks anywhere) rewritten to `.contains()`. New/extended proofs: active route Riding and active free roam replace their trigger in place with route status/heading/map staying visible, Cancel restoring focus to the remounted trigger; the unfinished-route, unfinished-free-roam and unresumable launcher panels each render their confirmation within that panel's own slot with `Resume route`/`Resume free roam`/the explanation text staying visible; a new `e2e/ridingFinishAndEnd.spec.ts` phone-viewport (390×844) test proves no horizontal overflow with the confirmation open and that both the trigger and Cancel stay real ≥44×44px touch targets, extending `e2e/freeRoam.spec.ts`'s pre-existing equivalent with the same slot/focus proof; `Finish ride` is confirmed to show no alertdialog at any point. Every existing storage-clear-then-reset, `onRideFinalized`, retry, and no-GPS-from-an-unresumed-launcher assertion in these files is preserved unchanged.
    - Files changed: `src/ui/riding/RidingScreen.tsx`, `src/ui/riding/FreeRoamScreen.tsx`, `src/ui/riding/RidingLauncher.tsx`; tests: `src/ui/riding/RidingScreen.test.tsx`, `src/ui/riding/RidingScreen.finishEndRide.test.tsx`, `src/ui/riding/FreeRoamScreen.endRide.test.tsx`, `src/ui/riding/RidingLauncher.test.tsx`, `e2e/ridingFinishAndEnd.spec.ts`, `e2e/freeRoam.spec.ts`, `e2e/ridingLauncher.spec.ts`, `e2e/gradientColouring.spec.ts`.

Version bumped to 0.3.38 (`package.json` and `package-lock.json`'s two matching `version` fields, hand-patched, following the established precedent).

51. **Pre-ride return to the Ride launcher — done**
    - Confirmed current behaviour: `App.tsx` has no "return to the launcher without finalising" path today — `ridingContent` (a `RidingContent` union with a `NONE_RIDING_CONTENT` sentinel) is only reset to `{kind:"none"}` by `handleRideFinalized` (a completed End/Finish ride) or an explicit re-selection; nothing lets the rider back out of a selected route's pre-ride screen without one of those.
    - Required outcome: add a clearly labelled pre-ride-only action (e.g. "Back to Ride options"), visible only in the idle/pre-ride/recovery panel state — never once GPS tracking is active, which already has its own End-ride lifecycle. Pressing it resets only `App.tsx`'s in-memory `ridingContent` pointer to `{kind:"none"}`, returning to whichever Ride launcher state storage actually reflects. It must never delete/modify the saved route, nor silently discard an unfinished route or free-roam session — a persisted, unfinished `rideState` row must still be correctly reflected by the launcher's resumable/unresumable state afterwards, exactly as a direct nav-tab-away-and-back already behaves per item 42's own reset mechanism. It must not start, stop, or otherwise touch geolocation, camera, or wake-lock state. Reuse `App.tsx`'s existing reset-to-launcher mechanism from item 42 if it fits this narrower pre-ride-only trigger, rather than inventing a second, parallel reset path.
    - Required coverage: a component/App-level test proving the action only renders pre-ride, returns to the correct launcher state without touching persisted storage, and issues no geolocation calls.
    - **Implementation.** A new `.btn-secondary "Back to Ride options"` button was added inside `RidingScreen.tsx`'s existing `.ride-start-panel`, directly after the primary Start/Resume button and before Edit copy — placed inside the pre-existing `nav.geolocationStatus === "idle"` branch, so it covers both the clean ("Start riding") and resumable ("Resume riding") pre-ride states for free and is structurally absent the instant status leaves idle (watching/error, including a transient GPS error mid-ride), with no new condition needed beyond its placement. It is disabled while `activeFinalizeSource !== null` — a deliberate addition beyond this item's own literal required-test list: the resumable state's inline End-ride row and this new button are both direct children of the same panel, simultaneously mounted, and `performFinalizeRide` is async and doesn't close its own confirmation state until after `nav.finish()` resolves — without this guard a rider could confirm "End ride" then immediately tap "Back to Ride options" before the storage write settled, unmounting the screen mid-write. This mirrors the End-ride trigger's own identical `disabled` condition and does not attempt to close the pre-existing, separately out-of-scope version of the same race reachable via an ordinary main-nav tab click during finalize (closing that would require touching `handleNavigate`, which this item explicitly does not).
    - `App.tsx` gained a new optional `RidingScreen` prop, `onReturnToRideLauncher`, wired to a new `handleReturnToRideLauncher` handler. Both `handleReturnToRideLauncher` and the pre-existing `handleRideFinalized` now delegate to one shared two-line private helper, `resetRidingContentToLauncher` (`setRidingContent(NONE_RIDING_CONTENT); notifyNewRideContent();`) — `handleRideFinalized`'s own behaviour is unchanged, only its body became a delegation. `handleRideFinalized`'s doc comment was corrected: it previously claimed clearing `ridingContent` "(never elsewhere)" was its own sole responsibility, which was already slightly stale (the pre-existing free-roam branch of `handleNavigate` also clears it) — it now records all three distinct reset paths and the crucial distinction that only the finalize path follows an already-completed persisted-storage clear, while the other two (this new action, and `handleNavigate`'s free-roam nav-away case) leave a still-unfinished session's storage row completely untouched, letting `RidingLauncher`'s own re-hydration keep reflecting it correctly. `RidingLauncher` needed no changes at all — it already re-reads `getActiveRideState()`/`getRoute()` purely from storage on every mount, taking no session-describing props, so simply causing it to re-render is sufficient. `handleNavigate`, `checkFreeRoamConflict`, `handleResumeRoute`, `handleOpenFreeRoam`, `blockedRouteOpenReason` and its state, and all `FreeRoamScreen` wiring are unchanged — confirmed by direct reading, not merely left alone by omission; `blockedRouteOpenReason` needs no handling from this new action since both its setters live inside `handleOpenRoute`/`handleRouteSaved`'s conflict branch and every path reaching `ridingContent.kind === "route"` runs through their own success branch, which already clears it first. No storage, geolocation, camera, or wake-lock call exists anywhere on the new code path — the new code touches only React state.
    - **A real, pre-existing behaviour surfaced while writing the storage-unchanged tests, not caused by this change.** Merely mounting `RidingScreen` (or the launcher's own "Resume route" transition into it) on an already-resumable `rideState` row already normalises/expands several of that row's fields (`cameraMode`, `wakeLockDesired`, `completionArmed`, elevation-view fields, etc.) via `useRideNavigation`'s own mount-time hydration/persistence effect — entirely unrelated to this item. Both the new component-level and e2e "no storage mutation" tests snapshot the row only once this settles (after the pre-ride panel itself is visible), so they prove precisely what this item requires — that pressing "Back to Ride options" itself causes no further write — without being confused by that pre-existing, separate mechanism.
    - **Test evidence.** `RidingScreen.test.tsx` gained a new `"Back to Ride options (item 51)"` describe block (8 tests): placement/DOM order directly after Start/Resume and before Edit copy in both pre-ride states; absence once watching and through a transient GPS error; clicking it in both pre-ride states calls the prop exactly once with no `watchPosition` call, no camera-spy call-count change, and no `onRideFinalized` call; the still-unfinished row is left exactly as settled; and the button is disabled while an End-ride finalize is genuinely in flight (a pending, externally-rejectable mock of `clearActiveRideState`) and re-enabled once that finalize fails. `App.test.tsx` gained a new `"App — Back to Ride options (item 51)"` describe block (2 tests) proving the clean-route flow (opened directly from Routes, returns to the empty launcher, route and storage untouched, scroll reset fires, no geolocation watch) and the unfinished-route flow (resumed via the launcher's own "Resume route", returned via the new button, launcher shows the same route with "Resume route"/"End ride" again, storage byte-identical, no geolocation watch) end-to-end through the real component tree — using a `navigator` stub that overrides only `geolocation.watchPosition` while explicitly preserving `navigator.onLine` (read as a primitive, not spread, since `Navigator` is a class instance and ESLint's `no-misused-spread` correctly flagged a naive spread attempt). `e2e/ridingLauncher.spec.ts` gained 3 new tests (fresh route; persisted/resumed route; a 390px-viewport touch-target/no-overflow check across both pre-ride states), introducing one new file-local `installGeolocationWatchCounter`/`readWatchPositionCallCount` pair (a transparent `page.addInitScript` wrapper around `navigator.geolocation.watchPosition`) since no such mechanism existed anywhere in this repo's e2e suite before this slice — kept local, not added to `e2e/support/`, per this file's own established no-shared-helpers convention (CLAUDE.md item 25). Each new test/assertion set was confirmed, by temporarily removing only the new button from `RidingScreen.tsx` and re-running, to fail with the predicted symptom (6 of the 8 new `RidingScreen.test.tsx` cases failed — the two "absent" cases correctly still passed) before the button was restored and the full suite re-verified green.
    - Files changed: `src/App.tsx`, `src/ui/riding/RidingScreen.tsx`; tests: `src/App.test.tsx`, `src/ui/riding/RidingScreen.test.tsx`, `e2e/ridingLauncher.spec.ts`. No CSS, storage-schema, GPX, routing, or `FreeRoamScreen` behaviour changed anywhere.
    - Verification: `npm run format`, `npm run lint`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run build`, `npm test` — 2435/2435 across 128 files, `npm run e2e` — 135/135 across both the `chromium` and `android-chrome` projects, `npm run format:check` run last, immediately before committing — all pass. Version bumped to 0.3.39 (`package.json` and `package-lock.json`'s two matching `version` fields, hand-patched, following the established precedent).

Physical Android verification has not happened either.

### Map camera controls

52. **Planning zoom controls — done**
    - Confirmed current behaviour (pre-implementation): Planning's existing `Locate me`/`North-up` controls live in `.planning-map-controls`, `position: absolute; top: 8px; right: 8px`, 48px circular buttons. No zoom in/out control existed anywhere in the app before this slice — confirmed by an exhaustive grep of `src/ui/` and `src/map/` for "zoom": every hit was MapLibre's own camera-state tracking, the existing zoom-interpolated line-width policy (`routeWidthPolicy.ts`, item 23), or marker/badge zoom-band logic — none of it a manual zoom button. Zooming was native pinch/scroll only.
    - **Architecture.** A fourth explicit camera-command prop/type pair was added to `MapView.tsx`, mirroring the existing `boundsTarget`/`centreTarget`/`orientNorthTarget` trio exactly: `zoomTarget?: ZoomCameraTarget | null` (`{delta: number; requestId: string}`), deduped by `requestId` not value via a new `lastAppliedZoomRequestIdRef` (reset in the map-creation effect's existing reset block alongside the other three) and a new `useEffect` mirroring `centreTarget`'s own — no eager `getZoom()` read-back, since `changeZoomBy` always eases and the existing `onCameraSettled`/`moveend` handler already catches up once the ease finishes. `MapLibreLike` gained one new narrow method, `changeZoomBy(delta: number): void`, implemented in `MapLibreAdapter` as `this.map.easeTo({ zoom: this.map.getZoom() + delta, duration: ZOOM_EASE_DURATION_MS, essential: true })` — mirroring `centreOn`'s own "omit every field but the one that's changing, let MapLibre's own `easeTo` semantics leave everything else genuinely untouched" contract, deliberately never routed through the broader `setCamera` (which always writes bearing/pitch explicitly and exists for a different contract). A new `ZOOM_EASE_DURATION_MS = 300` constant is its own named constant even though no test pins its numeric value, matching this file's established "distinct constant per camera-motion concern" convention already used for `FOLLOW_EASE_DURATION_MS`/`CENTRE_EASE_DURATION_MS`. MapLibre's own zoom setter clamps the resulting value to the style's min/max zoom internally, satisfying "let MapLibre enforce its own valid zoom limits" with no clamping logic of this app's own.
    - **Planning wiring.** `PlanningScreen.tsx` gained `PLANNING_ZOOM_STEP = 1` (MapLibre's own already-established single-level step, never a configurable product setting) and two `useCallback` handlers, `handleZoomIn`/`handleZoomOut`, each issuing `setZoomTarget({ delta: ±PLANNING_ZOOM_STEP, requestId: generateId() })`. The existing inline `onUserCameraInteraction` arrow (three statements: `hasManualCameraActionRef.current = true; hasManualGestureRef.current = true; setIsCameraSettled(false);`) was factored into a new shared `noteManualCameraControl` callback, called both by that prop and by both zoom handlers — **not** by redefining `MapLibreLike.onUserCameraInteraction` itself, whose contract stays gesture-only (a zoom press is a real but programmatic MapView command, so this is an explicit Planning-level state transition invoked directly). A zoom press therefore has the exact same consequences as a genuine gesture: it marks manual camera control, blocks a still-pending fresh-session/restored/seeded camera fit from later applying, makes a later Locate-me press use recentre-only behaviour rather than repeating the one-time regional box fit, and marks the camera unsettled (disabling the crosshair-callout placement button) until the resulting short ease genuinely settles. **`hasManualGestureRef` decision, deliberately made, not merely defaulted:** this ref's own pre-existing doc comment restricted it to "only a genuine pan/pinch/rotate/pitch gesture — never a Locate-me tap, successful or not", specifically because a failed Locate-me establishes no camera view at all. A zoom press is different from a Locate-me tap in exactly the relevant way: it synchronously and genuinely moves the camera the instant it's pressed, exactly like a gesture, so `noteManualCameraControl` sets `hasManualGestureRef` too — required by this item's own "consistent with a pinch/pan gesture" wording, and by `handleLocateMe`'s own `hadEstablishedViewFromGesture` check, which reads specifically this ref (not `hasManualCameraActionRef`) to decide whether a later Locate-me press may still repeat the one-time regional fit. Every doc comment enumerating the plain-callback writers of `hasManualCameraActionRef`/`hasManualGestureRef` (including the block comment restricting these two refs to a single reader effect, for the underlying React-Compiler `react-hooks/immutability` lint rule) was updated to name the new handlers; `handleClearDraftConfirm`'s own "third caller of this same discipline" note became "a fourth caller" to stay numerically accurate against the now-three-not-two plain-callback writers it follows.
    - **CSS.** A new `.planning-map-zoom-controls` mirrors `.planning-map-controls` exactly (`position: absolute; top: 8px; z-index: 5; display: flex; flex-direction: column; gap: 8px`) but `left: 8px` instead of `right: 8px`, reusing `.planning-map-control` unchanged for individual button shape/touch geometry (48×48 circular) — no new button class was needed.
