The visible glyphs are `+` and the proper minus sign `−` (U+2212 MINUS SIGN, not a hyphen), both with explicit `aria-label`s `Zoom in`/`Zoom out`; neither button is ever `disabled` (mirrors North-up). **A real, necessary CSS fix found and applied, not merely a nice-to-have:** `.planning-map-status-overlay` (the locate-failed/warning-selected/route-feature-selected status-message overlay) previously sat at `left: 8px`, sharing `z-index: 5` with the new cluster and rendering as a later DOM sibling — without widening it, an active status message would render on top of the new top-left buttons. `.planning-map-status-overlay`'s `left` was widened to `64px` (mirroring its own pre-existing `right: 64px` clearance for `.planning-map-controls`, the identical `8+48+8` arithmetic), and its doc comment updated to describe both clearances.
    - **A required, non-optional e2e fix found during implementation, not left for later:** three existing test files tapped the Planning map at x-coordinates that collide with the new top-left cluster's `x:[8,56]` bounding column, which (once the cluster exists) makes Playwright's own click-interception check fail rather than silently mis-clicking — confirmed empirically by temporarily reverting each fix and re-running, reproducing exactly `<div class="planning-map-zoom-controls">…</div> intercepts pointer events`. Fixed: `e2e/planning.spec.ts`'s two `TAP_X_POSITIONS` arrays (the item-34 double-digit-waypoint test and the ordinal-10-badge test) had their first element widened from `40` to `70`, with both tests' existing "clear of `.planning-map-controls`" doc comments extended to also name the new cluster; `e2e/distanceBadges.spec.ts`'s two `{x: 60, y: 100}` taps (only 4px clear of the new cluster's right edge) were widened to `{x: 80, y: 100}`; `e2e/lowZoomLegibility.spec.ts`'s 14-waypoint dense-fixture loop (`x: 40 + (index % 7) * 35`) had its base offset widened from `40` to `70`. No other collision exists anywhere in the e2e suite — confirmed by grepping every `mapContainer.click({position:...})` call site, including computed/dynamic positions, across all spec files.
    - **Tests.** `mapAdapter.test.ts` gained two `changeZoomBy` tests proving the underlying `easeTo` call carries only `zoom`/`essential` (never `center`/`bearing`/`pitch`/`offset`) for both a positive and negative delta, relative to the fake's own `getZoom()` return value — mirroring `centreOn`'s existing field-omission tests exactly, deliberately never asserting the literal duration value (matching this file's existing convention). `MapView.test.tsx` gained a `changeZoomBySpy` on its mock factory and four new tests mirroring the existing `centreTarget`/`orientNorthTarget` blocks: apply-once-ready, no-reapply-on-identical-requestId, reapply-on-a-second-requestId-with-an-identical-delta (proving two consecutive Zoom-in presses both apply — dedup is by `requestId`, not value), and a "retry preserves and re-applies a still-current zoomTarget" case in the existing style-failure/fallback/manual-retry describe block. `PlanningScreen.test.tsx` gained a `changeZoomBySpy` and a new `describe("Zoom controls", ...)` block (7 tests): both buttons render with the correct accessible names; a Zoom-in press calls `changeZoomBy(1)` and touches none of `setCamera`/`centreOn`/`fitBounds`; a Zoom-out press calls `changeZoomBy(-1)` likewise; two consecutive Zoom-in presses each issue a fresh call; a zoom press disables the crosshair-callout exactly like a genuine gesture already does, re-enabling once the map reports settled (mirroring the existing gesture-based test); a zoom press never calls the injected `requestApproximateLocation` and never shows "Locating…"; a race test using a deferred `requestApproximateLocation` promise proves a Zoom-in press issued before the promise resolves prevents the fresh-session bounds-fit (`fitBounds`) from ever applying once it later resolves, while the position dot itself still updates (proving only the fit, not the whole request, was blocked); and existing waypoint DOM state is unaffected by a zoom press. The 11 other pre-existing `MapLibreLike` test doubles (`App.test.tsx`, `PlanningScreen.clearDraft/.draftHydration/.reverseRoute/.saveAutosaveRace.test.tsx`, `FreeRoamScreen.test.tsx`/`.endRide.test.tsx`, `RidingScreen.test.tsx`/`.completionArming/.finishEndRide/.closedLoopCompletion.test.tsx`) each gained a one-line mechanical no-op `changeZoomBy: () => undefined,` addition — no behavioural change to Riding/free-roam anywhere, confirmed by an exhaustive `grep -rln "MapLibreLike" src` cross-check that no 12th/hidden implementor exists.
    - `e2e/planning.spec.ts`'s existing "reloading Planning after placing waypoints and a route name recovers the same draft..." test (item 35's own reload-recovery test, the one place a restored waypoint set's one-time camera fit is already proved) was extended in place, immediately after the existing marker/fit assertions: pressing Zoom in changes `data-camera-zoom` to a strictly greater value (polled for a genuine change, matching this file's own established CI-contention-aware convention, never a fixed wait); pressing Zoom out afterwards changes it to a strictly lower value than after zoom-in; `data-camera-center` stays within the file's existing `1e-4`-degree tolerance across both presses; `data-camera-bearing`/`data-camera-pitch` stay byte-identical; the Locate-me button's text never becomes "Locating…" and no "Your location could not be determined." text ever appears. The existing `test.describe("phone viewport", ...)` layout test gained bounding-box assertions for both new buttons (each ≥44×44px, fully within `.planning-map-container`, no intersection between the two zoom buttons themselves, none with the Locate-me/North-up cluster, none with the attribution). Every new/changed e2e assertion was confirmed, by temporarily reverting the relevant production code (the tap-position fix, and the whole `.planning-map-zoom-controls` JSX block) and re-running, to fail with the exact predicted symptom before being relied on as a regression detector.
    - Files changed: `src/map/mapAdapter.ts`, `src/map/MapView.tsx`, `src/ui/planning/PlanningScreen.tsx`, `src/index.css`; tests: `src/map/mapAdapter.test.ts`, `src/map/MapView.test.tsx`, `src/ui/planning/PlanningScreen.test.tsx`, plus the 11 mechanical no-op stub files listed above; `e2e/planning.spec.ts`, `e2e/distanceBadges.spec.ts`, `e2e/lowZoomLegibility.spec.ts`. No change to Locate-me's or North-up's own semantics or position, no routing/GPX/waypoint-history/warning behaviour, and item 53 (Riding/free-roam zoom controls) was not touched.
    - Verification: `npm run format`, `npm run lint`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run build`, `npm test` — 2449/2449 across 128 files, `npm run e2e` — 135/135 across both the `chromium` (121) and `android-chrome` (14) projects, `npm run format:check` run last, immediately before committing — all pass. Version bumped to 0.3.40 (`package.json` and `package-lock.json`'s two matching `version` fields, hand-patched, following the established precedent).
    - **Real-device acceptance checklist (none of this has been checked yet — no physical device is available in this environment).** All evidence above is automated: Vitest unit/component tests and Playwright's Chromium (`chromium`) and Chromium-emulated Pixel-7 (`android-chrome`) projects — neither is a substitute for a genuine physical device, per this file's own established distinction (item 25). iPhone (installed Home Screen PWA, portrait orientation): both buttons visible, legible and comfortably tappable at the top-left of the Planning map; a clear, deliberate gap between Zoom in and Zoom out and between the two clusters; each press changes zoom by one comfortable step with no perceptible lag or jank; no interference with waypoint placement (the crosshair/"Add waypoint here" flow), Locate me, North-up, the status-overlay messages, or the map attribution; repeated rapid presses behave sensibly; a zoom press after a restored/seeded-waypoint camera fit does not disturb it. Android (physical device, distinct from the `android-chrome` Chromium-emulated Playwright project): the same checklist, not yet attempted at all.
    - **Post-deployment acceptance note (general, non-itemised).** On 18 August 2026 the user reported that the deployed Planning zoom controls work very well — zooming behaves correctly with an already rotated map and with a pitched or skewed perspective, without disturbing those camera properties. This is informal, general confirmation only — it does not identify a device or platform, and does not individually confirm any single item in the real-device acceptance checklist above. That itemised checklist remains the authoritative, not-yet-individually-verified record for item 52; this note must not be read as satisfying it.

53. **Route Riding and free-roam zoom controls, with follow-zoom persistence — done**
    - Confirmed pre-implementation behaviour: Riding/free-roam `North-up`/`Follow` controls lived in `.ride-map-control`, each individually `position: absolute; right: 12px`, with `--north-up` at `bottom: 72px` and `--follow` at `bottom: 12px` — a bottom-right cluster with no wrapper container at all (unlike Planning's own `.planning-map-controls`/`.planning-map-zoom-controls` container-plus-shared-button-class split), so there was no `gap`/shared `z-index` mechanism to reuse directly. No zoom control existed in Riding/free-roam either. `rideCamera.ts`'s `followCommand()` hardcoded `zoom: NAVIGATION_ZOOM` (16) into every following camera command, with no per-rider zoom concept anywhere in the reducer.
    - **Settled interaction decision.** Pressing Zoom in/out while Follow is active keeps Follow engaged: `Follow my location` stays `aria-pressed="true"`, no `"Map follow paused."` toast appears, and later accepted GPS fixes keep recentring/rotating the followed camera at the rider's selected zoom. This is deliberate — zooming while still being followed is a normal riding action, unlike a genuine manual pan/rotate/pitch gesture, which continues to pause Follow exactly as before. The zoom handlers never call `camera.reportUserInteraction`, and `MapLibreLike.onUserCameraInteraction` keeps its existing gesture-only contract untouched — mirrors item 52's own `noteManualCameraControl`-is-a-separate-explicit-call-not-a-redefinition precedent, adapted for Riding's Follow concept, which Planning has no analogue of.
    - **Camera-reducer architecture (`src/ui/riding/rideCamera.ts`).** `NAVIGATION_ZOOM`'s declaration moved above `RideCameraState`/`INITIAL_RIDE_CAMERA_STATE` (a genuine forward-reference fix, not cosmetic — `INITIAL_RIDE_CAMERA_STATE` is a module-level `const` that now depends on it, and JS's temporal-dead-zone rules would otherwise throw at module load). `RideCameraState` gained `followZoomLevel: number` — the zoom used for the next following camera command: the default `NAVIGATION_ZOOM`, a value chosen via the zoom controls, or (once reconciled) the real settled zoom after MapLibre's own min/max clamping. `followCommand()` gained a third `zoomLevel` parameter, threaded from `state.followZoomLevel` at both its call sites (`follow-requested`'s fresh-coordinate branch, `fresh-fix`). Two new `RideCameraEvent` variants: `"follow-zoom-changed"` (dispatched by the zoom buttons; accumulates `delta` into `followZoomLevel` **unconditionally regardless of current mode** — never gated on `"following"` — since the actual on-screen zoom change always travels through MapView's separate `zoomTarget`/`changeZoomBy` channel, never through this event's `command`, so applying it unconditionally is simplest and gives the best continuity if the rider zooms while free-panning then re-engages Follow; never produces a command, never pauses); `"follow-zoom-settled"` (dispatched only from the hook's `camera-settled` handling, never from a button — reconciles `followZoomLevel` to MapLibre's real settled zoom, but **only** while `mode === "following" && !awaitingFreshFix`). The `awaitingFreshFix` guard is essential, not optional: immediately after a `restore` into `"following"` (or right after `requestFollow()` with no fresh fix yet), `mode` is already `"following"` but the _displayed_ map is still MapView's own pre-ride overview-fit-to-bounds (route Riding) or a stale/no camera (free roam) — a `camera-settled` event firing from that unrelated overview-fit settling (a much lower zoom than any follow zoom) must never overwrite a just-restored/chosen `followZoomLevel`; this mirrors the same distinction `hasActionableCameraTarget`/`nextCameraTarget !== null` already draws elsewhere in the hook. Every existing state-literal branch (`user-interaction`, `north-up-requested`, `follow-requested`'s pending branch) now preserves `followZoomLevel: state.followZoomLevel` rather than dropping it, so a temporary departure from Follow never forgets the chosen zoom within the same mounted session; `route-opened` still resets to the full `INITIAL_RIDE_CAMERA_STATE` (so `followZoomLevel` returns to `NAVIGATION_ZOOM` on End/Finish ride or a new route, since `resetCamera()`/`performFinalizeRide` already dispatch this event). `restore` into `"following"` reads `event.zoom`, defaulting to `NAVIGATION_ZOOM` when null or non-finite (`Number.isFinite` guard); `restore` into `"free"` sets `followZoomLevel` to `NAVIGATION_ZOOM` (no meaningful prior follow zoom to restore into free mode).
    - **Hook wiring (`useRideCamera.ts`, `useFreeRoamCamera.ts`).** Both hooks' `hookReducer`s replace the previous "camera-settled is a no-op unless mode is free" early return with one that always routes through the pure reducer via `rideCameraReducer(state.camera, {type:"follow-zoom-settled", zoom: event.zoom})`, then only rebuilds `freeCameraPosition` while `mode === "free"` exactly as before — with a reference-equality bail-out (`zoomReconciled === state.camera ? state : {...state, camera: zoomReconciled}`) preserving the old "true no-op, no re-render" behaviour whenever nothing genuinely changed (the common steady-state case), avoiding an extra re-render on every ordinary `moveend` while following. `persistableCameraState`'s `"following"` branch, which previously always wrote `zoom: null`, now writes `zoom: state.camera.followZoomLevel`. Both hooks gained `zoomTarget: ZoomCameraTarget | null` state and a stable `requestZoom(delta)` action, mirroring `PlanningScreen.tsx`'s exact `handleZoomIn`/`handleZoomOut` convention — `generateId()` from `src/platform/idGenerator.ts` for each `ZoomCameraTarget.requestId` (Planning's own established, separate mechanism for this MapView-level dedup prop; not the `nextCameraRequestIdRef` monotonic counter used for `RideCameraCommand`'s own north-up/follow `requestId`, whose "never `crypto.randomUUID()`" rule is specific to that different contract). `useFreeRoamCamera.ts`'s `initialFramingCommand` (the synthetic pre-fix framing command that avoids MapLibre's raw default world view on a resumed-into-following session) now sources its zoom directly from `restoredCameraState.zoom` — mirroring exactly how `bearingDegrees` already sources from `restoredLastReliableBearingDegrees` as a raw prop rather than through `state.camera`, avoiding any hook-internal dispatch/render lag — falling back to `NAVIGATION_ZOOM` when absent/null/non-finite.
    - **Storage — broadened, not a new field.** `StoredCameraState.zoom` (`src/storage/mapping.ts`) and `StoredRouteRideState.cameraZoom`/`StoredFreeRoamRideState.cameraZoom` (`src/storage/db.ts`) keep their exact existing shape and meaning while `mode`/`cameraMode` is `"free"` (the settled pan zoom, as before) but now _also_ carry the rider's selected follow zoom while `"following"` — broadening one field's contract rather than adding a second, overlapping stored zoom value, per this file's own established additive-field convention. `toStoredRideState`/`fromStoredRideState`/`toStoredFreeRoamState`/`fromStoredFreeRoamState` needed **zero code changes**: they already pass `cameraState.zoom`/`stored.cameraZoom` through verbatim, and the reducer/hook layer is entirely responsible for what the value means in `"following"` mode and for applying the `NAVIGATION_ZOOM` default on restore — this storage layer never depends on a UI camera constant. No Dexie `version()` bump.
    - **Screen wiring.** `RidingScreen.tsx`/`FreeRoamScreen.tsx` each gained a local `RIDING_ZOOM_STEP = 1` (mirroring `PlanningScreen.tsx`'s `PLANNING_ZOOM_STEP`) and `handleZoomIn`/`handleZoomOut` callbacks, destructuring `requestZoom` off the camera hook result first (`const { requestZoom: cameraRequestZoom } = camera;`) before using it inside `useCallback`, matching this file's own pre-existing `{start: navStart}`/`{requestFollow: cameraRequestFollow}` destructuring convention (needed to satisfy `react-hooks/exhaustive-deps`, since a bare `camera.requestZoom` property access inside the callback body would otherwise demand the whole `camera` object in the dependency array). `<MapView>` gained `zoomTarget={camera.zoomTarget}` in both screens, alongside the existing `cameraTarget`/`suppressInitialOverviewFit`/`onUserCameraInteraction`/`onCameraSettled`. A new `.ride-map-zoom-controls` div (Zoom in/out, gated on the same `nav.geolocationStatus === "watching"` condition as the existing camera controls — never on the idle pre-ride overview or during a geolocation error) sits alongside a new `.ride-map-camera-controls` div wrapping the existing North-up/Follow buttons (unchanged JSX bodies/`aria-label`/`aria-pressed`/`onClick` wiring, only the wrapping and position changed). The `−` glyph is the literal U+2212 MINUS SIGN character, matching Planning's own item-52 convention exactly, not a hyphen.
    - **CSS reconciliation — old bottom-right description superseded.** `.ride-map-control` lost its own `position: absolute; right: 12px` (now shape-only: size/border/background/colour, reused by all four buttons); `.ride-map-control--north-up`/`--follow` lost their own `bottom` offsets; a new `.ride-map-control--zoom` modifier supplies the same plain bold-glyph styling as `--north-up`'s "N". Two new wrapper classes carry the actual positioning, mirroring Planning's own `.planning-map-controls`/`.planning-map-zoom-controls` split: `.ride-map-zoom-controls` (`position: absolute; top: 8px; left: 8px; z-index: 5; display: flex; flex-direction: column; gap: 8px`) and `.ride-map-camera-controls` (identical shape, `right: 8px` instead of `left: 8px`) — replacing the old bottom-right, no-wrapper, no-gap arrangement this item's own earlier text described. Two now-stale comments were corrected for accuracy, matching this file's established "CSS comments stay precisely correct" convention: `.map-attribution`'s (previously "max-width leaves clear of Riding's two 48px, right:12 camera control buttons," now noting both Riding clusters are top-anchored and clear of its bottom-left corner by construction) and `.planning-map-controls`'s (previously "Riding's equivalent controls ... stay inline/bottom-right and are intentionally left untouched here," now noting Riding/free-roam's own top-right cluster shares the corner convention while staying its own class). `src/map/MapView.tsx`'s `ZoomCameraTarget`/`zoomTarget` doc comments were broadened from "Planning-only; Riding never supplies this" to describe a generic command shared by Planning, Riding and free roam alike — no code change in that file, since the `zoomTarget` effect/dedup logic was already fully generic.
    - **Tests.** `rideCamera.test.ts` (69 tests, +21 from 48): `followingState()`'s default gained `followZoomLevel: NAVIGATION_ZOOM`; new coverage for `follow-zoom-changed` (while following, while free — pinning the "always accumulate regardless of mode" decision explicitly), `follow-zoom-settled` (reconciliation while following-and-settled, no-op while `awaitingFreshFix`, no-op while free/overview, reference-equality on an unchanged value), zoom preserved across `user-interaction`/`north-up-requested`/pending `follow-requested`, zoom reused (not reset to `NAVIGATION_ZOOM`) across a leave-then-rejoin-Follow round trip, `route-opened` resetting it, `restore` with a valid/null/non-finite persisted zoom, and several consecutive `fresh-fix` events never resetting a previously-set zoom. A new `src/ui/riding/useRideCamera.test.ts` (10 tests) — no focused hook-level test file existed for this hook before this slice — covers baseline mode/Follow/pause/reset behaviour plus `requestZoom` issuing distinct `zoomTarget` identities, a subsequent fresh fix reflecting the requested zoom, `reportCameraSettled` reconciling `persistableCameraState.zoom` while following, and a restored following session using its persisted zoom for its first command. `useFreeRoamCamera.test.ts` (21 tests, +6 from 15) gained the equivalent zoom-controls coverage plus `initialFramingCommand`'s zoom sourcing from `restoredCameraState.zoom` (present/finite, and falling back to `NAVIGATION_ZOOM` when null or non-finite) and a brand-new session defaulting to `NAVIGATION_ZOOM`. `src/storage/mapping.test.ts` (65 tests, +2 from 63) gained round-trip tests proving a `"following"` camera state's non-default zoom survives `toStoredRideState`/`fromStoredRideState` and `toStoredFreeRoamState`/`fromStoredFreeRoamState` unchanged, alongside the pre-existing `zoom: null` case (left intact, since a following state with no zoom yet is still valid). `RidingScreen.test.tsx` (142 tests, +7 from 135) and `FreeRoamScreen.test.tsx` (14 tests, +4 from 10) each gained a `describe("Zoom controls (backlog item 53)", ...)` block: absence pre-ride/during a geolocation error, correct accessible names/glyphs, `changeZoomBy(1)`/`changeZoomBy(-1)` on press, Follow staying pressed with no paused toast and no `setCamera` call from a zoom press, and a genuine manual gesture still pausing Follow unaffected by the new controls — both screens' `buildStubMapFactory()` stubs gained a `changeZoomBySpy` (and, for `FreeRoamScreen.test.tsx`, `onCameraSettled`/`triggerCameraSettled` capture, mirroring `RidingScreen.test.tsx`'s already-fuller stub).
    - **Playwright.** `e2e/layout.spec.ts` gained a new test proving all four controls (Zoom in/out, North-up, Follow) are fully inside `.ride-map-container`, each a real ≥44×44px touch target, a real gap within each cluster, no intersection between the top-left and top-right clusters, no intersection with `.map-attribution`, and no horizontal overflow at the 390×844 viewport; the pre-existing attribution/camera-buttons test's bottom-right collision checks are now a baseline sanity check rather than a narrow proof, with a comment pointing at the new test as the real geometry proof. `e2e/freeRoam.spec.ts`'s existing 390px-viewport test gained the equivalent four-control geometry proof. Both `e2e/ridingCamera.spec.ts` and `e2e/freeRoam.spec.ts` gained a new end-to-end test ("zoom while followed persists across a later GPS fix, storage, reload and Resume riding/free roam") proving, in order: the followed camera settling; a Zoom-in press producing a genuinely greater `data-camera-zoom` with `data-camera-center`/`-bearing`/`-pitch` unchanged; Follow staying `aria-pressed="true"` with no paused-toast text; a later accepted GPS fix re-centring the camera while the selected zoom holds; the zoom genuinely committed to IndexedDB (polled via `readActiveRideStateRow`/`readSavedRouteId` from `e2e/support/rideStateDb.ts`, never a fixed sleep, per this file's own established accepted-fix-versus-Dexie-write race discipline); a real reload landing on Routes/the empty launcher (never silently resuming GPS) with the zoom controls absent until an explicit Resume; and the restored zoom holding through the first fresh fix and a further one beyond it. `e2e/freeRoam.spec.ts` gained local `numbersClose`/`centresClose`/`isFullyWithin`/`intersects` helpers mirroring `ridingCamera.spec.ts`'s/`layout.spec.ts`'s own identical ones, duplicated per this file's established no-shared-e2e-helpers-across-specs convention. Both new zoom-persistence e2e tests were confirmed, by stashing every production change and running them against the reverted pre-change build, to fail for the intended reason (`locator.click: Test timeout... waiting for getByRole('button', { name: 'Zoom in' })`, the button not existing yet) before being relied on as regression detectors.
    - **Files changed:** `src/ui/riding/rideCamera.ts`, `src/ui/riding/useRideCamera.ts`, `src/ui/riding/useFreeRoamCamera.ts`, `src/ui/riding/RidingScreen.tsx`, `src/ui/riding/FreeRoamScreen.tsx`, `src/storage/db.ts`, `src/storage/mapping.ts`, `src/map/MapView.tsx` (doc comments only), `src/index.css`; tests: `src/ui/riding/rideCamera.test.ts`, `src/ui/riding/useRideCamera.test.ts` (new), `src/ui/riding/useFreeRoamCamera.test.ts`, `src/storage/mapping.test.ts`, `src/ui/riding/RidingScreen.test.tsx`, `src/ui/riding/FreeRoamScreen.test.tsx`, `e2e/layout.spec.ts`, `e2e/freeRoam.spec.ts`, `e2e/ridingCamera.spec.ts`. Item 54 and the immersive-Riding-redesign items (55–58) were not touched.
    - **Verification.** `npm run format`, `npm run lint`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run build`, `npm test` — 2499/2499 across 129 files, `npm run e2e` — 138/138 across both the `chromium` (124) and `android-chrome` (14) projects (run at reduced worker concurrency for a reliable count; two isolated flakes were observed once each under this environment's default heavy parallel load — `e2e/lowZoomLegibility.spec.ts`'s unrelated Planning-only keyboard-zoom test, and, in a separate run, the exact pre-existing `e2e/ridingFinishAndEnd.spec.ts` CPU-contention flake this file's own item 32 already documents — both confirmed passing cleanly in isolation and attributable to environment load, not this slice's changes), `npm run format:check` run last, immediately before committing — all pass. Version bumped to 0.3.41 (`package.json` and `package-lock.json`'s two matching `version` fields, hand-patched, following the established precedent).
    - **Real-device acceptance checklist (none of this has been checked yet — no physical device is available in this environment).** All evidence above is automated: Vitest unit/component tests and Playwright's Chromium (`chromium`) and Chromium-emulated Pixel-7 (`android-chrome`) projects — neither is a substitute for a genuine physical device, per this file's own established distinction (item 25). For both route Riding and free roam, on the installed iPhone Home Screen PWA (portrait orientation, and landscape if practical) and on physical Android: touch comfort and clear visual separation of the top-left Zoom cluster and the top-right North-up/Follow cluster; zooming while followed, while rotated, and while pitched, without disturbing bearing/pitch/centre; no unwanted Follow pause or "Map follow paused" toast from a zoom press; a later GPS fix retaining the selected zoom rather than snapping back to the default; a genuine manual pan/rotation still pausing Follow as before; North-up and Follow continuing to behave correctly alongside the new controls; suspension/reload plus an explicit Resume restoring the selected zoom; and no obvious jank from repeated zoom presses. Android (physical device, distinct from the `android-chrome` Chromium-emulated Playwright project): the same checklist, not yet attempted at all.
    - **Post-deployment acceptance note (general, non-itemised).** On 18 August 2026 the user reported that the deployed zoom controls work correctly in a static (non-moving) test, in both route Riding and free roam. This is informal, general confirmation only — it does not identify a device or platform, and does not individually confirm any single item in the real-device acceptance checklist above. That itemised checklist remains the authoritative, not-yet-individually-verified record for item 53; this note must not be read as satisfying it. In particular, a separate MOVING field test — proving Follow correctly retains and behaves with the rider's selected zoom level while real GPS fixes advance the rider — remains explicitly outstanding and untouched by this note.

### Riding elevation enhancement

54. **Active-Riding elevation-window simplification and distance markers — done**
    - Confirmed current behaviour: `ElevationViewMode` (`src/navigation/types.ts`) is `{kind:"full"} | {kind:"upcoming", windowMetres: 2000|5000|10000}` — Climb is a separate, non-persisted overlay (`EffectiveElevationView` in `climbElevationView.ts`), not a member of `ElevationViewMode` itself. `DEFAULT_ELEVATION_VIEW_MODE` (`src/navigation/upcomingElevation.ts`) is currently `{kind:"upcoming", windowMetres:5000}` (5 km), confirmed as `useRideNavigation.ts`'s initial state. `StoredElevationViewMode` (`storage/db.ts`) mirrors this shape; `resolveElevationViewMode` (`storage/mapping.ts`) validates a restored `windowMetres` against `ELEVATION_WINDOW_OPTIONS_METRES` and falls back to a legacy numeric field, then the default, for anything invalid — never rejecting a stored row.
    - Required outcome: reduce the standard window set to `Full`, `2 km`, `10 km` (remove `5 km`), plus the existing contextual `Climb` overlay, unchanged. Change the default for a new ride to `2 km`. A restored `StoredElevationViewMode`/legacy numeric value of `5000` must resolve to `2 km`, not be rejected or fall through to a confusing value — extend `resolveElevationViewMode`'s validation/normalisation explicitly for this case (`ELEVATION_WINDOW_OPTIONS_METRES` drops 5000, gains no new value), so the UI can never try to render a now-impossible selected state. Add relative "ahead of the rider" distance markers to the elevation chart: one marker at +1 km for the 2 km view; four markers at +2/+4/+6/+8 km for the 10 km view. These are distances ahead of the current rider position, not the existing absolute route-kilometre distance badges on the map (`src/map/distanceBadgeLayer.ts`) — do not conflate the two, and give them a visually distinct treatment from elevation values, climb-boundary markers, and the live progress marker already on the chart. Preserve `Full` view and all existing current-climb behaviour completely unchanged (automatic Climb availability, manual dismissal for the remainder of that climb, re-offering Climb on a later climb — `climbElevationView.ts`'s existing state machine, untouched by this item). Land independently; it does not need to wait for the staged immersive-Riding redesign (items 55–58) and should work in the current, non-immersive Riding layout too.
    - Required coverage: storage mapping/migration tests proving a legacy `5000` value (both the modern shape and the older bare numeric field) normalises to `2000`; a `useRideNavigation`/UI test confirming the new default for a fresh ride; chart tests for the new relative markers at both window sizes, including that they move correctly as the rider's position advances and remain visually distinguishable from existing chart elements; existing elevation-view/window-button tests updated for the reduced button set.
    - **Architecture.** `ElevationWindowMetres` (`src/navigation/types.ts`) narrows to `2000 | 10000`; `DEFAULT_ELEVATION_WINDOW_METRES`/`ELEVATION_WINDOW_OPTIONS_METRES` (`src/navigation/upcomingElevation.ts`) update to `2000`/`[2000, 10000]` — `DEFAULT_ELEVATION_VIEW_MODE`/`ELEVATION_VIEW_MODE_OPTIONS` need no code change of their own, since both already derive from these two constants, and `RidingScreen.tsx`'s window-button row (`ELEVATION_VIEW_MODE_OPTIONS.map(...)`) auto-shrinks to three standard buttons with no code change either, the conditional `Climb` button still appended after. A new pure derivation, `selectElevationDistanceGuides(window: UpcomingElevationWindow, windowMetres: ElevationWindowMetres): ElevationDistanceGuide[]` (same module), maps a small offsets table (`{2000: [1000], 10000: [2000, 4000, 6000, 8000]}`) against the live window's own `startDistanceMetres`, filtering out any guide whose resolved route-global distance exceeds the window's own (already route-end-clamped) `endDistanceMetres` — boundary-inclusive, so a guide landing exactly at a truncated window's own edge still renders. Stateless and re-run fresh every render, so "each guide's position advances with the window" and "guides beyond a truncated window are omitted near the finish" both fall out for free with no special-case tracking.
    - **Chart.** `ElevationChart` (`src/ui/shared/ElevationChart.tsx`) gains an optional `distanceGuides?: readonly ElevationChartDistanceGuideInput[]` prop (`{distanceFromStartMetres, aheadMetres}`, route-global, trusted verbatim — the chart never infers which offsets apply, mirroring `marker`/`selectedRangeMetres`). Guides render as a new `<g pointerEvents="none">` inserted directly after the tap-target `<rect>` and before the profile-segment paths, so the profile line/area-fill/marker always paint over them; each is a short (14px, not full-height) dashed tick (`stroke-dasharray="2 4"`, distinct from both `STALE_MARKER_DASHARRAY` ("4 3") and `COMPLETED_DASHARRAY` ("5 4")) plus a small `+N km` text label whose `text-anchor` flips from `middle` to `start`/`end` within 24px of either chart edge, so a guide near a truncated window's right edge never renders clipped/overflowing text. `distanceToX` (already exported by `elevationChartGeometry.ts` for the identical conversion elsewhere) is reused directly — no new geometry helper needed. A new, plain, non-`aria-live` `<p className="elevation-chart-distance-guides-caption">` after the existing marker paragraph lists every guide's label in plain text, mirroring that paragraph's own established "re-renders every fix, no live-region" precedent; omitting `distanceGuides` (or passing an empty array) renders nothing extra at all, so every pre-existing caller is unaffected.
    - **Screen wiring.** Exactly one of `RidingScreen.tsx`'s five `<ElevationChart>` call sites changes — the plain "upcoming rolling window" branch (never Full, Climb, the pre-ride whole-route chart, or the pre-ride selected-climb preview). `windowMetres` is read via a local, commented defensive narrow on `nav.elevationViewMode.kind === "upcoming"` (not by widening `useRideNavigation.ts`'s `ElevationProfileDisplay` type, which has exactly one consumer — this file — making the narrower, lower-risk local check the correct choice) and piped into `selectElevationDistanceGuides` alongside the already-computed `window`. This structurally guarantees Planning's chart, the pre-ride whole-route chart, `Full` view, the current-climb chart, the pre-ride selected-climb preview, and free roam (no elevation chart there at all) can never gain guides — none of them pass the prop.
    - **Storage — explicit normalisation, not accidental fallthrough.** `StoredElevationWindowMetres = ElevationWindowMetres | 5000` (`src/storage/db.ts`, new) widens both `StoredElevationViewMode.windowMetres` and `StoredRouteRideState.elevationWindowMetres` (the legacy bare field), so a genuine historical `5000` row — tagged or legacy-bare — can be represented and round-tripped through test fixtures with no unsafe cast anywhere; purely additive/non-indexed, no Dexie `version()` bump. `resolveElevationViewMode` (`src/storage/mapping.ts`) gained `normalizeStoredElevationWindowMetres(value): ElevationWindowMetres | undefined`, called at both its tagged and legacy-bare check sites: a modern value (2000/10000) passes through unchanged; `5000` is recognised and normalised to `2000` **explicitly**, as its own deliberate branch, distinct from "malformed" — proven by a dedicated test asserting a row carrying **both** a tagged `5000` **and** a different, valid legacy bare field (`10000`) still resolves to `2000`, not `10000` (the only test that actually distinguishes explicit normalisation from a bug that would treat tagged-5000 as merely "invalid tagged, fall through to the bare field"); anything else falls through past, exactly as before. Exact preserved precedence: modern tagged wins outright → tagged `5000` normalises without consulting the bare field at all → a genuinely invalid tagged value falls back to the bare field, itself normalised the same way → malformed/absent resolves to the (now 2 km) default. `toStoredRideState` never writes the bare legacy field (confirmed by direct reading), so new writes structurally cannot contain `5000` anywhere — the live `ElevationViewMode` type makes it unconstructable at every write call site.
    - **CSS.** `.elevation-window-group`'s comment ("...five buttons (Full/2 km/5 km/10 km/Climb)...") updated to "four buttons (Full/2 km/10 km/Climb)" — no functional CSS change; guide styling follows this component's existing all-inline-SVG-attributes convention (classNames exist only as test-query hooks).
    - **Files changed:** `src/navigation/types.ts`, `src/navigation/upcomingElevation.ts`, `src/storage/db.ts`, `src/storage/mapping.ts`, `src/ui/shared/ElevationChart.tsx`, `src/ui/riding/RidingScreen.tsx`, `src/index.css`; tests: `src/navigation/upcomingElevation.test.ts` (32 tests — updated constants/boundary-interpolation fixtures, +7 new `selectElevationDistanceGuides` tests), `src/navigation/climbElevationView.test.ts` (30 tests — pure `UPCOMING_5KM`→`UPCOMING_10KM` fixture rename, no assertion-intent change), `src/storage/mapping.test.ts` (67 tests — `upcoming5km`→`upcoming10km` fixture rename, `it.each` shrunk to `[2000, 10000]`, default/malformed-value assertions updated to `2000`, +3 new explicit-normalisation tests including the tagged-vs-bare precedence proof), `src/ui/shared/ElevationChart.test.tsx` (47 tests — new `describe("distance guides", ...)` block: rendering/position/labelling/dasharray-distinctness/paint-order/tap-non-interception/edge-anchor-flipping/caption), `src/ui/riding/RidingScreen.test.tsx` (150 tests — 6 pre-existing tests fixed for the new button set/default, including a hand-verified boundary-interpolation fixture rewrite confirmed against the real rendered figcaption value; new `describe("Elevation distance guides", ...)` block using a dedicated 20 km densified fixture, covering the 2 km/10 km guide sets, constant-relative-position while advancing, route-end truncation, absence in Full/pre-ride/Climb views, the accessible caption, and tap non-interception).
    - **Playwright.** `e2e/ridingClimbView.spec.ts`'s three `"5 km"` selector references replaced with `"10 km"` (deliberately distinct from the new `"2 km"` default, so the "manually dismiss Climb via a standard view" action stays an unambiguous deliberate choice). A new `e2e/ridingElevationWindows.spec.ts` (4 tests, `chromium` project only) uses a new dedicated fixture, `e2e/fixtures/elevation-distance-guides-route.gpx` (126 points at 200 m spacing, flat 20 m elevation, 25 km total) — **not** a reuse of `distanceBadges.spec.ts`'s own `distance-badges-route.gpx`, whose ~1000 m point spacing exceeds `gradient.ts`'s `MAX_ELEVATION_GAP_METRES` (500 m) and so causes `analyzeRouteElevationProfile` to drop its elevation entirely (every point becomes an unanalysable single-point "run"), leaving `ElevationChart` permanently stuck on its own "Elevation data is not available for this route." early-return with no guides ever able to render — discovered directly by an initial real run of this spec against that fixture, which timed out waiting for guide text that could never appear, not assumed in advance. Covers: the reduced button set with `2 km` selected and its one `+1 km` guide on a fresh ride; the `10 km` view's four guides at a mid-route (10 km) position; guides progressively omitted approaching the finish (`+2/+4/+6/+8 km` at 10 km from the finish, only `+2 km` at 2.5 km from the finish); no horizontal overflow at the 390×844 phone viewport. `e2e/gradientColouring.spec.ts`/`e2e/layout.spec.ts`/`e2e/androidPersistenceAndOffline.spec.ts` reference the elevation-window control only by its generic `group` role name, never an individual button label — confirmed unaffected, no edits needed.
    - **Verification.** `npm run format`, `npm run lint`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run build`, `npm test` — 2526/2526 across 129 files, `npm run e2e` — 142/142 across both the `chromium` (128) and `android-chrome` (14) projects (the new `ridingElevationWindows.spec.ts` additionally repeated 20/20 with zero flakiness under `--repeat-each=5 --workers=1`), `npm run format:check` run last, immediately before committing — all pass. Every new/materially-changed test was confirmed, before being relied on, to fail for the intended reason against the pre-change code: the storage/navigation/chart/screen unit and component suites failed 32/32 on the intended assertions when the six production files above were reverted (via a temporary `git stash` of only those files, then restored), and the e2e-relevant production revert failed to even type-check (`ElevationChart.test.tsx` referencing the not-yet-existing `distanceGuides` prop), an even stronger compile-time proof. Version bumped to 0.3.42 (`package.json` and `package-lock.json`'s two matching `version` fields, hand-patched, following the established precedent).
    - **Real-device acceptance checklist (none of this has been checked yet — no physical device is available in this environment).** All evidence above is automated: Vitest unit/component tests and Playwright's Chromium (`chromium`) and Chromium-emulated Pixel-7 (`android-chrome`) projects — neither is a substitute for a genuine physical device, per this file's own established distinction (item 25). On the installed iPhone Home Screen PWA (and on physical Android): the reduced `Full`/`2 km`/`10 km` selector and its `2 km` default on a genuinely new ride; the `+1 km` guide (2 km view) and the `+2/+4/+6/+8 km` guides (10 km view) both showing correctly and legibly while actually moving, not just in a static/mocked test; guides correctly thinning out while genuinely approaching a route's finish; a ride resumed from a stored session that predates this change (a real on-device `5 km` selection, if one exists) correctly restoring as `2 km`; and `Full`/`Climb` view behaviour — including automatic Climb entry/dismissal/re-entry — staying completely unaffected by any of the above.

### Immersive active-Riding redesign

Design-reference caveat, applying to items 55–58 collectively: the user supplied illustrative mock-up images (`acn-riding-immersive-overview.png`, `acn-riding-navigation-mockup.png`, `acn-riding-profile-2km-mockup.png`, `acn-riding-profile-10km-mockup.png`, `acn-riding-active-climb-mockup.png`, `acn-riding-end-confirmation-mockup.png`, `acn-riding-paused-launcher-mockup.png`) showing the general direction liked — immersive active Riding, a compact Pause/title/End header, a large map, fixed Map/Profile navigation, a dedicated Profile presentation, inline End confirmation, and a paused Ride launcher. These are references for hierarchy and direction only, not exact specifications, not screenshots of implemented behaviour, and not real-device acceptance evidence. Pixel dimensions, exact colours, icon artwork, sample route names/numbers, and incidental control positions are not settled merely by appearing in an image; where any image detail conflicts with the written requirements here, the text governs — in particular the agreed map-control placement (zoom top-left; Locate/North-up or North-up/Follow top-right, items 52–53) supersedes any conflicting position shown in a mock-up.

55. **Immersive active-Riding shell and Pause lifecycle — done**
    - Supersedes item 24's "never auto-hides" contract for active-Riding navigation specifically: item 24 deliberately kept the global five-tab navigation non-sticky-but-visible during active GPS tracking, to maximise dashboard space without hiding wayfinding. This item's immersive redesign goes further — the global `MainNavigation` (and its wrapping `<header className="app-header--sticky">`) is now genuinely **absent from the DOM**, not merely non-sticky, the instant either route Riding or free roam is genuinely GPS-tracking, replaced by a new compact `Pause`/title/`End ride` header owned by the active screen itself. Item 24's behaviour for every other screen, and for pre-ride/recovery states (where the global nav stays sticky and visible exactly as shipped), is unaffected; this item narrows item 24's scope only for the genuinely-active-tracking case. `src/ui/shared/navPositionMode.ts` (`NavPositionMode = "sticky" | "static"`, `deriveNavPositionMode`) is deleted outright, along with its test file — that three-state framing (App's header always mounted, only its CSS `position` toggling between "sticky" and "static") no longer describes reality, since the "static" state (a visible-but-non-sticky nav) doesn't exist any more.
    - **`isImmersiveRidingShell`** (`src/ui/shared/immersiveRidingShell.ts`, new): `(screen: Screen, isRidingActive: boolean) => screen === "riding" && isRidingActive` — a genuine simplification over the old two-value CSS-position framing, since there is now only a binary "nav rendered and sticky" vs. "nav genuinely absent" choice. `App.tsx` computes `isImmersive = isImmersiveRidingShell(screen, isRidingActive)` and renders `{isImmersive ? null : (<header className="app-header--sticky"><MainNavigation .../></header>)}` — every case that previously resolved to `"sticky"` still does, unconditionally, once rendered at all; the one case that previously resolved to `"static"` now renders nothing. `isRidingActive` is unchanged from item 24: sourced only from `RidingScreen`'s/`FreeRoamScreen`'s own `onRidingActiveChange` callback, itself `nav.geolocationStatus !== "idle"` — never inferred from button text, GPS freshness, route presence, or CSS. A transient GPS `"error"` state still counts as active (the underlying watch is never torn down for it), so a mid-ride error stays part of one continuous, still-immersive session.
    - **`pause(): Promise<void>`** on both `useRideNavigation.ts` and `useFreeRoamNavigation.ts`, mirroring `finish()`'s existing "hook does the work, screen owns pending/error UI" division of labour exactly — no new pending state returned from the hook itself. A new `isPausingRef` (set synchronously as `pause()`'s first statement, before any `await` — the same rationale as `isFinalizingRef`, but for this non-destructive transition) is checked by the persistence effect (`if (isFinalizingRef.current || isPausingRef.current) return;` — never by `handleFix`/`handleError`, so a genuine fix arriving mid-pause still updates in-memory state normally, just isn't persisted) and by `finish()`'s own guard, giving hook-level mutual exclusion between the two transitions as a defensive backstop beneath the screen's own primary guard (see below). `pause()` lazily defaults `startedAtRef.current` exactly like `handleFix` does, so a Pause issued before the very first GPS fix still produces a valid, resumable row (`lastFix: null`); writes the full snapshot — including a **fresh, synchronous** `getCameraState()`/`getPersistableSnapshot()` read, not the persistence effect's own deps-gated cached value — via the existing `setActiveRideState`/`toStoredRideState`/`toStoredFreeRoamState`; and only calls `stop()` (bumps the watch generation, tears down the watch, sets status `"idle"`, which is what makes `RidingWakeLockControl`/the free-roam wake-lock control unmount and release the lock for free, exactly as `finish()` already does) **on success** — a storage rejection leaves the ride/session fully live and resumable, throws to the caller, and `isPausingRef` is cleared so a retry can proceed. Unlike `finish()`, `pause()` never calls `clearActiveRideState()` and never resets `currentFix`/`coreState`/`elevationViewMode`/`wakeLockDesired`/`dismissedClimbFeatureId`/`restoredCameraState`/`completionArmed` (route) or the equivalent free-roam fields — every one of these is preserved exactly, satisfying "preserves progress/camera/elevation/wake-lock-preference state" as a direct, verified consequence of `pause()` simply never touching them, not a separate mechanism. One accepted, documented edge case: a fix arriving during `pause()`'s own `await` (the watch is still nominally live until `stop()` runs, after success) updates in-memory state harmlessly but is never persisted — dropped, not wrong, since the screen unmounts moments later once `onRidePaused` fires; no further guard was needed or added for this.
    - **Mutual exclusion is enforced primarily at the screen level**, mirroring the existing, already-documented division of labour between `isFinalizeActionPendingRef` (primary, UI-level) and `isFinalizingRef` (defensive, hook-level backstop): `RidingScreen.tsx`/`FreeRoamScreen.tsx` each gained an independent `isPauseActionPendingRef`/`isPausePending`/`pauseError` triple, **not** a widened `activeFinalizeSource` union — a `"pause"` member would be semantically wrong, since Pause doesn't finalise anything. `performPauseRide` checks `isFinalizeActionPendingRef.current` before proceeding; `performFinalizeRide`'s own existing guard gained `|| isPauseActionPendingRef.current`. Both refs are set synchronously only at the moment their respective action actually begins (Pause's own click, or the End-ride/Finish-ride confirm click) — **not** merely while a confirmation dialog is open awaiting a decision, so Pause stays available and enabled while the End-ride confirmation is merely open (a real, deliberate architectural property, confirmed directly by an e2e assertion after an earlier draft of that same test wrongly assumed the opposite).
    - **`RidingImmersiveHeader`** (`src/ui/riding/RidingImmersiveHeader.tsx`, new): a small presentational component taking `title`, `pauseLabel`, `onPause`, `pauseDisabled`, an optional `pauseButtonRef`, and an `endAction: ReactNode` slot — it owns no End-ride business logic, confirmation state, or error state itself; each screen keeps calling its own existing `renderEndRideAction()` (item 50) and passes the result in, reusing that in-place-morph pattern rather than forking it into the header. Renders exactly one `<h1 className="screen-title riding-immersive-header-title">` per state (mirroring the idle-only `.ride-route-header`'s own `<h1>`), visually truncated to one line via CSS (`overflow: hidden; white-space: nowrap; text-overflow: ellipsis`) so a long route name can never push Pause/End off-screen — the accessible name and DOM `textContent` stay the full, untruncated string (CSS truncation never touches the DOM text), with a native `title` attribute additionally surfacing it on hover/long-press as a convenience, not as the accessibility mechanism. A genuine `<header>` element is safe here (no ARIA `banner`-landmark collision) since it's nested inside `<section aria-label="Riding">`/the free-roam screen's own section — only a non-nested `<header>` gets the implicit `banner` role. Pause uses `.btn-secondary` (non-destructive framing, an explicit, deliberate contrast with End's `.btn-danger`); 44×44 touch targets come from the existing global `button` rule for free.
    - **`RidingScreen.tsx` wiring.** JSX restructured so exactly two conditionals change and every other element (offline notice, idle panel's own content, error panel, waiting-for-fix, status strip, manoeuvre panel, completion panel, paused toast, map, elevation section) is byte-for-byte unchanged, same relative order: the idle branch still renders `.ride-route-header`'s plain `<h1>`+meta line; the active branch now renders `<RidingImmersiveHeader .../>` (`endAction={!isEndRideConfirmOpen ? renderEndRideAction() : null}`), a conditional `pauseError` paragraph, and — only while `isEndRideConfirmOpen` — a new `.ride-end-ride-confirm-row` holding the second `renderEndRideAction()` call (exactly one of the two ternary branches is ever actually invoked per render, so calling it from two gated sites is correct and cheap). The old unconditional-else `.ride-end-ride-row` branch (item 40/50's own container) is removed entirely — End now lives in the header's `endAction` slot plus this confirm-row, an explicit, deliberate ordering change from item 40 (whose own DOM-order claim for the _active_ case is superseded here; item 40's pre-ride ordering is untouched). The pre-existing `pendingEndRideFocusRef` mechanism needed **no changes** — it already refocuses `endRideTriggerRef.current` once mounted+enabled regardless of where in the DOM that button currently renders, so Cancel's focus-restore is inherited for free. `RidingRouteCompletionPanel` gained a new, purely additive optional `disabled?: boolean` prop (both its buttons become `disabled={isFinishing || disabled}`; the label logic stays keyed on `isFinishing` alone, so it never wrongly claims to be "Finishing ride…" while merely blocked by an unrelated Pause) — `RidingScreen` passes `disabled={isPausePending}`. `RidingScreen` gained a new optional `onRidePaused?: () => void` prop, called only once `nav.pause()` has resolved successfully, wrapped in its own nested `try/catch` (a throwing callback is logged only via `logError`, never surfaced as a pause failure — mirrors `onRideFinalized`'s identical nested-catch precedent) — deliberately **not** calling `camera.resetCamera()`/`completion.reset()`/`setReachedManoeuvreIndex(0)`, since Pause preserves camera choice, climb-dismissal and completion-armed state; only `finish()` resets them.
    - **`FreeRoamScreen.tsx` wiring** mirrors `RidingScreen.tsx`'s shape exactly (its own `performPauseRide`, `isPauseActionPendingRef`/`isPausePending`/`pauseError`, mutual exclusion with `isFinalizing`, its own `onRidePaused?: () => void` prop, its own log tags `"free-roam-pause-ride"`/`"free-roam-ride-paused-callback"` and error copy "Free roam could not be paused on this device. Try again."), with one deliberate architectural difference: `RidingImmersiveHeader` renders **unconditionally** here (matching the exact unconditional rendering of the `<h1>`/`.ride-end-ride-row` it replaces), not gated on `geolocationStatus !== "idle"` — free roam auto-starts its watch on mount (item 42) and has no idle pre-ride panel of its own to gate against. A theoretical one-frame window, before `onRidingActiveChange(true)` propagates up from the mount-effect chain, where both the global nav and this immersive header could in principle both be visible, was considered and deliberately accepted rather than gated away: gating would introduce a strictly worse trade — a new one-frame window with _no_ `<h1>` at all, which doesn't exist today — and `body`'s own background colour already matches the header's opaque background exactly, so any such transient gap is visually inert (the same "blank-but-same-colour" tolerance `.app-header--sticky`'s own doc comment already documents elsewhere in this codebase, not a new risk). `RidingScreen` has no equivalent risk: its idle→active transition is driven synchronously by a click handler (`nav.start()` inside `handleStart`), never a mount effect, so it never has an intermediate render with the wrong title.
    - **`App.tsx` wiring.** A new `handleRidePaused = () => { resetRidingContentToLauncher(); };` — storage is deliberately **not** cleared (the hook's own `pause()` already wrote a full resumable snapshot and stopped the watch); only the in-memory `ridingContent` pointer resets to `{kind:"none"}`, dropping back to the Ride launcher, which re-hydrates from the still-present, now-resumable persisted row exactly like a direct nav-away-and-back already does per item 42. `resetRidingContentToLauncher`'s own comment now enumerates all four call sites and their distinct storage contracts: `handleRideFinalized` (End/Finish — storage cleared first, empty launcher), `handleRidePaused` (storage retained, watch already stopped, resumable launcher), `handleReturnToRideLauncher` (item 51 — no active watch ever existed, no storage mutation), and `handleNavigate`'s own separate free-roam-specific inline reset (a different, already-documented silent-scroll contract, deliberately not routed through this shared helper). `onRidePaused={handleRidePaused}` is wired to both `<RidingScreen>` and `<FreeRoamScreen>`. No transient "Navigation paused" launcher notice was built — a deliberate scope decision, not an oversight: `RidingLauncher`'s existing "You have an unfinished ride on this route."/"You have an unfinished free roam session." text already unambiguously communicates the resumable state regardless of _why_ it's unfinished (paused, interrupted, or otherwise).
    - **CSS — two real, evidence-backed fixes over the naive design, both verified with a real-browser Playwright check before being relied on (mirroring item 34's own minimal-repro discipline).** First, a top-gap/double-counting bug: `.app-shell`'s existing `:has(> .app-header--sticky) { padding-top: 0 }` cancellation (item 34) only matches when App's own sticky header is present, which it isn't while immersive — so a second, deeper selector, `.app-shell:has(.riding-immersive-header)` (no `>` combinator, since the new header is several levels deep, not a direct child), was added alongside it; a matching `.screen:has(.riding-immersive-header) { padding-top: 0 }` cancels `.screen`'s own top padding too, so `.riding-immersive-header`'s own `padding-top: calc(var(--safe-area-inset-top) + var(--space-8))` is the _only_ thing reserving that strip, mirroring `.app-header--sticky`'s own item-34 fix exactly (a sticky element's one-time ancestor padding cannot continuously cover the strip while scrolled — only the sticky element's own box, at every scroll position, can). Second, a horizontal double-counting bug: `.app-shell`'s own padding already reserves `env(safe-area-inset-right/left)` **unconditionally on every screen**, including while immersive — so the new header's own edge-to-edge negative-horizontal-margin breakout (`margin: 0 calc(-1 * var(--space-16))`, escaping `.screen`'s own `--space-16` padding) uses plain `--space-16` horizontal padding, **not** `--safe-area-inset-right/left + --space-16`, which would double-count a space `.app-shell` already reserved one level up. Three new project-owned custom properties, `--safe-area-inset-right/bottom/left` (mirroring the existing `--safe-area-inset-top` exactly), let `.app-shell`'s padding be expressed entirely via `var()` — same resolved values in production, but now independently overridable in a Playwright test the same way `--safe-area-inset-top` already was (`e2e/stickyNavigation.spec.ts`'s established synthetic-inset technique), which is what makes the new spec's four-sided safe-area coverage below possible at all. A minimal real-browser check (not merely reasoned about) confirmed: the header's opaque box genuinely covers the safe-area top strip once scrolled, not just at rest; no double safe-area gap appears at the top on load; and `position: sticky` behaves correctly as a non-first flex child inside `.screen`'s `flex-direction: column` — this last point mattered specifically because `RidingWakeLockControl` renders _before_ this header in document order (unchanged, non-pushing per its own existing contract), which means at rest (scroll 0) the header's own natural flow position sits _below_ that control, not yet genuinely "stuck"; every Playwright assertion of "the header sits at the true viewport top" therefore first scrolls to the bottom of the page's own (often modest) scrollable range and polls for a real `window.scrollY > 0`, mirroring `stickyNavigation.spec.ts`'s own already-established "two-step comparison is unreliable, scroll to the true end instead" technique — never comparing two arbitrary scroll positions against each other.
    - **Scope discipline, verified, not merely asserted.** Today's existing active-Riding content (status strip, manoeuvre panel, completion panel, elevation section, climb selector, map, camera/zoom controls) is rendered inside the new shell completely unchanged — no reorganisation into Map/Profile views (that is item 56, untouched by this slice). No camera, progress, manoeuvre-selection, elevation, gradient, climb-detection, completion-arming, wake-lock state-machine, GPX, routing, or IndexedDB-schema logic changed anywhere; every production diff outside `App.tsx`/`RidingScreen.tsx`/`FreeRoamScreen.tsx`/the two new files/`index.css`/`RidingRouteCompletionPanel.tsx`/the two hook files is a doc-comment-only edit (`src/ui/shared/MainNavigation.tsx`, `src/map/MapView.tsx` untouched entirely).
    - **Files changed:** `src/ui/riding/useRideNavigation.ts`, `src/ui/riding/useFreeRoamNavigation.ts`, `src/ui/riding/RidingScreen.tsx`, `src/ui/riding/FreeRoamScreen.tsx`, `src/ui/riding/RidingRouteCompletionPanel.tsx`, `src/App.tsx`, `src/index.css`, `src/ui/shared/MainNavigation.tsx` (doc comment only); new: `src/ui/riding/RidingImmersiveHeader.tsx`, `src/ui/shared/immersiveRidingShell.ts` (replacing the deleted `src/ui/shared/navPositionMode.ts`).
    - **Tests.** New: `src/ui/riding/useRideNavigation.pause.test.ts` (11 — complete snapshot incl. a fresh `getCameraState()` read; row stays present/resumable; a pre-first-fix pause creates a valid `lastFix: null` row; preserves elevation view/wake-lock/dismissed-climb/completion-armed state; a storage rejection leaves the watch+session fully live and permits retry; duplicate concurrent calls don't double-write/double-stop; late callbacks from the stopped generation are ignored; never calls `clearActiveRideState`/applies `finish()`'s reset; a fix arriving during the pause write is not persisted but doesn't throw; both directions of the `pause()`/`finish()` hook-level cross-guard, including the documented asymmetry), `src/ui/riding/useFreeRoamNavigation.pause.test.ts` (10, the same matrix adapted to `getPersistableSnapshot()`/`toStoredFreeRoamState`), `src/ui/shared/immersiveRidingShell.test.ts` (6, replacing the deleted `navPositionMode.test.ts` — the four non-Riding screens × both `isRidingActive` values always `false`; Riding+inactive `false`; Riding+active `true`), `src/ui/riding/RidingImmersiveHeader.test.tsx` (7), `src/ui/riding/RidingScreen.pause.test.tsx` (11 — header appears only while active with the correct title; no confirmation dialog ever shown for Pause; pending/failure(`role="alert"`)/success states; success calls `onRidePaused` only after `nav.pause()` resolves; progress/storage preserved, `onRideFinalized` never called; wake lock releases while the `wakeLockDesired` preference is preserved in storage; a storage failure shows a retryable error and moves focus to Pause; End ride still opens/cancels correctly from its new slot; both directions of the screen-level mutual exclusion), `src/ui/riding/FreeRoamScreen.pause.test.tsx` (10, the same matrix adapted to free roam's unconditional-header shape), `e2e/ridingImmersiveShell.spec.ts` (8, see below). Modified: `src/ui/riding/RidingRouteCompletionPanel.test.tsx` (7 total — new `disabled` prop cases: both buttons disabled, label stays "Finish ride" not "Finishing ride…" while merely `disabled` and not `isFinishing`, plus a default-to-`false`-when-omitted case matching every pre-existing caller), `src/App.test.tsx` (33 total — the `describe("App — sticky/static main navigation")` block renamed to `describe("App — immersive Riding shell")`; existing cases updated to assert the global header/nav is genuinely _absent_ via `queryByRole`, not merely non-sticky; new cases: active route Riding and active free roam both omit `MainNavigation` and render the immersive header; a live geolocation error mid-ride stays immersive; Pause restores `MainNavigation` for both route and free-roam sessions, with the resumed pre-ride screen offering `Resume riding` — proven via a richer geolocation-watch stub exposing a real `emitFix()` and full `watchPosition` call-count verification through Pause→Resume→Resume riding), `src/ui/riding/RidingScreen.test.tsx` (150 total — two DOM-order assertions rewritten from the deleted `.ride-end-ride-row` container to the new header/`endAction`-slot/`.ride-end-ride-confirm-row` structure), `src/ui/riding/RidingScreen.finishEndRide.test.tsx` (20 total, one structural assertion rewritten identically), `src/ui/riding/FreeRoamScreen.endRide.test.tsx` (8 total, likewise). `src/ui/riding/useRideNavigation.test.ts` (26)/`src/ui/riding/useFreeRoamNavigation.test.ts` (23) re-run unmodified in substance and confirmed still green.
    - **A genuine `eslint-plugin-react-hooks@7` `react-hooks/immutability` constraint, found and resolved during implementation, not merely anticipated.** Mutating `isPausingRef.current` inside `pause()`'s own `useCallback` was flagged ("Modifying a value previously passed as an argument to a hook is not allowed") **only** once `isPausingRef` was _also_ read from a second, separate `useCallback` (`finish()`) — a genuine widening of the constraint item 35's own doc comment already recorded for two separate `useEffect` reads; this is the first confirmed instance of the identical rule tripping across two `useCallback`s rather than two `useEffect`s. Resolved with the asymmetric guard described above (`pause()` checks both refs; `finish()` only checks its own), confirmed clean via `npm run lint` with zero new suppressions — screen-level guards remain the primary enforcement either way, so the asymmetry has no observable behavioural effect.
    - **Playwright.** `e2e/stickyNavigation.spec.ts` (9 tests, all rewritten/extended in place): its own doc comment now records item 55's supersession explicitly; a `headerLocator`/`immersiveHeaderLocator` pair distinguishes the two mutually-exclusive `<header>` elements; the former "scrolls out of view while active Riding" test is replaced with a positive proof that the new immersive header stays genuinely _pinned_ (not merely absent-then-present) even when scrolled, using the scroll-to-true-bottom technique described above (a first attempt using an intermediate scroll position failed, since this page's own total scrollable range was too small for a reliable two-step comparison); a new "Pause restores the global nav header immediately, and the route stays resumable in the background" test proves the only-way-to-leave-an-active-ride-reversibly claim directly, since the pre-item-55 path (navigating away via `MainNavigation` while riding) no longer exists at all once the nav is genuinely absent; the synthetic-safe-area-inset describe block gained a rewritten case proving the global header stays genuinely absent (`toHaveCount(0)`) under a synthetic inset while the immersive header shows instead, deferring full four-sided safe-area/orientation coverage to the new file below. `e2e/gradientColouring.spec.ts`, `e2e/ridingFinishAndEnd.spec.ts` (both occurrences), and `e2e/freeRoam.spec.ts` each had their `.ride-end-ride-row`-based "confirmation renders in the trigger's own slot" structural check rewritten to the new `header.nextElementSibling === confirmRow` shape, reusing the identical `page.evaluate` proof across all three files. `e2e/androidFreeRoam.spec.ts`, `e2e/androidRiding.spec.ts`, and `e2e/androidMobileLayout.spec.ts` (found via a broader repo-wide grep sweep for `.ride-end-ride-row`/`toHaveCSS("position", "static")`, beyond the plan's own original migration list) each had their stale `header`/`"static"` assertions replaced with the same `toHaveCount(0)`/immersive-header-visible pair.
    - **`e2e/ridingImmersiveShell.spec.ts`** (new, 8 tests, `chromium` project — a wholly independent spec file per this repo's no-shared-e2e-helpers convention, owning the immersive header's full four-sided safe-area behaviour, the Pause lifecycle, and the GPS-restart-gating contract that `stickyNavigation.spec.ts` deliberately leaves to it): active route Riding hides the global nav and shows the immersive header with exactly one `watchPosition` call; Pause is a single, confirmation-free tap (`getByRole("alertdialog")` count 0 throughout) that stops the watch, persists a full resumable row (`kind: "route"`, `lastFix` populated), and returns to the launcher — landing on the launcher, and opening the resumed pre-ride recovery screen, both leave the `watchPosition` call count unchanged at 1, with only an explicit further "Resume riding" tap producing a second call; a companion test proves Pause releases the Wake Lock stub (`released === true`) while `wakeLockDesired: true` survives in the persisted row and the checkbox re-shows checked, with a fresh lock re-acquired, once resumed; the End-ride confirmation renders inline directly beneath the header (the same `nextElementSibling` proof as the migrated files above) with Pause staying enabled while it's merely open, and Cancel/confirm both still work, restoring the global nav on confirm; Finish ride (armed via two interior fixes, confirmed via two consecutive finish-line fixes, mirroring `ridingFinishAndEnd.spec.ts`'s own established sequencing — each `context.setGeolocation` call is individually awaited via its own observable effect before the next is issued, never issued back-to-back) restores the normal shell exactly like Pause and End ride do; a free-roam counterpart proves the identical Pause/wake-lock/GPS-restart-gating contract for `Start free roam`/`Resume free roam`. A `test.describe("390×844 phone viewport", ...)` block covers: the header's opaque box starting at the true viewport top under a synthetic four-sided inset (`{top:59, right:20, bottom:34, left:20}`, using a `useSyntheticSafeAreaInsets` helper extending `stickyNavigation.spec.ts`'s established top-only technique to all four new custom properties), with Pause/End ride both real ≥44×44px touch targets fully contained inside the header's own box and never overlapping each other, plus no horizontal overflow; a long (implausibly long, deliberately) route name staying visually truncated with a genuine ellipsis (`scrollWidth > clientWidth`, `text-overflow: ellipsis`) while the full string remains both the DOM `textContent` and the accessible name, and Pause/End ride staying fully within the viewport regardless; and a portrait→landscape `page.setViewportSize` change proving the header and both controls stay usable (real touch targets, no overflow) and Pause still works correctly afterwards.
    - **Two genuine bugs found and fixed in this spec's own test logic during verification, not in production code.** First, an over-strict assertion claiming Pause is _disabled_ merely while the End-ride confirmation dialog is open — direct reading of `RidingScreen.tsx` showed `activeFinalizeSource` (which drives `pauseDisabled`) is only ever set once the dialog's own confirm button is clicked, not merely while it's open awaiting a decision; the assertion was corrected to prove the real, positive architectural property instead (Pause stays enabled while the confirmation is merely a cancellable, non-destructive decision), with a comment pointing at the unit-level tests for the narrower in-flight window. Second, a genuinely reproducible (3/10 under `--repeat-each=10 --workers=1`, i.e. not a CPU-contention artefact) strict-mode-violation flake in the End-ride test: `getByRole("heading", {name: "Ride"})`'s default case-insensitive substring matching could transiently match this test's own route name (which, uniquely among this file's route names, contained the literal substring "ride") _and_ the dialog's own still-settling "End this ride?" heading simultaneously. Fixed two ways: the route name was changed to avoid the substring entirely, and the assertion was additionally hardened with a deterministic `expect.poll(() => readActiveRideStateRow(page), ...).toBeNull()` (mirroring `ridingFinishAndEnd.spec.ts`'s own established `waitForClearedRideState` convention — `finish()` awaits the storage clear strictly before `onRideFinalized` fires, a race-free signal) ahead of the now-`exact: true` heading check. Confirmed fixed via 15/15 repeats in isolation (`--repeat-each=15 --workers=1`) after the fix, versus the reproducible failure before it.
    - **Revert-and-fail verification**, per this repository's established discipline: the seven modified production files (`useRideNavigation.ts`, `useFreeRoamNavigation.ts`, `RidingScreen.tsx`, `FreeRoamScreen.tsx`, `RidingRouteCompletionPanel.tsx`, `App.tsx`, `index.css`) were stashed and the two new production files (`RidingImmersiveHeader.tsx`, `immersiveRidingShell.ts`) were moved aside together, in one pass; the complete set of new/materially-changed unit and component test files was then run against this fully-reverted tree — 11 files failed outright (46 of 227 tests in that subset failing, the rest being pre-existing, unrelated cases within the same files that stayed green regardless) — before every file was restored and the identical run re-confirmed fully green (273/273). This proves the new tests genuinely exercise this slice's own behaviour rather than passing vacuously.
    - **Verification.** `npm run format`, `npm run lint`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run build`, `npm test` — 2582/2582 across 134 files, `npm run e2e` — 150/150 across both the `chromium` (136) and `android-chrome` (14) projects, `npm run format:check` run last, immediately before committing — all pass. The new `e2e/ridingImmersiveShell.spec.ts` was additionally repeated 24/24 clean under `--repeat-each=3 --workers=4` (parallel load) after the two logic fixes above landed. Version bumped to 0.3.43 (`package.json` and `package-lock.json`'s two matching `version` fields, hand-patched, following the established precedent).
    - **Real-device acceptance checklist (none of this has been checked yet — no physical device is available in this environment).** All evidence above is automated: Vitest unit/component tests and Playwright's Chromium (`chromium`) and Chromium-emulated Pixel-7 (`android-chrome`) projects — neither is a substitute for a genuine physical device, per this file's own established distinction (item 25). On the installed iPhone Home Screen PWA (and on physical Android): the global nav genuinely disappearing the instant tracking starts, for both route Riding and free roam, and reappearing correctly after Pause/End/Finish; the compact header's real safe-area behaviour under the true iOS status-bar/Dynamic-Island and home-indicator insets (portrait and landscape), and the equivalent Android insets; Pause feeling instantaneous and requiring no confirmation, with the resumed ride/free-roam session genuinely picking back up (GPS, camera, wake-lock preference) once explicitly resumed; a long route name truncating legibly without crowding Pause/End; the End-ride confirmation's real on-device layout directly beneath the compact header; no unexpected battery/heat regression from the header's own `position: sticky` recomputation while scrolling (today's active-Riding content still scrolls beneath the pinned header, unchanged from before this slice). This item's own shell/Pause slice is a foundation only — items 56–58's Map/Profile reorganisation, the non-disruptive climb cue, and the free-roam-specific immersive adaptation remain entirely unimplemented and unscoped beyond what item 55 itself required.
    - **Post-deployment follow-up, corrected by item 56 (screenshot-evidenced, not a full item-55 re-verification).** A deployed-iPhone screenshot after this slice shipped showed a real presentation defect: `RidingWakeLockControl` rendered **before** `RidingImmersiveHeader` in both `RidingScreen.tsx` and `FreeRoamScreen.tsx` (see this item's own paragraph above, which already documented this exact ordering as the reason `e2e/stickyNavigation.spec.ts`'s and `e2e/ridingImmersiveShell.spec.ts`'s own "header sits at the true top" proofs had to scroll to the bottom of the page first, rather than asserting the header started there). Since the header's own top-inset handling assumes it is the FIRST opaque box in `.screen`, the wake-lock row occupying that position instead meant the header's natural flow position — and hence, at `scrollY === 0`, its visible position — began below the wake-lock row rather than at the true viewport top. Item 56 moves `RidingWakeLockControl` to render immediately **after** the header block in both screens (same existing render condition, unchanged) as part of its own restructuring — see item 56's own entry below for the fix and its verification. This note records the finding and points to where it was actually fixed, rather than re-stating it there.

56. **Fixed route Map/Profile views — done**
    - Depends on item 55's shell. Reorganises the DOM/layout item 40 (Riding information and action proximity) established — climb-selector/Route-profile placement, the top-of-screen End-ride row — into the new fixed views; preserve every behavioural contract item 40 proved, only its container structure changes.
    - Scope: replace the long scrolling active-Riding page (route Riding only, not free roam — see item 58) with two fixed, non-scrolling views: **Map** and **Profile**, switched via large bottom `Map`/`Profile` buttons sized and spaced for use while cycling. Map view priority order (informed by, not dictated by, the mock-ups): on-route status and remaining distance; the next trusted real manoeuvre and its distance (reusing item 47's corrected selection logic); a route-complete/Finish cue where eligible (item 29's existing `RidingRouteCompletionPanel`, relocated/restyled as needed, not reimplemented); a substantially larger, preferably visually borderless map; the zoom/camera controls from items 52–53. Profile view carries `Full`/`2 km`/`10 km` (post item 54) plus contextual `Climb`, and the relevant elevation/climb metrics and chart — reorganising existing components (`ElevationChart`, `RidingClimbProgressPanel`, `RouteFeatureDetailsPanel`, `GradientColoursDisclosure`, etc.) into the new fixed view, not reimplementing their calculations. Presentation only: no change to route-matching, progress, off-route classification, manoeuvre selection (beyond consuming item 47's fix), elevation/gradient/climb calculation, completion-arming, or persistence logic.
    - **Wake-lock/header ordering fix (corrects item 55's own post-deployment finding above).** `RidingWakeLockControl`'s render call moved to directly after the header block (after `RidingImmersiveHeader` and its `pauseError`/`.ride-end-ride-confirm-row` siblings) in both `RidingScreen.tsx` and `FreeRoamScreen.tsx`, with its own existing render condition (`isWakeLockSupported() && geolocationStatus !== "idle"` for Riding; unconditional-while-mounted for free roam) completely unchanged. No CSS changes were needed for this specific fix alone — `.riding-immersive-header`'s own padding/`:has()` safe-area handling was always correct regardless of what followed it; the bug was purely about what preceded it. For route Riding, the wake-lock control additionally becomes the first of the new "shared" status stack described below (visible from either Map or Profile), which the reorder already achieves for free.
    - **Architecture — why `<MapView>` and `GradientColoursDisclosure` never remount across idle↔active or Map↔Profile.** React only preserves a component instance across renders when its JSX expression sits in a stable, unconditional textual slot in the source — the same "stable outer wrapper, conditional only on the inside" pattern this file's own Milestone-4 sixth-slice entry already established for `GradientColoursDisclosure`'s uncontrolled `<details>` state. Concretely: writing `{cond ? <div><MapView/></div> : <div><Other/><MapView/></div>}` **does** remount `MapView` (different children arrays at the same slot, confirmed directly by reasoning through React's keyless-children reconciliation, not assumed); writing `<div>{cond ? <Other/> : null}<MapView/></div>` **does not** (MapView's own JSX expression is textually fixed; only what precedes it toggles between `null` and content).
